# Multi-agent adversarial code review — post-VCR full-library sweep

**Date:** 2026-07-07 · **Reviewed at:** `main` @ `18c6a23` (v0.8.0 + the full merged VCR fix stream, PRs #238–#249)

**Question under review:** what defects remain in IbkrConduit at the current HEAD — now that every fix story from the 2026-07-04 RTOS venue-consumer review (VCR-01..VCR-11) has merged — that could harm a consumer at the `IIbkrClient`/`IIbkrClientManager` facade? Unlike the previous review, this sweep covers the whole library (including the REST periphery, Flex, health, OAuth crypto, and DI/multi-tenancy surfaces the RTOS review deliberately skipped), judged from the consumer's seat: the library is a money boundary for automated trading systems.

**Method:** multi-agent adversarial review (67 agents, orchestrated rate-limit-safe at a maximum of 3 concurrent agents). Nine sweep lenses — OAuth signing & credential hygiene, session lifecycle, streaming delivery & backpressure, wire fidelity & serialization, orders & money path, concurrency & disposal, DI pipeline & multi-tenancy, error taxonomy, REST breadth & periphery — deep-read `src/` in bounded waves; a dedup stage merged cross-lens duplicates (46 raw → 41 unique); every finding then went to an independent skeptic agent instructed to refute it against the actual code; a completeness critic named four coverage gaps, which were probed and verified the same way. Read-only: no code changes, no builds, no live IBKR calls, no credential access. Severity rubric (consumer's seat): **critical** = can lose money, duplicate/lose orders, lose or corrupt fills or money numerics silently; **high** = auth/session/liveness integrity failure, silently wrong data, or a crash/wedge of the consumer's pipeline; **medium** = robustness or observability gap that hides one of the above; **low** = nit, or unreachable through the public surface.

**Verdict meanings:** CONFIRMED = the skeptic independently traced the failure path end-to-end. PLAUSIBLE = the skeptic could not refute it, but full confirmation depends on unpinned live IBKR behavior. UNVERIFIED = swept up but not skeptic-checked (verification cap); treat as unconfirmed. REFUTED = the skeptic produced concrete counter-evidence; listed under "refuted claims".

**Result:** 50 verified findings (0 critical, 12 high, 24 medium, 14 low — 37 CONFIRMED, 13 PLAUSIBLE), 1 unverified overflow finding, 2 claims refuted, 126 areas deep-read and found clean. No critical findings — the two criticals from the 2026-07-04 review are fixed at this HEAD and nothing of that severity was found to replace them.

## Verified findings

### CON-1 — high — SessionManager (dispose vs in-flight reauth)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:486` · lens: concurrency, thread safety & disposal
- **Claim:** DisposeAsync never cancels _disposeCts (it only disposes it at line 486; the only other usages are lines 565, 589, 597) and never serializes with an in-flight ReauthenticateAsync (it disposes _semaphore at line 485 without acquiring it). A proactive-refresh reauth runs on _disposeCts.Token (line 597), which dispose cannot cancel; CancelProactiveRefresh (line 468) only cancels the slot's Task.Delay, not the reauth already in flight. A reauth that passed the ShuttingDown check (line 292) before dispose set _state therefore runs to completion concurrently with dispose: its ssodh/init (line 339) races the dispose-time LogoutAsync (line 477), and on success it calls ScheduleProactiveRefresh (line 406) which either throws ObjectDisposedException from CreateLinkedTokenSource on the disposed _disposeCts (line 565) or — if dispose has not yet reached line 486 — arms a fresh refresh slot that nothing will ever cancel (dispose already ran CancelProactiveRefresh), whose loop condition !_disposeCts.IsCancellationRequested (line 589) is true forever, so RunProactiveRefreshAsync retries ReauthenticateAsync (now throwing ODE on the disposed semaphore, swallowed at line 605-608) every 5s-5min for the remaining process lifetime. Additionally, disposing _semaphore while an EnsureInitializedAsync/ReauthenticateAsync waiter is queued leaves that waiter pending forever (SemaphoreSlim.Dispose does not fault waiters), wedging the in-flight consumer request.
- **Consequence:** A tenant disposed while a proactive refresh (or 401-triggered reauth) is in flight can have its just-issued logout undone by the racing ssodh/init — a zombie authenticated IBKR server session that competes with the consumer's next session (RTOS restart / manager re-add of the same tenant loses the compete or ping-pongs); plus an immortal background error-logging retry loop and a possible forever-hung in-flight request.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:439-489 (DisposeAsync: no _disposeCts.Cancel, no semaphore acquisition, LogoutAsync at 477, _semaphore.Dispose at 485, _disposeCts.Dispose at 486); :597 (reauth on _disposeCts.Token), :565 (linked CTS from _disposeCts.Token), :589 (loop gated only on never-cancelled _disposeCts), :631-645 (CancelProactiveRefresh cancels only the slot CTS), :292 (ShuttingDown check window), :406+548-576 (success path re-arms a slot post-dispose)
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Independently traced at HEAD. (1) DisposeAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:439-489) never cancels _disposeCts: grep over src shows the only SessionManager usages are creation (:69), Dispose (:486), linked-CTS creation (:565), loop gate (:589), and the reauth token (:597) — no Cancel/CancelAsync anywhere (contrast IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:402 which CancelAsyncs its _disposeCts before disposing, proving the intended pattern). (2) DisposeAsync never acquires _semaphore (no WaitAsync in :439-489; it disposes it at :485), so the only shutdown gate is the volatile _state==ShuttingDown check at :292, read once at reauth entry under the semaphore while dispose writes at :455 without it — a reauth past :292 runs its full RefreshAsync (:337) + ssodh/init (:339) network round-trip concurrently with dispose. (3) The proactive-refresh reauth deliberately runs on _disposeCts.Token (:597, comment :594-596), so dispose's CancelProactiveRefresh (:468 → :631-645) cancels only the slot's Task.Delay (:582, :615), never the in-flight reauth; the tickle path IS mitigated (dispose's _tickleTimer.StopAsync at :465 cancels the tickle CTS and awaits the callback chain, TickleTimer.cs:112-143), and the 401 path (TokenRefreshHandler.cs:98) runs on the consumer request token — also uncancelled by dispose. (4) Consequences follow concretely: the racing reauth's ssodh/init (:339) can land after dispose's LogoutAsync (:477) — in the manager path the window is even wider because ManagedTenant.DisposeAsync issues its logout (ManagedTenant.cs:38) before provider.DisposeAsync (:46) ever sets ShuttingDown; on reauth success, ScheduleProactiveRefresh (:406, body :548-576) has no _disposed guard — if dispose already ran :486 it throws ODE from the disposed _disposeCts.Token getter (:565), which surfaces via :423-429/:434 (Release on the disposed semaphore also throws ODE, framework semantics); if dispose has not yet reached :486, a fresh slot is armed that nothing will ever cancel (dispose's single CancelProactiveRefresh already ran), whose gate !_disposeCts.IsCancellationRequested (:589) is false-forever-safe on a disposed-but-never-cancelled CTS, so after the slot delay elapses RunProactiveRefreshAsync retries forever: :597's _disposeCts.Token throws ODE each iteration, swallowed and logged at :605-608, Task.Delay(backoff, never-cancelled slotToken) at :615, backoff capped at 5 min (:616-618) — an immortal error-logging loop plus leaked timer for the remaining process lifetime. (5) _semaphore.Dispose (:485) while the reauth holds it makes the holder's finally Release (:434) throw ODE, and SemaphoreSlim.Dispose abandons queued async waiters without faulting them — an EnsureInitializedAsync (:148) or ReauthenticateAsync (:288) waiter queued at dispose with a non-cancellable token hangs forever. (6) Reachability: IbkrClient.DisposeAsync → _sessionManager.DisposeAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IbkrClient.cs:132-134) and the manager path via ManagedTenant.cs:46; registered singleton at src/IbkrConduit/Http/SessionServiceRegistration.cs:106-114. (7) Tests pin the absence of serialization, not its presence: tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Session/SessionManagerEdgeTests.cs:77-90 explicitly accepts ObjectDisposedException from the disposed semaphore as expected; no test exercises dispose racing a reauth past :292. Nothing in the merged VCR-01..11 stream addressed this (VCR-08/MGR-1 only deduplicated the logout). Only the magnitude of the zombie-session harm (lifetime of an untickled brokerage session; whether a same-credential successor ssodh/init competes with it) rests on live IBKR behavior not pinned by docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md or recordings/ — the trigger and the code-side outcomes (ssodh/init issued after logout, immortal loop, hung waiter) do not.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A tenant's proactive LST refresh fires (once per token lifetime, or immediately at re-init when the token is inside the refresh margin) and its ReauthenticateAsync — running on the never-cancelled _disposeCts.Token — acquires the semaphore, passes the ShuttingDown check at SessionManager.cs:292, and begins its RefreshAsync+ssodh/init network round-trip; during that multi-hundred-ms window the consumer disposes the client (RTOS shutdown/restart or IIbkrClientManager tenant removal — timing that is correlated with session trouble, exactly when reauths are in flight). Dispose cannot cancel or wait for the reauth (no _disposeCts.Cancel, no semaphore acquisition, CancelProactiveRefresh only kills the slot's Task.Delay), so: the reauth's ssodh/init can complete after the dispose-path logout, re-opening an authenticated, untickled IBKR brokerage session that the just-shut-down tenant no longer manages (how long it lingers and whether it competes with the consumer's restart is unpinned live behavior); if the reauth succeeds before dispose reaches _disposeCts.Dispose() at :486, its ScheduleProactiveRefresh arms a fresh refresh slot that nothing ever cancels, which — once its delay elapses — loops forever (gate at :589 reads a disposed-but-uncancelled CTS as not-cancelled) throwing and logging ObjectDisposedException every 5s-5min for the remaining process lifetime; and any consumer request queued on the session semaphore at dispose (EnsureInitializedAsync/ReauthenticateAsync via TokenRefreshHandler) is abandoned by SemaphoreSlim.Dispose and hangs forever if its CancellationToken is non-cancellable, wedging that in-flight pipeline task during the consumer's shutdown.
- **Corroboration:** SES (medium variant, SessionManager.cs:485: same no-cancel/no-serialize DisposeAsync mechanism, additionally tracing the in-flight EnsureInitializedAsync window — a post-semaphore init creates and STARTS a new tickle timer at :232-233 after DisposeAsync's StopAsync at :463-466 already ran, then hits ODE at :565 — leaking a live keepalive loop; and post-logout session re-establishment via the never-cancelled _disposeCts.Token at :597; also noted tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Session/SessionManagerEdgeTests.cs:53-91 covers only the pre-semaphore path)
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** In DisposeAsync: CancelAsync _disposeCts before anything else, then acquire _semaphore (bounded) before disposing it so an in-flight reauth completes or cancels first; gate ScheduleProactiveRefresh on _disposed.
- **Suggested regression test:** SessionManager_DisposeAsync_WithInFlightProactiveReauth_CancelsReauthAndArmsNoNewSlot — block the fake ISessionTokenProvider.RefreshAsync, advance FakeTimeProvider to fire proactive refresh, call DisposeAsync, unblock, assert no ssodh/init after logout, no new refresh slot runs, and DisposeAsync does not throw.

### ERR-1 — high — TokenRefreshHandler + ResponseBodyCaptureHandler + ResultFactory (401-retry raw-body plumbing)

> ⚠️ **RETRACTED 2026-07-09 — verdict FALSIFIED.** This finding's CONFIRMED premise — that `IApiResponse.RequestMessage` on the retry leg is the disposed clone, so `ResultFactory.GetCapturedBody` misses the stash and hidden-error detection is disabled on the public surface — was disproven while implementing the fix. Decompiling Refit 12.1.0 confirmed `ApiResponse<T>.RequestMessage` already returns the **original** request, so hidden-error detection **already fired** on the 401-retry leg through the Refit surface a consumer uses; the claimed "silently wrong data on `GetOrderStatusAsync`" consequence does not occur. PVR-11 (#267, commit `f3e9fc4`) still shipped the `retryResponse.RequestMessage = request` reassignment as **defense-in-depth, no behavior regression** — it removed a real dangling reference to the disposed clone and hardened the non-Refit `GetAmbiguousOrderOutcome` path, but neither is the high-severity defect claimed here. **Do not treat this finding as valid.** The original text is preserved verbatim below for the audit trail — a CONFIRMED verdict later disproven is itself a calibration signal for how much to trust an agent panel's verdicts.

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Session/TokenRefreshHandler.cs:150` · lens: error taxonomy & result mapping
- **Claim:** On the 401-reauth-retry leg, the captured raw response body is lost to ResultFactory, disabling hidden-error detection: TokenRefreshHandler sends a cloned request (CloneRequest copies headers only, not Options — TokenRefreshHandler.cs:150,155,216-242), so the retried HttpResponseMessage's RequestMessage is the clone. ResponseBodyCaptureHandler sits OUTSIDE TokenRefreshHandler in the pipeline (ConsumerPipelineRegistration.cs:94-103) and stashes the retried response's body on the ORIGINAL request's Options (ResponseBodyCaptureHandler.cs:30), but ResultFactory.GetCapturedBody reads Options off response.RequestMessage — the clone — and finds nothing (ResultFactory.cs:228-238; IApiResponse.RequestMessage is HttpResponseMessage.RequestMessage, pinned by the test helper comment at tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Errors/ResultFactoryTests.cs:124-125). For a retried 2xx, response.Error is null, so rawBody falls back to "" (ResultFactory.cs:36) and DetectHiddenError("") returns null (ResultFactory.cs:127-131,41).
- **Consequence:** After any session blip (401 → reauth → retry — the exact recovery path every endpoint's 401-recovery tests exercise), a 200-with-error-body response on the retried leg is NOT classified as IbkrHiddenError. For object-shaped DTO endpoints (e.g. GetOrderStatusAsync, OrderOperations.cs:386-395) the body {"error":"..."} deserializes into a DTO with all-null fields and surfaces as Result.Success — silently wrong data on the order-state path. For collection-shaped endpoints it degrades to an opaque IbkrApiError(200, "Response body could not be deserialized") with empty RawBody instead of the actual broker message.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Session/TokenRefreshHandler.cs:150,155,183 (clone sent, retryResponse returned; CloneRequest at 216-242 copies no Options); src/IbkrConduit/Http/ResponseBodyCaptureHandler.cs:30 (stash keyed on outer request); src/IbkrConduit/Http/ConsumerPipelineRegistration.cs:94-103 (capture handler registered outside TokenRefreshHandler, so the retry sub-send never passes it); src/IbkrConduit/Errors/ResultFactory.cs:36,41,127-131,228-238 (fallback chain and hidden-error gate); tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Errors/ResultFactoryTests.cs:124-125 (RequestMessage semantics). No integration test covers 401-then-hidden-error (all *_401Recovery_* tests end in a clean 200, e.g. tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Integration/Accounts/AccountsTests.cs:49-70).
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced end-to-end at HEAD 18c6a23. Pipeline order: ConsumerPipelineRegistration.cs:91-103 registers ResponseBodyCaptureHandler (line 94-95) OUTSIDE TokenRefreshHandler (99-103), so the retry sub-send never re-traverses the capture handler. TokenRefreshHandler.cs:150 sends a clone via CloneRequest (216-242), which copies method/URI/content/headers but not Options; the retried response is returned at line 183 with HttpResponseMessage.RequestMessage = the clone (set by the primary HttpClientHandler; no inner handler reassigns it). ResponseBodyCaptureHandler.cs:30 stashes the retried body on the ORIGINAL request's Options — unreachable downstream. ResultFactory.GetCapturedBody (ResultFactory.cs:228-238) reads response.RequestMessage?.Options — the clone — and finds nothing; Refit 12's ApiResponse<T>.RequestMessage = HttpResponseMessage.RequestMessage is pinned by tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Errors/ResultFactoryTests.cs:124-125. For a retried 2xx, response.Error is null so rawBody falls to "" (ResultFactory.cs:36) and DetectHiddenError returns null on empty (ResultFactory.cs:129-131), so the hidden-error gate at line 41 never fires. Consequence verified: OrderStatus (src/IbkrConduit/Orders/IIbkrOrderApiModels.cs:517-564) is a positional record with no required members and the tolerant serializer (src/IbkrConduit/Serialization/IbkrRefitSettings.cs:39-54) deserializes {"error":"..."} into a non-null all-default instance, so ResultFactory.cs:47-50 returns Result.Success — reachable from the public surface via IIbkrClient.Orders.GetOrderStatusAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/OrderOperations.cs:386-395). Mitigations checked and rejected: StrictResponseValidation defaults to false (IbkrClientOptions.cs:102) so the schema handler only warns; the string overload (ResultFactory.cs:76) is unaffected via response.Content but typed-DTO endpoints have no fallback; the AmbiguousOrderOutcome path works only because the original response is returned there (TokenRefreshHandler.cs:146). No test covers 401-then-hidden-error (all 401Recovery tests end in a clean 200, e.g. tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Integration/Accounts/AccountsTests.cs:49-81), and ResponseBodyCaptureHandlerTests exercise the handler in isolation only. The 200-with-error-body shape is an already-pinned IBKR behavior class (ErrorResultTests.cs:108-145 and OrderTests.cs:926 pin it), so the failure is reproducible in WireMock today with no unpinned live-wire dependency — hence CONFIRMED, not PLAUSIBLE. Severity: high per the rubric — silently wrong data (a Success-wrapped degenerate DTO) on the order-state read path during the exact session-recovery flow, plus loss of the broker's actual error message on collection-shaped endpoints; not critical because it cannot itself place/duplicate orders or fabricate plausible-but-wrong money numerics (fields come back null/zero, and nullability-as-presence consumers treat null as absent). Residual gap not closed by the VCR stream (closest story VCR-10 hardened schema validation, which is off by default).
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer calls any typed-DTO endpoint (e.g. IIbkrClient.Orders.GetOrderStatusAsync) just after a session blip: the first send returns 401, TokenRefreshHandler re-authenticates and re-sends a CloneRequest that lacks the original request's Options (TokenRefreshHandler.cs:150,216-242). IBKR answers the retried leg 200 with a hidden-error body such as {"error":"..."} — a wire shape the library already models and defends against on the non-retry path. ResponseBodyCaptureHandler stashes that body on the original request's Options (ResponseBodyCaptureHandler.cs:30), but the retried response's RequestMessage is the clone, so ResultFactory.GetCapturedBody finds nothing, rawBody degrades to "" (ResultFactory.cs:36), and DetectHiddenError never fires (ResultFactory.cs:129-131). The error body deserializes into an OrderStatus with null Status/Symbol and zero OrderId under the tolerant serializer and surfaces as Result.Success (ResultFactory.cs:47-50) — silently wrong order-state data on the money-reconciliation path; collection-shaped endpoints instead surface an opaque IbkrApiError(200, "Response body could not be deserialized") with empty RawBody, discarding the broker's actual message. Reproducible in WireMock (401 then 200-with-error-body); no existing test covers the combination.
- **Corroboration:** TEN (independent variant at TokenRefreshHandler.cs:219: same clone-without-Options mechanism — CloneRequest :219-241 copies only content+headers, retryResponse.RequestMessage = clone, ResultFactory.GetCapturedBody :230-235 misses the stash — with the same silently-wrong-Success consequence and the same fix; cites ResultFactoryTests.cs:125-144 and ErrorResultTests.cs:108-145 for the test gap)
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** In TokenRefreshHandler, before returning retryResponse, reassign retryResponse.RequestMessage = request (the original message whose Options the capture handler populates), or copy the original request's Options onto the clone in CloneRequest.
- **Suggested regression test:** GetOrderStatus_401ThenHiddenError_ReturnsIbkrHiddenError — WireMock scenario: first call 401, ssodh/init succeeds, retried call returns 200 {"error":"..."}; assert Result failure with IbkrHiddenError carrying the broker message and non-empty RawBody.

### PRB-1.1 — high — StreamingOperations.MarketDataAsync + IbkrWebSocketClient.ProcessMessage (smd frame routing)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:100` · lens: gap probe: Client/StreamingOperations.cs — the streaming facade no lens owned; prefix-only frame routing means cross-conid/cross-account fan-in on smd/ssd/sld
- **Claim:** Every MarketDataAsync subscription is registered under the bare topic prefix "smd" regardless of conid, and ProcessMessage routes by prefix only ("smd+265598" -> "smd") to EVERY writer in the shared per-prefix list, so with two concurrent MarketDataAsync subscriptions for different conids each subscription's Stream emits BOTH conids' ticks. Neither ChannelObservable nor MarketDataTickMapper filters by conid — the mapper happily maps whatever conid arrives (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/MarketDataTickMapper.cs:15-31, no comparison against a requested conid), and the XML doc promises 'Subscribes to real-time market data for the specified contract' with no consumer obligation to filter on MarketDataTick.Conid.
- **Consequence:** Silently wrong market data feeding a trading system: a consumer (e.g. RTOS) subscribing to two instruments and treating each returned Stream as that instrument's price feed consumes the other instrument's bid/ask/last as its own — wrong prices driving order decisions, with no error, no drop counter, and nothing in the public contract warning that cross-conid frames arrive.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:96-102 (topicPrefix hardcoded "smd" for every conid); src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:245-254 (writer added to _subscribers list keyed by topicPrefix only), :659-676 (prefix extracted from "smd+265598" and frame TryWrite to every writer under "smd"); src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/ChannelObservable.cs:49-85 (pump maps and delivers every frame, no filter); src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/MarketDataTickMapper.cs:8-59 (maps any conid, never compares); src/IbkrConduit/Client/IStreamingOperations.cs:74-81 (doc: 'market data for the specified contract'); no test pins two-conid isolation — tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Integration/Streaming/MarketDataFrameFlowTest.cs:65 uses a single conid, and grep of tests/ finds no two-simultaneous-MarketDataAsync test.
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced end-to-end from the public surface. (1) Registration: StreamingOperations.MarketDataAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:94-103) builds a per-conid subscribe message ("smd+{conid}+...", line 97) but passes the bare topicPrefix "smd" to SubscribeTopicAsync (line 100) for every conid; the ChannelObservable is also constructed with topic "smd" (line 102) and MarketDataTickMapper.Map as the mapper. (2) Writer registry: IbkrWebSocketClient.SubscribeTopicAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:225-265) does _subscribers.GetOrAdd(topicPrefix,...) and writers.Add(channel.Writer) (lines 246-254) — two MarketDataAsync calls for conids A and B put both writers in the single _subscribers["smd"] list. (3) Routing: ProcessMessage (lines 628-677) extracts the prefix only — "smd+265598" -> "smd" (lines 660-661) — and TryWrites the whole frame to EVERY writer under that prefix (lines 667-676); nothing matches the topic suffix to the subscription that requested it. (4) Delivery: ChannelObservable.PumpAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/ChannelObservable.cs:49-95) maps and delivers every frame with no filter, and MarketDataTickMapper.Map (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/MarketDataTickMapper.cs:8-60) extracts whatever conid is in the frame's topic (lines 15-32) with no comparison against a requested conid. (5) Contract: IStreamingOperations.MarketDataAsync's doc says "Subscribes to real-time market data for the specified contract" (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IStreamingOperations.cs:74-81) with no consumer obligation to filter on MarketDataTick.Conid; the design doc (docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md:904) documents only the wire messages, not the fan-out. Reachability is public: IIbkrClient.Streaming -> IStreamingOperations (public interface). Counter-evidence hunt: no test pins two-conid isolation — tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Integration/Streaming/MarketDataFrameFlowTest.cs and WebSocketUnsubscribeTest.cs (line 27: single _conid = 265598) use one conid; the VCR review explicitly scoped smd OUT (docs/findings/2026-07-04-rtos-venue-consumer-review.md:572 "market-data (smd) ... not reviewed" because RTOS does not consume market data per ADR-0028 §12), so no VCR fix touched this and it is not a re-report. Tellingly, the repo's own multi-symbol example works only by accident of its design: examples/IbkrConduit.Examples.MarketDataStream/StreamHost.cs:64-79 subscribes N conids and attaches a per-symbol observer to each, but every observer feeds a SHARED table whose UpdateTick routes by tick.Conid (examples/.../LiveTickTable.cs:44-49) — i.e., the example silently receives N copies of every tick on every stream and dedupes by conid-keyed idempotent writes, masking the cross-delivery rather than refuting it. The only wire assumption is that IBKR tags smd frames with "smd+{conid}" topics, which is pinned by the design doc (line 904), the mapper's own extraction logic, and the captured websocket reference — the defective fan-out itself is entirely client-side and deterministic. Severity: high per the rubric ("silently wrong data") — not critical, because each MarketDataTick does carry the correct Conid, so a consumer inspecting tick.Conid gets uncorrupted data; the defect is mis-scoped delivery plus an undocumented filtering obligation, and RTOS (the known real-money consumer) structurally does not consume market data.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer calls client.Streaming.MarketDataAsync(conidA, fields) and client.Streaming.MarketDataAsync(conidB, fields), then ConnectAsync. Both subscriptions' channel writers land in the same _subscribers["smd"] list (StreamingOperations.cs:100 hardcodes prefix "smd"; IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:246-254), and every inbound frame — "smd+A" and "smd+B" alike — is prefix-routed to BOTH writers (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:660-676). Each subscription's Stream therefore emits ticks for both contracts (and duplicates every tick across N concurrent smd subscriptions), with MarketDataTickMapper happily mapping whichever conid arrived (MarketDataTickMapper.cs:15-32). The XML contract promises "market data for the specified contract" (IStreamingOperations.cs:75) with no warning to filter on MarketDataTick.Conid, so a trading consumer that wires each returned Stream to a per-instrument price handler consumes contract B's bid/ask/last as contract A's — silently wrong prices driving order decisions, with no error, no drop counter, and no test pinning two-conid isolation (only single-conid tests exist; the repo's own multi-symbol example avoids the bug only by routing on tick.Conid into a shared table).
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Route solicited per-target topics by their full incoming topic string (register "smd+{conid}" and have ProcessMessage try a full-topic match before the prefix fallback), or filter in the facade: wrap the MarketDataAsync mapper to skip frames whose topic conid != the requested conid (and count them under a routed-elsewhere cause, not as drops). Apply the same keying to ssd/sld.
- **Suggested regression test:** MarketDataAsync_TwoConids_EachStreamReceivesOnlyItsOwnConid — DI-stack MockWebSocketServer test: subscribe conid A and conid B, broadcast one smd+A and one smd+B frame, assert stream A emits exactly one tick with Conid==A and never a Conid==B tick (and vice versa).

### PRB-1.2 — high — StreamingOperations.AccountSummaryAsync / AccountLedgerAsync (ssd/sld frame routing)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:174` · lens: gap probe: Client/StreamingOperations.cs — the streaming facade no lens owned; prefix-only frame routing means cross-conid/cross-account fan-in on smd/ssd/sld
- **Claim:** Same prefix-only fan-in as smd, per account: AccountSummaryAsync and AccountLedgerAsync register every accountId's subscription under the bare prefixes "ssd"/"sld" (StreamingOperations.cs:171-176, 186-191) while incoming frames are topic-targeted per account ("ssd+DUO873728" — pinned by the repo's own test fixtures), so under a multi-account tenant two AccountSummaryAsync subscriptions for different accounts each receive both accounts' summary/ledger frames. AccountSummaryUpdateMapper/AccountLedgerUpdateMapper map any account's frame without comparing against the requested accountId, and the XML doc promises 'real-time account summary updates for the specified account'. Additionally two subscriptions for the SAME account with different keys/fields filters fan into each other, so each stream sees the union of rows rather than its requested filter.
- **Consequence:** Silently wrong account money numerics: a consumer streaming account A's summary/ledger receives account B's cash balance, excess liquidity, and net liquidation rows on A's stream; risk/margin logic keyed off the stream identity rather than the AccountId property computes against the wrong account's money with no error surfaced.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:171-176 (ssd subscribe built per accountId but topicPrefix "ssd"), :186-191 (sld same); src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:246 (writers keyed by prefix), :659-676 (prefix routing); src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/AccountSummaryUpdateMapper.cs:13-29 and AccountLedgerUpdateMapper.cs:13-29 (map any account, AccountId parsed from topic but never checked); per-account incoming topic shape pinned at tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Streaming/StreamingOperationsTests.cs:392,414 ("topic":"ssd+DUO873728" / "sld+DUO873728"); src/IbkrConduit/Client/IStreamingOperations.cs:122-134,136-148 (per-account promise).
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced end-to-end from the public surface. (1) Reachable: IIbkrClient exposes Streaming (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IIbkrClient.cs:33), whose AccountSummaryAsync/AccountLedgerAsync promise updates "for the specified account" (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IStreamingOperations.cs:122-134, 136-148). (2) Registration: AccountSummaryAsync builds the per-account subscribe message "ssd+{accountId}+..." but passes the bare prefix "ssd" as the routing key (src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:171-176); AccountLedgerAsync does the same with "sld" (StreamingOperations.cs:186-191). (3) Routing: SubscribeTopicAsync stores every subscription's channel writer in _subscribers keyed only by that bare prefix (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:245-254), and ProcessMessage strips everything after '+' from the incoming topic ("ssd+DUO873728" -> "ssd", IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:659-661) then TryWrites the frame to EVERY writer under that prefix (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:667-676) — no comparison of the topic's account suffix against the subscription's account. (4) Mapping: AccountSummaryUpdateMapper.Map and AccountLedgerUpdateMapper.Map deserialize any frame's result rows and set AccountId from the frame topic, never filtering against the requested account (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/AccountSummaryUpdateMapper.cs:13-29, AccountLedgerUpdateMapper.cs:13-29). (5) Incoming per-account topic shape is pinned inside the repo: the reference doc shows a live frame with "topic": "sld+DU1234567" (docs/ibkr-websocket-api-reference.md:116) and the unit fixtures use captured paper-account frames "topic":"ssd+DUO873728" / "sld+DUO873728" (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Streaming/StreamingOperationsTests.cs:392, 414). (6) Counter-evidence hunt came up empty: DTO docs only say AccountId is "parsed from the frame's topic" — no consumer filtering obligation (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/StreamingModels.cs:135-136, 166-167); no ADR/design-doc entry documents the fan-in; no test pins isolation between two same-prefix subscriptions; the merged VCR fixes (VCR-03/VCR-10 touched mappers) added per-element isolation and money-field census but no account routing/filter. The same-account different-keys/fields fan-in variant is fully client-side and deterministic (both writers under "ssd" receive every ssd+acct frame). One unpinned wire detail, noted for completeness: whether IBKR accepts two concurrent ssd/sld subscriptions for different accounts on one session is not pinned by docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md or recordings/ — but any account-suffixed frame arriving while two subscriptions exist is cross-delivered by the traced client-side code, and the cross-delivery also fires transiently around unsubscribe/resubscribe of a second account. Severity re-judged as high (up from claimed medium): when triggered this is silently wrong data at the money boundary — account B's cashBalance/netLiquidationValue/ExcessLiquidity rows delivered on account A's stream with no error, on a surface whose XML docs affirmatively promise account scoping; not critical because each emitted item carries a correct AccountId discriminator (and sld rows carry acctCode), so the numerics are intact and self-labeled, and the trigger requires a multi-account tenant or dual-filter usage.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A tenant whose IBKR username carries two accounts (e.g. an advisor or linked-account setup) calls client.Streaming.AccountSummaryAsync("DU_A") and client.Streaming.AccountSummaryAsync("DU_B") (or the sld ledger equivalents). Both subscriptions register their channel writers under the bare prefix "ssd" (StreamingOperations.cs:174; IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:246), and every incoming frame — topic "ssd+DU_A" or "ssd+DU_B" — is prefix-stripped and written to both channels (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:661, 667-676); the mapper builds an AccountSummaryUpdate from whichever account's frame arrived without checking it against the requested account (AccountSummaryUpdateMapper.cs:28). A consumer that keys off stream identity — as the interface doc "real-time account summary updates for the specified account" (IStreamingOperations.cs:123) invites — feeds account B's ExcessLiquidity/NetLiquidation/cash rows into account A's risk and margin logic with no error, log, or metric signaling the misdelivery. The pinned, purely client-side variant of the same defect: two AccountSummaryAsync calls for the SAME account with different keys/fields filters each receive every frame for that account, so each stream sees the union rather than its requested filter.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Same fix as the smd finding: key solicited routing by full topic ("ssd+{accountId}") or filter in the facade mapper on AccountId != requested accountId; decide and document the union-of-filters semantics for same-account duplicate subscriptions.
- **Suggested regression test:** AccountSummaryAsync_TwoAccounts_EachStreamReceivesOnlyItsOwnAccount — MockWebSocketServer broadcasts ssd+DU1 and ssd+DU2 frames after two subscriptions; assert each stream emits only updates whose AccountId matches its requested account.

### PRB-3.1 — high — IbkrWebSocketClient frame dispatch + StreamingOperations.AccountSummaryAsync/AccountLedgerAsync (topic routing)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:174` · lens: gap probe: ssd/sld account-money streaming mappers (AccountSummaryUpdateMapper, AccountLedgerUpdateMapper) — money-carrying mappers absent from every WIR clean note
- **Claim:** Account-scoped subscriptions are routed by bare topic prefix, so every ssd/sld frame is fanned out to ALL same-prefix subscriptions regardless of account: AccountSummaryAsync passes topicPrefix "ssd" (StreamingOperations.cs:174) and AccountLedgerAsync passes "sld" (StreamingOperations.cs:189) to SubscribeTopicAsync; the writer is registered under that bare prefix (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:245-246 `_subscribers.GetOrAdd(topicPrefix, ...)`), and inbound dispatch strips the account from the wire topic ("ssd+DU1" -> "ssd", IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:660-661) and TryWrites the frame to every writer under the prefix (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:667-675). A consumer holding two subscriptions for accounts A and B receives B's balance frames on A's subscription (mapped with AccountId=B), directly contradicting the documented per-account scoping: "Subscribes to real-time account summary updates for the specified account" (IStreamingOperations.cs:122-125) and the same for the ledger (IStreamingOperations.cs:136-139). The identical mechanism cross-delivers smd ticks across conids for MarketDataAsync(conid,...) (StreamingOperations.cs:100, IStreamingOperations.cs:74-81), with MarketDataTick.Conid correctly stamped per frame (MarketDataTickMapper.cs:15-26).
- **Consequence:** A multi-account consumer (or any strategy with two market-data subscriptions) that wires one pipeline per subscription and trusts the documented scoping ingests another account's cash balances / another instrument's prices into the wrong pipeline — silently wrong money data driving trading decisions. The DTOs carry the correct AccountId/Conid, but nothing in the public docs tells the consumer they must filter, and the interface explicitly promises scoping.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:171-176 and 186-191 (topicPrefix "ssd"/"sld"); src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:245-246 (writers keyed by prefix), 659-661 (prefix extraction discards account segment), 667-675 (frame written to every writer under the prefix); src/IbkrConduit/Client/IStreamingOperations.cs:122-125, 136-139, 74-81 (per-account/per-contract scoping promise); no test in tests/ subscribes the same topic twice (grep for multi-subscription scenarios returned none).
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Full independent trace from the public surface confirms the claim. (1) Public entry: IStreamingOperations.AccountSummaryAsync documents "Subscribes to real-time account summary updates for the specified account" (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IStreamingOperations.cs:122-125), AccountLedgerAsync the same (136-139), and MarketDataAsync "for the specified contract" (74-81). (2) StreamingOperations.AccountSummaryAsync builds "ssd+{accountId}+..." but passes bare topicPrefix "ssd" to SubscribeTopicAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:171-174); AccountLedgerAsync passes "sld" (186-189); MarketDataAsync passes "smd" (97-100). (3) SubscribeTopicAsync creates a fresh per-subscription channel (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:237-243) and registers its writer under the bare prefix: `_subscribers.GetOrAdd(topicPrefix, _ => [])` then `writers.Add(channel.Writer)` (246-252) — the account/conid segment is never part of the routing key. (4) Inbound dispatch in ProcessMessage strips the suffix: `var plusIndex = topic.IndexOf('+'); var prefix = plusIndex >= 0 ? topic[..plusIndex] : topic;` (660-661) and TryWrites the whole frame to EVERY writer under that prefix (667-676). (5) No downstream filter exists: ChannelObservable.PumpAsync maps and delivers every frame from the reader (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/ChannelObservable.cs:49-85); AccountSummaryUpdateMapper stamps AccountId from the frame's own topic, not the subscription's (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/AccountSummaryUpdateMapper.cs:28); MarketDataTickMapper stamps Conid from the frame topic (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/MarketDataTickMapper.cs:14-32). Counter-evidence hunt came up empty: the routing unit tests only ever enqueue a frame matching the single subscription's own conid (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClientTests.cs:150-166 uses smd+265598 for an smd+265598 subscription; 168-188 uses smd+123 for smd+123) — nothing pins scoping and nothing exercises two same-prefix subscriptions; grep of docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md found no documented consumer-filter obligation, and the design doc's own streaming table (docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md:904, `smd+{conid}`/`umd+{conid}`) plus the per-cancel-message refcounting in UnsubscribeSolicitedAsync (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:815-820) show multiple concurrent same-prefix subscriptions are the designed usage, so the trigger is reachable and intended. None of the merged VCR-01..11 fixes touched this routing (dispatch is still bare-prefix at HEAD 18c6a23). The smd multi-conid variant is fully deterministic library-side: two MarketDataAsync subscriptions each receive every smd+* frame, no live-wire assumption needed beyond the pinned smd+{conid} topic shape (pinned by unit tests and design doc:904). The ssd/sld multi-account variant additionally assumes IBKR accepts two concurrent per-account ssd subscriptions on one socket — not pinned by recordings/ (which contain only order captures) — but the library-side cross-delivery is unconditional either way. Verdict CONFIRMED on the smd path; severity high per rubric ("silently wrong data"): frames are delivered to subscriptions they don't belong to, contradicting the documented scoping, but the DTOs carry the correct AccountId/Conid so the numbers themselves are not corrupted and a defensive consumer can filter — keeping it below critical.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer calls MarketDataAsync(conidA, fields) and MarketDataAsync(conidB, fields) on the same client (the designed usage — per-conid subscribe/cancel messages, docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md:904), wiring one pipeline per subscription and trusting the interface promise "Subscribes to real-time market data for the specified contract" (IStreamingOperations.cs:74-81). Both writers are registered under the bare key "smd" (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:246), so when IBKR pushes a frame with topic "smd+{conidB}", dispatch strips it to "smd" (660-661) and writes it to conidA's channel as well (667-676); conidA's pipeline then receives a MarketDataTick whose Fields carry instrument B's prices (Conid is stamped B, but nothing in the docs tells the consumer to check it). A strategy that reads price fields off "its" subscription ingests the wrong instrument's prices — silently wrong money data driving trading decisions. The identical mechanism cross-delivers ssd/sld account-summary and ledger frames between AccountSummaryAsync/AccountLedgerAsync subscriptions for different accounts (StreamingOperations.cs:174, 189), so a multi-account operator sees account B's cash balances on account A's stream, directly contradicting IStreamingOperations.cs:122-125/136-139.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Key solicited subscriptions by full topic identity (prefix + target segment, e.g. "ssd+DU1234567", "smd+265598") in _subscribers and match the full wire topic on dispatch, falling back to prefix for target-less topics (sor/spl/str); alternatively, if cross-delivery is intended, remove the "for the specified account/contract" promise and document AccountId/Conid filtering as a consumer obligation (contract-design: this is a guarantee-semantics gap needing a design-doc line either way).
- **Suggested regression test:** AccountSummary_TwoAccountSubscriptions_FramesRoutedOnlyToMatchingAccount — DI-stack integration test on MockWebSocketServer: subscribe ssd for DU1 and DU2, broadcast one {"topic":"ssd+DU1",...} frame, assert DU1's subscription emits exactly one update and DU2's emits none.

### SES-1 — high — TickleTimer.RunAsync (HTTP-401 tickle recovery branch, VCR-06/SES-2 residual)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Session/TickleTimer.cs:259` · lens: session lifecycle
- **Claim:** In the tickle loop's catch block, the 401 branch awaits `_onFailure(cancellationToken)` (TickleTimer.cs:259) INSIDE `catch (Exception ex)` (TickleTimer.cs:231) with no enclosing handler, so any exception thrown by the triggered ReauthenticateAsync (transient LST/ssodh failure wrapped by WrapCredentialException at SessionManager.cs:355/359, or ssodh authenticated=false throwing BuildUnauthenticatedInitError at SessionManager.cs:374) propagates out of RunAsync and permanently faults the background tickle task. The parallel authenticated=false-body path (TickleTimer.cs:208) is inside the try and IS protected by the same catch — only the 401 path added by the VCR-06/SES-2 fix lacks this tolerance. Nothing ever restarts the loop: _state stays Ready so EnsureInitializedAsync short-circuits (SessionManager.cs:139), and ReauthenticateAsync deliberately never recreates the timer (SessionManager.cs:318-325, 404-405).
- **Consequence:** One transient failure during tickle-401 recovery (e.g. IBKR invalidates the LST while the backend is flaky, or a lost compete under Compete=false makes reauth throw authenticated=false) silently kills session keepalive forever: no more tickle-driven reauth, health frozen at the last SetFailed snapshot, NotifyTickleSucceededAsync never fires so the WebSocket tickle-watchdog (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:748) never retries reconnect — a streaming-heavy consumer loses the str fill stream until an unrelated REST call happens to 401, i.e. unbooked fills with no recovery loop. Under Compete=false the ADR-0004 backoff design ('the tickle loop still fires' — SessionManager.cs:306-308) is broken by the first throwing reauth, making the stand-down permanent even after the competitor leaves.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Session/TickleTimer.cs:174-279 (RunAsync: try at 183, catch(OperationCanceledException) at 227, catch(Exception) at 231, `await _onFailure(cancellationToken)` at 259 inside the catch with no wrapper); contrast TickleTimer.cs:208 (in-try _onFailure whose reauth exceptions are caught at 231 and treated as a failed tickle). Throw sources: src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:346-360 (WrapCredentialException throw for transient RefreshAsync/ssodh failure), :365-374 (authenticated=false throw). No-restart: SessionManager.cs:139, :318-325, :404-405. Test gap: tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Session/TickleTimerTests.cs:218-268 pins the 401→onFailure trigger only with a non-throwing callback; no test makes onFailure throw on the 401 path.
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Mechanism independently traced and confirmed at HEAD. TickleTimer.RunAsync's catch block (src/IbkrConduit/Session/TickleTimer.cs:231) contains the SES-2/VCR-06 401 branch which awaits `_onFailure(cancellationToken)` at TickleTimer.cs:259 with no enclosing handler; `_onFailure` is SessionManager.OnTickleFailureAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:518-522) which returns ReauthenticateAsync directly. ReauthenticateAsync throws on (a) transient RefreshAsync/ssodh failure — WrapCredentialException at SessionManager.cs:355/359 produces IbkrTransientException/IbkrConfigurationException — or (b) ssodh 200 authenticated=false — BuildUnauthenticatedInitError at SessionManager.cs:374. An exception thrown inside the catch propagates out of RunAsync, terminating the while loop (TickleTimer.cs:176) and faulting the background task stored at TickleTimer.cs:105; the fault is unobserved (StopAsync catches only OperationCanceledException, TickleTimer.cs:130-140). The parallel 200-body authenticated=false path at TickleTimer.cs:208 is inside the try: a throwing reauth there is caught at :231, classified non-401 (IbkrApiException is not Refit ApiException, check at :248) and the loop survives via :261-276 — only the 401 branch lacks tolerance. Test gap confirmed: every onFailure callback in tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Session/TickleTimerTests.cs (lines 36, 93, 133, 180, 229, 281, 334) is non-throwing; the 401 test at :218-269 pins only the trigger, never a throwing callback. Reachable from the public surface: AddIbkrClient wires SessionManager; the timer starts on first EnsureInitializedAsync (SessionManager.cs:232-233) invoked by every REST call via OAuthSigningHandler.SendAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Auth/OAuthSigningHandler.cs:66-69). HOWEVER, the sweep's no-recovery claim is partially wrong: the throwing reauth's SES-3 catch sets _state = Uninitialized (SessionManager.cs:423-430), NOT Ready, so the next consumer REST call re-enters full init, which stops the dead timer (SessionManager.cs:227-230 — the first attempt rethrows the stale fault from StopAsync awaiting the faulted task, TickleTimer.cs:130-140, since _backgroundTask is nulled before awaiting at :117-123) and the second REST call recreates the timer and fully recovers. The residual real damage: (1) a streaming-heavy consumer with no REST traffic has no recovery trigger — WS drop reconnect is single-shot (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:596/616 → ReconnectAsync, failure merely logged at :712-715) and the designed retry, the tickle watchdog (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:748-769), is permanently dead; the only remaining path is the proactive-refresh slot (SessionManager.cs:578-629, still armed per :327-330) firing near LST expiry (potentially hours), whose success sets _state=Ready WITHOUT recreating the timer (SessionManager.cs:404-405), after which EnsureInitializedAsync short-circuits (:139) and keepalive stays dead until a later failed reauth knocks state off Ready; (2) one consumer REST call surfaces a stale, unrelated reauth exception; (3) SessionManager.DisposeAsync awaits the faulted timer via StopAsync unprotected (SessionManager.cs:463-466), so dispose can rethrow the stale exception; (4) under Compete=false, if the lost compete manifests as a tickle HTTP 401 (rather than 200 authenticated=false — which of the two IBKR emits after a competing takeover is not pinned by docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md), the ADR-0004 backoff loop (SessionManager.cs:306-313) dies on its first attempt. Health is not silent — SetFailed ran at TickleTimer.cs:257 before the fault. The trigger is concrete and needs no unpinned wire behavior: the 401-tickle branch exists for a pinned signal (SES-2 test at TickleTimerTests.cs:218) and the second failure is any transient exception during the recovery reauth. Severity: high (session/liveness integrity failure that wedges the streaming pipeline's designed recovery), not critical — recovery exists for REST-active consumers, the outage is observable via health SetFailed and the ConnectionDisconnected event (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:700), and no money numerics are corrupted.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A long-running consumer's LST is invalidated server-side, so the next tickle returns HTTP 401; the tickle loop's catch-block 401 branch (TickleTimer.cs:259) awaits the reauth callback, and that ReauthenticateAsync fails once transiently (network blip during LST refresh → IbkrTransientException via SessionManager.cs:355) — the exception propagates out of the catch, exits RunAsync's while loop, and silently faults the background tickle task. From that moment there is no keepalive and no tickle-succeeded watchdog: a streaming-heavy consumer whose brokerage session then times out loses the str fill stream, gets one single-shot reconnect attempt (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:712-715), and — with no REST traffic — stays down until the proactive-refresh slot fires near LST expiry (hours), booking no fills in the interim; even that recovery restores the session but not the tickle timer (SessionManager.cs:404-405 + :139), leaving keepalive dead. A REST-active consumer (e.g. RTOS) recovers faster but pays two broken calls: the first post-fault REST call re-enters init and rethrows the stale reauth exception out of TickleTimer.StopAsync (TickleTimer.cs:130-140) as a confusing error on an unrelated request, and only the second call recreates the timer. The same unprotected path makes the ADR-0004 Compete=false backoff loop die on its first throwing reauth if the lost compete surfaces as a tickle 401 (that wire shape is unpinned).
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Wrap the `await _onFailure(cancellationToken)` at TickleTimer.cs:259 in try/catch (rethrow only cancellation), logging the reauth failure and letting the loop continue at the failure interval — mirroring the tolerance the authenticated=false path already gets from the enclosing catch.
- **Suggested regression test:** RunAsync_TickleReturns401AndReauthThrows_TickleLoopContinues — FakeSessionApi returns a 401 ApiException and onFailure throws IbkrTransientException; advance FakeTimeProvider two failure intervals and assert a second tickle attempt occurs (loop still alive) and onFailure was invoked again.

### SES-2 — high — TickleTimer.StartAsync / SessionManager.EnsureInitializedAsync (keepalive loop lifetime)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Session/TickleTimer.cs:104` · lens: session lifecycle
- **Claim:** The tickle background loop's lifetime CTS is linked to the cancellation token of whichever caller happened to initialize the session: TickleTimer.StartAsync does CreateLinkedTokenSource(cancellationToken) (TickleTimer.cs:104) and SessionManager.EnsureInitializedAsync passes its caller's token straight through (SessionManager.cs:233). That token is a consumer request token — OAuthSigningHandler.cs:68 passes the first API call's token, TenantBuilder.cs:44-45 passes the AddAsync caller's token. If the consumer later cancels that token (timeout CTS not disposed, request-scoped/saga token cancelled after completion, shared batch token), Task.Delay at TickleTimer.cs:179 (outside the try at 183) throws OperationCanceledException, RunAsync exits, and the fault sits unobserved in _backgroundTask. Nothing restarts the loop: _state remains Ready so EnsureInitializedAsync returns at SessionManager.cs:139, and ReauthenticateAsync never recreates the timer (SessionManager.cs:318-325, 404-405).
- **Consequence:** Silent, permanent loss of session keepalive and liveness monitoring tied to an unrelated consumer cancellation: the IBKR brokerage session idles out server-side, SessionHealthState freezes at the last good snapshot (authenticated=true — the SES-2 staleness guard lives inside the dead loop), the WS tickle-watchdog never fires again (fills unbooked after any socket drop), and every REST call after an idle gap eats a 401+reauth round trip — with order-mutating POSTs during those gaps surfacing as IbkrAmbiguousOrderError (ADR-0003 gate) requiring manual reconciliation instead of clean placement.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Session/TickleTimer.cs:100-109 (StartAsync links caller token into _cts), :176-179 (loop delay on the linked token, outside the try/catch at 183); src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:233 (StartAsync(cancellationToken) with the EnsureInitializedAsync caller token); src/IbkrConduit/Auth/OAuthSigningHandler.cs:66-69 (per-request consumer token initializes the session); src/IbkrConduit/Client/TenantBuilder.cs:44-45 (AddAsync token); no-restart: SessionManager.cs:139, :318-325, :404-405; no test in tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Session/TickleTimerTests.cs or SessionManagerTests.cs cancels the start token after startup and asserts the loop survives.
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Mechanism traced end-to-end at HEAD (18c6a23). (1) Token plumbing: every session-initializing entry point passes a consumer-supplied token — OAuthSigningHandler.SendAsync calls _sessionManager.EnsureInitializedAsync(cancellationToken) with the per-request token (src/IbkrConduit/Auth/OAuthSigningHandler.cs:66-69); TenantBuilder.BuildAsync passes the IIbkrClientManager.AddAsync caller's token (src/IbkrConduit/Client/TenantBuilder.cs:44-47); IbkrClient.ValidateConnectionAsync:113 and IbkrWebSocketClient.ConnectAsync:191 likewise. (2) SessionManager.EnsureInitializedAsync forwards that token to _tickleTimer.StartAsync(cancellationToken) (src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:233), and TickleTimer.StartAsync does _cts = CancellationTokenSource.CreateLinkedTokenSource(cancellationToken) (src/IbkrConduit/Session/TickleTimer.cs:104), so the keepalive loop's lifetime is permanently linked to whichever caller token happened to initialize the session. (3) Loop death: RunAsync's while condition checks cancellationToken.IsCancellationRequested (TickleTimer.cs:176) and Task.Delay at TickleTimer.cs:179 sits OUTSIDE the try that begins at :183; a later cancel of the caller token makes the delay throw OperationCanceledException out of RunAsync (and the catch at :227-230 rethrows OCE anyway). The canceled task sits unobserved in _backgroundTask (TickleTimer.cs:105) — a Canceled task raises no UnobservedTaskException, so death is fully silent. (4) No restart: _state remains Ready so EnsureInitializedAsync early-returns forever (SessionManager.cs:139-142); ReauthenticateAsync deliberately never stops or recreates the timer (SessionManager.cs:318-325 and :404-405 — 'No tickle timer recreate either... The existing timer keeps running', which is now false). The only recreate path is a FAILED reauth resetting _state to Uninitialized (SessionManager.cs:428) followed by a fresh EnsureInitializedAsync (SessionManager.cs:227-233) — under normal operation (reauths succeed) the loop stays dead permanently. (5) Consequences verified: the server-side idle-out is pinned by the captured spec — docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:6678 'If the gateway has not received any requests for several minutes an open session will automatically timeout. The tickle endpoint pings the server to prevent the session from ending... approximately every 60 seconds'. SessionHealthState carries no timestamp or staleness signal (src/IbkrConduit/Health/SessionHealthState.cs:9-113) and the SES-2 staleness guard (_consecutiveTransportFailures / _maxConsecutiveTransportFailuresBeforeUnhealthy) lives inside the dead loop (TickleTimer.cs:34, :190-191, :270-275), so passive health freezes at the last snapshot (authenticated=true). Order-mutating POSTs that 401 after an idle gap are never replayed and surface as ambiguous outcomes (src/IbkrConduit/Session/TokenRefreshHandler.cs:135-146, ADR-0003), requiring manual reconciliation. No test pins survival: tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Session/TickleTimerTests.cs uses cts.Token on every StartAsync and relies on cancelling it to STOP the loop (e.g. :55, :112) — the tests confirm the linkage kills the loop and none cancels the start token post-startup asserting survival. Two consequence corrections vs the sweep's claim: (a) the WS tickle watchdog (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:138, :748-763) is only a backstop — heartbeat_failure/server_close/receive_error reconnects run on _disposeCts independently (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:549, :596, :616), so most socket drops still recover; (b) the proactive-refresh slot is linked to _disposeCts, not the caller token (SessionManager.cs:565), so LST refresh survives. Severity: high per rubric — silent session/liveness integrity failure plus silently-wrong health data (frozen authenticated=true); not critical because order 401s surface loudly as IbkrAmbiguousOrderError rather than silently losing orders, and streaming mostly self-heals.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer (e.g. RTOS) makes its first API call — or calls IIbkrClientManager.AddAsync / ValidateConnectionAsync / WebSocket ConnectAsync — with a cancellable token whose lifetime outlives the call, e.g. a new CancellationTokenSource(30s) timeout CTS that is not disposed after the call completes, or a batch/saga token cancelled once the batch finishes. Session init succeeds and TickleTimer.StartAsync links the keepalive loop's lifetime CTS to that token (TickleTimer.cs:104 via SessionManager.cs:233). When the token later fires, Task.Delay at TickleTimer.cs:179 throws outside the try, RunAsync exits, and the canceled task sits unobserved — no log, no health change, no UnobservedTaskException. _state stays Ready so EnsureInitializedAsync never re-inits (SessionManager.cs:139) and successful reauths never recreate the timer (SessionManager.cs:404-405), so the loop is dead for the tenant's remaining lifetime. Per docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:6678 the brokerage session then times out server-side after a few quiet minutes; every REST call after an idle gap eats a 401+reauth round trip, any order place/modify/reply POST landing in such a gap is gated by ADR-0003 into IbkrAmbiguousOrderError requiring manual reconciliation (TokenRefreshHandler.cs:135-146), the WS tickle-watchdog backstop never fires again (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:748-763), and passive health keeps reporting authenticated=true indefinitely because the only staleness guard lives inside the dead loop (SessionHealthState.cs has no timestamps; TickleTimer.cs:270-275).
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Decouple the loop lifetime from the start token: in TickleTimer.StartAsync use a fresh CTS (or link to a SessionManager-owned dispose token) so the caller's token governs only startup; alternatively have SessionManager pass CancellationToken.None (or _disposeCts.Token) to StartAsync.
- **Suggested regression test:** TickleTimer_StartTokenCancelledAfterStart_LoopKeepsTickling — StartAsync with a caller CTS, cancel it after the first interval elapses, advance FakeTimeProvider further intervals, assert tickle count keeps increasing and StopAsync still terminates cleanly.

### STR-2 — high — SingleObserverChannelObservable/ChannelObservable (subscription disposal vs in-flight pump)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/SingleObserverChannelObservable.cs:30` · lens: streaming delivery & backpressure
- **Claim:** Subscribe fires PumpAsync as a dangling task (line 31) and SubscriptionSlot.Dispose (lines 57-67) cancels the CTS and frees the single-observer slot immediately without awaiting pump termination. The pump reads via ChannelReader.ReadAllAsync (ChannelObservable.cs:53, FanOutChannelObservable.cs:53), whose default implementation checks cancellation only in WaitToReadAsync — the inner TryRead loop drains every already-buffered frame (up to StreamingBufferSize=2048) to the now-disposed observer. Because the slot is freed instantly, a documented fresh Subscribe (class doc lines 8-10; pinned by Subscribe_AfterFirstDisposed_SecondSubscribeSucceeds) starts a second pump that races TryRead against the still-draining old pump, splitting buffered frames non-deterministically between the disposed observer and the new one.
- **Consequence:** In the dispose-then-resubscribe pattern the API explicitly invites, buffered sor/str frames (order state transitions, fills) are delivered to the discarded observer instead of the live one — silent fill/order-update loss with no drop-counter increment under any of the three ADR-0002 causes, violating the loss-is-observable contract on the money stream.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/SingleObserverChannelObservable.cs:30-32 (fire-and-forget pump, slot handed out), :57-67 (Dispose cancels + releases slot without joining the pump); src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/ChannelObservable.cs:53 and FanOutChannelObservable.cs:53 (ReadAllAsync — BCL default drains buffered items between cancellation checks); channel capacity src/IbkrConduit/Session/IbkrClientOptions.cs:89 (2048); tests pin only the empty-buffer case: tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Streaming/ChannelObservableTests.cs:57-86 (item written only after dispose settles) and :282-292 (resubscribe succeeds, no delivery-exclusivity assertion).
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Independently traced end-to-end from the public surface. (1) Reachability: IIbkrClient.Streaming (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IIbkrClient.cs:33) -> StreamingOperations.OrderUpdatesAsync / TradeExecutionsAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:106-122, 125-154) return IbkrSubscription<T> whose Stream is one stored FanOutChannelObservable instance (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrSubscription.cs:23) over a single shared bounded ChannelReader (Channel.CreateBounded, capacity StreamingBufferSize=2048, DropOldest — src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:237-243, src/IbkrConduit/Session/IbkrClientOptions.cs:89). Dispose-then-resubscribe therefore reuses the same reader. (2) Subscribe fires the pump fire-and-forget — `_ = PumpAsync(observer, cts.Token)` at src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/SingleObserverChannelObservable.cs:31; the Task is never retained. (3) SubscriptionSlot.Dispose (SingleObserverChannelObservable.cs:57-67) cancels the CTS (CancellationDisposable.cs:18-26) and calls ReleaseSlot (lines 66, 35) immediately, with no join on the pump. (4) The pump reads via `_reader.ReadAllAsync(cancellationToken)` (ChannelObservable.cs:53, FanOutChannelObservable.cs:53). The BCL default ChannelReader<T>.ReadAllAsync (not overridden by BoundedChannel) observes the token only in WaitToReadAsync; its inner `while (TryRead)` loop yields every buffered item without a cancellation check, so after Dispose the old pump keeps delivering buffered frames — and frames still arriving, as long as TryRead keeps succeeding — to the disposed observer, terminating only when it next sees an empty buffer (OCE -> OnCompleted, ChannelObservable.cs:87-90). (5) Because the slot is freed at Dispose rather than pump exit, the fresh Subscribe the API explicitly invites (class doc SingleObserverChannelObservable.cs:8-10 and exception text lines 25-27) starts a second pump on the same reader while the old one may still be draining; both race TryRead and frames split non-deterministically. BoundedChannel is internally locked (the SingleReader=true hint at IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:241 does not select an unsafe implementation for bounded channels), so no corruption — but exactly the "silently splitting delivery across two pumps" the class doc claims to prevent. (6) The loss is invisible: RecordDrop fires only for mapper/observer causes (ChannelObservable.cs:64,78; FanOutChannelObservable.cs:65,80) and overflow eviction (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:243); frames handed to the dead observer count as delivered. This violates ADR-0002's stated guarantee "at-most-once per subscriber, loss-is-observable ... never silently" (docs/adr/0002-streaming-delivery-guarantee.md:12). (7) Test pins confirm the gap rather than refute it: Subscribe_Dispose_StopsReceiving (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Streaming/ChannelObservableTests.cs:57-86) delays 100ms so the buffer is empty before Dispose (pinning only the parked-pump case), and both resubscribe tests (ChannelObservableTests.cs:282-293, FanOutChannelObservableTests.cs:241-251) assert only that the second Subscribe returns non-null — no delivery-exclusivity assertion. No VCR-merged code adds a pump join; VCR-02/FIL-5 introduced this slot mechanism without one. Secondary note: the connection-events channel is CreateUnbounded with SingleReader=true (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:308), which does select the single-consumer implementation where two concurrent readers is a genuine contract violation — the same dispose/resubscribe race reaches it via ConnectionEventObservable. No live IBKR wire behavior is involved; the trigger is purely consumer-side and deterministic given buffered frames.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer holds a live sor or str subscription from IIbkrClient.Streaming, disposes the IDisposable returned by Stream.Subscribe (e.g., to swap observers — the pattern the exception message and class doc explicitly direct: "Dispose the existing subscription before subscribing again"), and immediately calls Subscribe again on the same IIbkrSubscription.Stream. Any frames buffered in the topic's 2048-slot channel at dispose time (routine on a hot stream — the socket receive loop writes independently of consumer OnNext pace, and fills burst) are drained by the still-running old pump to the discarded observer, because ReadAllAsync's inner TryRead loop never checks the cancelled token; simultaneously the new pump starts (the slot was freed synchronously in Dispose) and the two pumps race TryRead, splitting subsequent frames non-deterministically between the dead and live observers until the old pump happens to see an empty buffer. Order-state transitions and trade executions land on the discarded observer and vanish; no drop counter increments (the frames were "delivered") and no log fires, so the consumer's live observer silently misses fills/order updates with no reconciliation trigger — directly violating ADR-0002's loss-is-observable guarantee on the money streams. Severity high rather than critical: the loss window exists only during a consumer-initiated dispose-and-resubscribe transition, not in the steady-state subscribe-once lifecycle.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Check the token per item in the pump loop (manual WaitToReadAsync/TryRead loop with ct.IsCancellationRequested per iteration), and have SubscriptionSlot.Dispose release the single-observer slot only after the pump task has observed cancellation and exited (store the pump Task; ReleaseSlot in its continuation).
- **Suggested regression test:** Subscribe_DisposeWithBufferedFrames_ResubscribedObserverReceivesAllRemainingFrames — prefill the channel with N frames while the first observer's OnNext blocks, dispose the first slot, subscribe a second observer, assert the first observer receives no further OnNext after Dispose returns and the second receives every undelivered frame.

### STR-3 — high — IbkrWebSocketClient (DisposeAsync vs in-flight reconnect)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:387` · lens: streaming delivery & backpressure
- **Claim:** DisposeAsync never acquires _connectLock: it disposes the semaphore (line 429) while a watchdog/session-refresh ReconnectAsync may hold it, and runs DisconnectAsync concurrently with the reconnect's own DisconnectAsync (racing double Cancel/Dispose on _heartbeatCts/_messagePumpCts at lines 494-523 → ObjectDisposedException out of DisposeAsync). Worse: ReconnectAsync checks _disposed only once at entry (line 681) with the notifier's token — not _disposeCts (OnTickleSucceededAsync/OnSessionRefreshedAsync pass the TickleTimer/SessionManager token, lines 745/763; SessionLifecycleNotifier.cs:124/138 subscription disposal only delists the callback, an in-flight invocation continues). ConnectCoreAsync has no _disposed check, and DisposeAsync clears _subscribers but never _subscriptions (lines 405-417), so a reconnect that slipped past the entry check completes AFTER disposal: it opens a new socket (line 472-473), starts a fresh heartbeat and pump (484-485) whose CTSes nothing ever cancels, and replays all subscriptions (486, 723-735).
- **Consequence:** A client disposed during a reconnect window (e.g. IbkrClientManager tenant remove/re-add during connection churn — precisely when reconnects are in flight) leaves a zombie WebSocket session at IBKR: live wire subscriptions streaming into cleared writer lists, a heartbeat sending 'tic' forever, competing with the replacement client's session (IBKR competing-WS behavior itself unpinned); additionally DisposeAsync can throw ObjectDisposedException at the consumer, and the reconnect's finally Release on the disposed semaphore throws into a fire-and-forget task.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:387-435 (DisposeAsync: no lock acquisition, _connectLock.Dispose at 429, _subscriptions not cleared), :679-721 (ReconnectAsync: single _disposed check at 681, ConnectCoreAsync at 707), :437-487 (ConnectCoreAsync: no disposed gate, new socket 472-473, StartHeartbeat/StartMessagePump/replay 484-486), :737-769 (notifier callbacks pass external tokens), :494-523 (DisconnectAsync CTS mutation, unsynchronized); src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionLifecycleNotifier.cs:113-139 (subscription dispose only delists; in-flight callback unaffected). Test SessionRefreshAfterDispose_DoesNotReconnect (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClientTests.cs:451-467) pins only the sequential case, not the interleaved race.
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Independently traced at HEAD. Mechanism: DisposeAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:387-435) never acquires _connectLock, disposes it at :429, clears _subscribers (:417) but never clears _subscriptions (only UnsubscribeSolicitedAsync removes entries, :804). ReconnectAsync checks _disposed only once at entry (:681-684), then holds _connectLock across DisconnectAsync (:702), a fixed 1000ms Task.Delay (:706, _reconnectDelayMs=1000 at :24), and ConnectCoreAsync (:707), which has NO disposed gate (:437-487): it opens a new socket (:472-473), starts a fresh heartbeat whose CTS (:530) only DisconnectAsync can ever cancel, starts the pump (:485), and replays every never-cleared subscription (:486, :723-735). In-flight notifier callbacks survive subscription disposal (SessionLifecycleNotifier.cs:39-56 snapshot; Dispose at :124/:138 only delists). Counter-evidence I found narrows but does not refute: (a) pump/heartbeat-triggered reconnects use _disposeCts.Token (:549, :596, :616), which DisposeAsync cancels at :402 — cooperatively aborted except the narrow window after ws.ConnectAsync completes; (b) TickleTimer-token reconnects (tickle_watchdog :748-769, tickle-driven reauth via TickleTimer.cs:208/259) are cancelled AND awaited before the WS client disposes, because provider teardown disposes the IbkrClient facade first (reverse creation order), which calls SessionManager.DisposeAsync (IbkrClient.cs:134) → TickleTimer.StopAsync (SessionManager.cs:463-466; TickleTimer.cs:112-143 awaits the background task, which is awaiting the in-flight ReconnectAsync). BUT (c) the 401-recovery path is fully open: TokenRefreshHandler.cs:98 calls ReauthenticateAsync with the consumer's per-request HTTP token; SessionManager.cs:408 NotifyAsync(that token) → OnSessionRefreshedAsync (:737-746) → ReconnectAsync("session_refresh", requestToken). Teardown neither cancels this token nor waits for the reauth — SessionManager.DisposeAsync disposes _semaphore at :485 without acquiring it, and the ShuttingDown guard (:292) is entry-only. So a tenant disposal (ManagedTenant.cs:28-48 → provider.DisposeAsync) landing inside the reconnect's 1000ms delay completes fully (WS client DisposeAsync's DisconnectAsync at :403 is a no-op because the reconnect already nulled _webSocket and both CTSes at :494-523); then ConnectCoreAsync runs ungated: signs a tickle, opens a new IBKR WebSocket, starts an unkillable heartbeat and pump, and replays all subscriptions. DisposeAsync re-entry no-ops (:389-392); a later heartbeat-failure reconnect self-aborts on _disposed (:681), so the heartbeat loop breaks only when the socket itself errors — until then 'tic' keepalives hold the IBKR session open indefinitely while frames route into the cleared _subscribers map. The reconnect's finally _connectLock.Release() (:719) throws ObjectDisposedException, swallowed by the notifier catch (SessionLifecycleNotifier.cs:51). Secondary hazard: DisposeAsync's DisconnectAsync (:403) can interleave with the reconnect's own DisconnectAsync/StartHeartbeat CTS mutation (:494-530, unsynchronized _heartbeatCts/_messagePumpCts) → Cancel-after-Dispose ObjectDisposedException propagating out of the consumer-facing DisposeAsync. Tests pin only sequential dispose-then-event ordering (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClientTests.cs:77, 327, 451-467, 865) — no interleaved pin; nothing in the merged VCR-01..11 stream touched this. Severity: high per rubric — session/liveness integrity failure (disposed client leaves a live authenticated IBKR WS session with active subscriptions and keepalive; DisposeAsync can also throw at the consumer). Not critical: the disposed client's channels are completed so no consumer reads wrong data from it; the claim that the zombie corrupts the REPLACEMENT tenant's stream depends on IBKR competing-WebSocket-session behavior, which is not pinned by docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md or recordings/.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer REST call hits a 401 (LST expiry or competing-session churn), so TokenRefreshHandler (TokenRefreshHandler.cs:98) runs SessionManager.ReauthenticateAsync on the consumer's request token; the successful reauth fires NotifyAsync (SessionManager.cs:408) → IbkrWebSocketClient.ReconnectAsync("session_refresh"), which passes its single entry _disposed check (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:681), acquires _connectLock, tears down the old socket, and sits in its fixed 1000ms Task.Delay (:706). During that window the operator removes the tenant (ManagedTenant.DisposeAsync → provider.DisposeAsync): SessionManager.DisposeAsync does not wait on its semaphore (:485) and nothing cancels the request token, so IbkrWebSocketClient.DisposeAsync runs to completion — no lock taken, _subscribers cleared but _subscriptions left intact (:417), _connectLock disposed (:429). The delay then elapses and ConnectCoreAsync (no disposed gate, :437-487) opens a brand-new authenticated IBKR WebSocket, starts a heartbeat and message pump whose fresh CTSes nothing will ever cancel (DisposeAsync cannot run again, :389-392), and replays every subscription (:486, :723-735); the finally-block Release on the disposed semaphore throws ObjectDisposedException that the notifier swallows (SessionLifecycleNotifier.cs:51). Result: a zombie IBKR WS session held open indefinitely by 'tic' keepalives, streaming replayed subscription data into cleared writer lists, occupying the tenant's session slot and — if the tenant is re-added — competing with the replacement client's WebSocket (IBKR competing-WS behavior unpinned); a tighter interleaving of the two unsynchronized DisconnectAsync calls (:494-523) can additionally throw ObjectDisposedException out of DisposeAsync at the consumer.
- **Corroboration:** CON (independent variant at IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:387 with the same mechanism — no _connectLock in DisposeAsync, single _disposed check at :681, non-dispose tokens from SessionManager.cs:408 / TickleTimer.cs:219 defeating _disposeCts.CancelAsync at :402, finally Release ObjectDisposedException at :719 swallowed by SessionLifecycleNotifier.cs:47-54)
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Have DisposeAsync acquire _connectLock before tearing down (or gate ConnectCoreAsync on _disposed inside the lock and re-check after every await); link reconnect work to _disposeCts regardless of the caller's token; clear _subscriptions on dispose so a straggler replay has nothing to resubscribe.
- **Suggested regression test:** DisposeAsync_WhileWatchdogReconnectInFlight_NoSocketOrHeartbeatSurvives — block FakeWebSocketAdapter.ConnectAsync on a TCS from a watchdog-triggered reconnect, run DisposeAsync to completion, release the TCS, then assert no new adapter reaches Open state, no subscribe replay is sent, and no heartbeat fires after time advance.

### AUT-1 — high — OAuthSigningHandler (base-string URL selection)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Auth/OAuthSigningHandler.cs:77` · lens: OAuth signing & credential hygiene
