- **Claim:** The OAuth signature is computed over `request.RequestUri!.ToString()` (src/IbkrConduit/Auth/OAuthSigningHandler.cs:77), which returns the SafeUnescaped form of the URI — .NET's Uri.ToString() un-escapes percent-encoded octets such as %20 (space) and most non-ASCII sequences — while the wire request-target sent by SocketsHttpHandler uses the escaped form (AbsoluteUri/PathAndQuery). Refit percent-encodes query values via Uri.EscapeDataString, so any query value that needs escaping (e.g. IContractOperations symbol search with IBKR's real class-share symbology 'BRK B' → wire 'symbol=BRK%20B') produces a signed URL ('...symbol=BRK B' → QuotePlus → 'BRK+B', StandardBaseStringBuilder.cs:18) that differs byte-for-byte from the URL the server received. A byte-exact HMAC verification on the server cannot match both forms; live E2E/smoke evidence only pins the scheme for queries with no escapable characters (examples/SubmitAndMonitorOrders.cs:37 uses 'SPY').
- **Consequence:** Any API call whose query (or path) value contains a space or non-ASCII character fails with 401. TokenRefreshHandler then runs a full re-authentication (new LST handshake + ssodh/init, TokenRefreshHandler.cs:98 → SessionManager.ReauthenticateAsync) and the retry re-signs the same divergent URL and 401s again — so the consumer gets a hard auth failure on a legitimate request PLUS gratuitous session churn (brokerage re-init, notifier fire) on every attempt, on a money-boundary tenant whose streaming session depends on session stability.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Auth/OAuthSigningHandler.cs:77 (ToString), src/IbkrConduit/Auth/StandardBaseStringBuilder.cs:18-21 (QuotePlus of that URL into the base string), src/IbkrConduit/Auth/OAuthHeaderBuilder.cs:54-56 (signature over that base string), src/IbkrConduit/Contracts/IIbkrContractApi.cs:15 ([Query] string symbol reachable via IIbkrClient.Contracts), src/IbkrConduit/Session/TokenRefreshHandler.cs:71-98,150-155 (401 → re-auth → retry re-signed the same way), src/IbkrConduit/Http/ConsumerPipelineRegistration.cs:99-126 (handler ordering making retry re-enter OAuthSigningHandler)
- **Skeptic verdict:** **PLAUSIBLE** — Independent end-to-end trace. (1) Reachability: IIbkrClient.Contracts (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IIbkrClient.cs:18) -> ContractOperations.SearchBySymbolAsync passes the caller's symbol through unmodified (src/IbkrConduit/Client/ContractOperations.cs:38-47) -> Refit [Query] string symbol on GET /iserver/secdef/search (src/IbkrConduit/Contracts/IIbkrContractApi.cs:13-20). Refit encodes query values with Uri.EscapeDataString, so a symbol like "BRK B" becomes "symbol=BRK%20B" in the request URI. (2) Signing: the pipeline registers OAuthSigningHandler innermost (src/IbkrConduit/Http/ConsumerPipelineRegistration.cs:120-126); it signs `request.RequestUri!.ToString()` (src/IbkrConduit/Auth/OAuthSigningHandler.cs:77). .NET Uri.ToString() returns the SafeUnescaped form: it un-escapes %20 to a space and non-ASCII UTF-8 sequences, while keeping RFC2396-reserved escapes (%2C, %2F, %3D, %26...) escaped — documented framework behavior (I could not execute a probe under the read-only constraint). SocketsHttpHandler writes the request-target in UriEscaped form, so the wire keeps %20. StandardBaseStringBuilder embeds the URL string byte-for-byte via QuotePlus (src/IbkrConduit/Auth/StandardBaseStringBuilder.cs:15-21 — query params are NOT parsed or renormalized), and OAuthHeaderBuilder signs that base string (src/IbkrConduit/Auth/OAuthHeaderBuilder.cs:54-56). So for any query value containing a space or non-ASCII char, the signed URL ("...symbol=BRK B" -> QuotePlus -> "BRK+B") diverges byte-for-byte from the wire request-target ("BRK%20B"). Client-side mechanism: CONFIRMED. (3) Counter-evidence hunted: (a) live/E2E successes with comma-separated values (conids=..., fields=31%2C84%2C86) do NOT prove the server tolerates the divergence, because SafeUnescaped keeps %2C escaped — signed and wire forms match for commas; only %20/non-ASCII diverge; (b) grep found no %20 in recordings/, tests/, or examples/ — no capture pins a successfully-signed space-bearing query; (c) tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Auth/OAuthEncodingTests.cs:9-18 pins only the client-side quote_plus algorithm, never server acceptance; (d) docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md contains no OAuth base-string/verification documentation (grep for "base string"/"quote_plus"/"signature" — only unrelated "Signatures and Owners" endpoint); (e) the VCR-01..11 fix stream did not touch Auth/ signing; (f) LiveSessionTokenClient.cs:59 signs a query-less URL, so the LST flow is unaffected. (4) The decisive unknown that blocks CONFIRMED: IBKR's server-side verifier. If it rebuilds its base string from the DECODED request URL then quote_plus's it (as IBKR's own Python demo semantics suggest), the client's unescaped form matches exactly ("BRK B" -> "BRK+B" both sides) and there is NO defect; if it uses the raw request-target ("BRK%20B" -> "BRK%2520B"), the HMAC mismatches and the call 401s. Neither is pinned by the captured spec or recordings — this is unpinned live IBKR wire behavior. (5) Consequence if the mismatch is real: TokenRefreshHandler (outer handler, ConsumerPipelineRegistration.cs:99-103) sees the 401 (src/IbkrConduit/Session/TokenRefreshHandler.cs:71-76), runs a full ReauthenticateAsync (new LST + ssodh/init, TokenRefreshHandler.cs:98), then re-sends a clone with the same RequestUri (TokenRefreshHandler.cs:150-155, CloneRequest at :219) through the same inner chain — re-signed with the same divergence -> 401 again. Worse, if the session-health snapshot shows Competing, the post-retry 401 is thrown as an IbkrSessionError with IsCompeting=true (TokenRefreshHandler.cs:167-176), misdirecting the consumer's session-loss recovery at what is actually a signing bug. Severity judged for the impact if real: deterministic hard failure of a legitimate request category plus gratuitous full re-auth/brokerage re-init per attempt on a tenant whose streaming session depends on stability, plus possible competing-session misclassification = session/liveness integrity -> high. Verdict is PLAUSIBLE, not CONFIRMED, solely because the server-side verification behavior is unpinned and could equally make the client's form the correct one.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer calls IIbkrClient.Contracts.SearchBySymbolAsync("BRK B") (IBKR's space-separated class-share symbology) or any GET facade method whose [Query] string value contains a space or non-ASCII character. Refit puts "symbol=BRK%20B" on the wire, but OAuthSigningHandler.cs:77 signs Uri.ToString()'s SafeUnescaped form ("symbol=BRK B" -> quote_plus -> "BRK+B" in the base string, StandardBaseStringBuilder.cs:18-21) — a byte-for-byte different string from the request-target the server received. IF IBKR's verifier computes its base string from the raw request-target (unpinned — if it decodes first, the signature matches and nothing fails), the call returns 401; TokenRefreshHandler then runs a full re-authentication (new LST handshake + ssodh/init brokerage re-init, TokenRefreshHandler.cs:98) and retries the identically-mis-signed clone (TokenRefreshHandler.cs:150-155,219), which 401s again — so the consumer gets a hard, unrecoverable auth error on a legitimate request, a gratuitous session-churning re-auth on every attempt under its live streaming session, and (when the health snapshot shows a competing session) the failure surfaces as a competing-session IbkrSessionError (TokenRefreshHandler.cs:167-176) that can trip the consumer's session-loss recovery. Commas and other reserved characters are unaffected (SafeUnescaped keeps %2C escaped, matching the wire), which is exactly why all existing live/E2E evidence (SPY, comma-joined conids/fields) passes without exercising this divergence.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Sign the exact bytes that go on the wire: use request.RequestUri.AbsoluteUri (or Scheme+Authority+PathAndQuery) instead of ToString() in OAuthSigningHandler, and pin the server's accepted form with an attended live probe of a space-bearing query (e.g. secdef/search?symbol=BRK B) before shipping, since IBKR's exact canonicalization is unpinned.
- **Suggested regression test:** OAuthSigningHandler_QueryWithEscapedCharacters_SignsWireForm — fake inner handler captures the Authorization header for a GET to 'iserver/secdef/search?symbol=BRK%20B' and asserts the recomputed HMAC over the AbsoluteUri-based base string equals the transmitted oauth_signature (fails today because ToString() unescapes %20).

### WIR-1 — high — OrderUpdateMapper / PnlUpdateMapper (sor/spl frame fan-out)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/OrderUpdateMapper.cs:31` · lens: wire fidelity & serialization
- **Claim:** Per-element mapper isolation (FIL-2) was applied only to the str topic in VCR-03; OrderUpdateMapper.MapMany deserializes each sor args element with no try/catch (OrderUpdateMapper.cs:31), and FanOutChannelObservable materializes the whole frame via _mapper(frame).ToList() before delivering anything (FanOutChannelObservable.cs:61), so a single malformed element (e.g. a money field carrying a non-empty non-numeric string, which EmptyTolerantNullableDecimalConverter rethrows as JsonException at EmptyTolerantNumberConverters.cs:266) discards EVERY order update in that sor frame — including fill/status transitions for unrelated healthy orders — counted as one cause=mapper frame drop. PnlUpdateMapper.MapMany (PnlUpdateMapper.cs:26) has the same unguarded per-entry Deserialize, so one bad account entry drops all accounts' PnL in the frame.
- **Consequence:** A real-money consumer's order-state tracking silently misses status/fill transitions for orders that shared a sor frame with one type-drifted element; the single frame-drop metric gives no indication of how many orders were lost or which, and sor deltas are not replayed — the loss persists until REST reconciliation. This is the same defect class VCR-03 fixed for str fills, on the more order-critical topic, and the FanOut all-or-nothing materialization makes it strictly worse than the pre-VCR-03 tail-only loss.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/OrderUpdateMapper.cs:29-44 (unguarded element.Deserialize, no onElementDropped); src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/FanOutChannelObservable.cs:55-67 (ToList + whole-frame catch, comment at :59-60 explicitly scoping per-element isolation to VCR-03/str); src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:118-121 (sor wiring passes no drop callback) vs :146-153 (str wiring passes RecordMapperDrop); src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/TradeExecutionMapper.cs:46-53 (the str-only guard); src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/PnlUpdateMapper.cs:24-31; docs/backlog.md VCR-03 entry (scope decided as TradeExecutionMapper only)
- **Skeptic verdict:** **PLAUSIBLE** — Code-side mechanism fully traced end-to-end and confirmed at HEAD. Reachability: IIbkrClient.Streaming (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IIbkrClient.cs:33) -> StreamingOperations.OrderUpdatesAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:106-122); the sor mapper lambda (lines 118-119) passes only the WIR-5 census callback because OrderUpdateMapper.MapMany has no onElementDropped parameter at all (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/OrderUpdateMapper.cs:21-22). MapMany deserializes each args element with no per-element try/catch (OrderUpdateMapper.cs:29-31, lazy iterator). FanOutChannelObservable.PumpAsync materializes via _mapper(frame).ToList() (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/FanOutChannelObservable.cs:61) before delivering anything, so an exception on ANY element discards ALL elements of the frame — including ones before the bad element — via the catch at :63-67 (one RecordDrop cause=mapper + one warning log). The comment at :59-60 explicitly scopes per-element isolation to VCR-03. Throw paths are real: OrderUpdate.Size/Price/FilledQuantity/RemainingQuantity are decimal? (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/StreamingModels.cs:61,69,77,81) deserialized via EmptyTolerantNullableDecimalConverter, which throws JsonException on a non-empty non-numeric string (src/IbkrConduit/Serialization/EmptyTolerantNumberConverters.cs:264-266) and on unexpected token types (:47); Conid is int? (:49); a non-object args element also throws. PnlUpdateMapper.MapMany has the identical unguarded per-property Deserialize (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/PnlUpdateMapper.cs:24-31), wired to spl at StreamingOperations.cs:161. VCR-03 (Done #247) deliberately scoped FIL-2 isolation to TradeExecutionMapper only (docs/backlog.md:85-92 — Done-when names only str); the str wiring passes RecordMapperDrop (StreamingOperations.cs:146-153) into TradeExecutionMapper's per-element guard (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/TradeExecutionMapper.cs:45-54), while sor/spl got no equivalent — this is a residual gap of the VCR stream left open by VCR-03's scope, not a regression. Tests pin the current whole-frame-drop behavior (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Streaming/FanOutChannelObservableTests.cs:130-162); OrderUpdateMapperTests has no malformed-element case. Why PLAUSIBLE rather than CONFIRMED: the trigger requires IBKR to emit a sor args element outside the shapes the converters already tolerate (empty/whitespace string, quoted numbers, null — all handled; StreamingSerialization.cs:17-31), and neither the captured spec (docs/ibkr-websocket-api-reference.md:268-277 shows only well-formed sor frames) nor recordings/ (REST order captures only, no WS sor captures) pins such a malformed element ever occurring live. This is the same unpinned-trigger status FIL-2 had when it was confirmed and fixed at high for str — IBKR's demonstrated shape drift on this very topic (empty-string price on sor is why the converters exist) makes the class credible, but the specific throwing shape is unpinned wire behavior. Sweep inaccuracies noted but non-refuting: the converter file is under Serialization/ not Streaming/, and the loss is counted+logged (not wholly silent) — though the counter gives no indication of which or how many orders were lost. Severity high per rubric: if triggered, a real-money consumer silently misses status/fill transitions for healthy orders sharing the frame (silently wrong order-state data from the consumer's seat); sor deltas are not replayed, and a lost terminal-status frame persists until REST reconciliation. Consistent with FIL-2's accepted high rating on the less order-critical str topic.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer subscribes via IIbkrClient.Streaming.OrderUpdatesAsync (sor) while holding several live orders. IBKR delivers one sor frame whose args array carries updates for multiple orders, and one element carries a type-drifted field the tolerant converters reject — e.g. "price":"n/a" or a non-numeric "conid" (JsonException at EmptyTolerantNumberConverters.cs:266 or :47). Because OrderUpdateMapper.MapMany has no per-element guard (OrderUpdateMapper.cs:31) and FanOutChannelObservable materializes the whole frame before delivery (FanOutChannelObservable.cs:61), the exception discards every OrderUpdate in that frame — including fill/status transitions for unrelated healthy orders, even those preceding the bad element — recorded only as a single cause=mapper frame-drop counter increment and one warning log that identifies neither the orders nor the count. sor deltas are not replayed, so a lost terminal transition (e.g. Filled/Cancelled) stays invisible to the consumer's order-state tracking until an out-of-band REST reconciliation; the same repeated malformed element re-drops every subsequent frame it appears in. PnlUpdateMapper has the identical unguarded shape (PnlUpdateMapper.cs:26), so one bad account entry in an spl frame drops all accounts' PnL for that frame. The trigger depends on IBKR emitting a sor element outside the already-tolerated shapes, which no recording or captured-spec entry pins — the same unpinned trigger class that FIL-2/VCR-03 was fixed for on str.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Give OrderUpdateMapper.MapMany (and PnlUpdateMapper.MapMany) the same materialize-then-yield per-element try/catch with an onElementDropped callback wired to RecordMapperDrop, exactly as TradeExecutionMapper.MapMany does; the ssd/sld row loops share the pattern and can adopt it too.
- **Suggested regression test:** OrderUpdateMapper_MalformedElement_YieldsRemainingOrders — mock-WS/unit test feeding a sor frame with two args elements, the first carrying price:"N/A"; asserts the second order's status update is still delivered and one cause=mapper drop is counted.

### WIR-3 — high — Position / LedgerEntry (portfolio REST wire mapping — presence erasure outside VCR-01 scope)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Portfolio/IIbkrPortfolioApiModels.cs:177` · lens: wire fidelity & serialization
- **Claim:** Position's money/quantity fields (position, mktPrice, mktValue, avgCost, avgPrice, realizedPnl, unrealizedPnl — IIbkrPortfolioApiModels.cs:177-183) and every LedgerEntry balance field (:267-290 area, e.g. cashbalance, netliquidationvalue, settledcash) are NON-nullable decimal, so the globally registered EmptyTolerantDecimalConverter maps an omitted, JSON-null, or empty-string wire value to a fabricated 0m (EmptyTolerantNumberConverters.cs:23-24 '?? default'). VCR-01's presence retrofit covered only OrderUpdate/TradeExecution/Trade/LiveOrder/session flags, and the prior adversarial review explicitly declared portfolio endpoints out of scope — this surface still violates ADR-0001 presence semantics.
- **Consequence:** A consumer reconciling positions or balances reads a silently fabricated zero (Quantity=0 = 'flat', CashBalance=0) where IBKR sent nothing — the exact absence-as-zero corruption class rated critical in the sor review; the streaming twin of the ledger (AccountLedgerRow) already documents blank rows carrying only key+timestamp (StreamingModels.cs:174-177), so partially-populated ledger data from this source is a pinned-adjacent reality, not pure hypothesis.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Portfolio/IIbkrPortfolioApiModels.cs:177-183 (Position), :267-290 (LedgerEntry non-nullable decimals); src/IbkrConduit/Serialization/EmptyTolerantNumberConverters.cs:19-26 ('':""->default 0m for non-nullable decimal); src/IbkrConduit/Serialization/IbkrRefitSettings.cs:45 (converter applied to all Refit clients); src/IbkrConduit/Client/IPortfolioOperations.cs:23,67,132 (public reachability); src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/StreamingModels.cs:174-177 (blank-row semantics of the same ledger source); docs/findings/2026-07-04-rtos-venue-consumer-review.md:572 (portfolio out of prior scope)
- **Skeptic verdict:** **PLAUSIBLE** — Code-side mechanism independently confirmed end-to-end. Reachability: IIbkrClient.Portfolio (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IIbkrClient.cs:13) -> IPortfolioOperations.GetPositionsAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IPortfolioOperations.cs:23), GetLedgerAsync (:40), GetPositionByConidAsync (:67), GetRealTimePositionsAsync (:132); the portfolio Refit client is in the default consumer pipeline (src/IbkrConduit/Http/ConsumerPipelineRegistration.cs:47). DTOs: Position's seven money/quantity fields are non-nullable decimal (src/IbkrConduit/Portfolio/IIbkrPortfolioApiModels.cs:177-183) and every LedgerEntry balance field is non-nullable decimal (:267-297). Converter: EmptyTolerantDecimalConverter maps "" -> ParseDecimalOrNull(...) ?? default = 0m and JSON null -> default = 0m (src/IbkrConduit/Serialization/EmptyTolerantNumberConverters.cs:23-24), registered on all Refit clients (src/IbkrConduit/Serialization/IbkrRefitSettings.cs:45); a wholly-omitted field also yields default(decimal)=0m via the positional-record ctor. No VCR PR touched this file (git log: last change 83b67c8, pre-VCR) and the prior review explicitly scoped portfolio out (docs/findings/2026-07-04-rtos-venue-consumer-review.md:572). One mitigation the sweep understated: VCR-10's ResponseSchemaValidationHandler IS in the portfolio pipeline (ConsumerPipelineRegistration.cs:105) and treats Position's no-default money fields as required (src/IbkrConduit/Http/DtoFieldMap.cs:99), so a WHOLLY OMITTED field is detected — but StrictResponseValidation defaults to false (src/IbkrConduit/Session/IbkrClientOptions.cs:102), so by default it only logs a warning while the deserialized DTO still hands the consumer a fabricated 0m; and a field present with JSON null or "" is in responseFields so it is neither extra nor missing (ResponseSchemaValidationHandler.cs:128-152) — that arm is fully silent even in strict mode. Why not CONFIRMED: the concrete trigger (IBKR actually sending absent/null/"" for these REST portfolio money fields) is not pinned — recordings/ contains only order captures (grep for cashbalance/mktValue in recordings/ is empty) and the DTOs were generated from full-field recorded responses. Adjacent evidence makes the trigger realistic rather than hypothetical: the captured spec's own ledger example object omits many documented fields (docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:5826-5843), its positions example carries JSON nulls for inapplicable fields (:5582-5585), the streaming twin AccountLedgerRow of the same ledger source is fully nullable with documented blank rows carrying only key+timestamp (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/StreamingModels.cs:173-177, 192-205), and AccountSummaryEntry carries IBKR's own isNull flag (IIbkrPortfolioApiModels.cs:217) — but documented-but-unverified is not pinned per this repo's own contract rules. Severity re-judged as high (not the claimed medium): if triggered, a consumer reads silently fabricated money numerics (CashBalance=0, Quantity=0) — the rubric's "silently wrong data" tier; not critical because it corrupts read-side reconciliation rather than directly duplicating/losing orders or fills, and the full-omission arm is at least observable via a warning log.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer calls client.Portfolio.GetLedgerAsync (or GetPositionsAsync/GetRealTimePositionsAsync) and IBKR returns a ledger/position object with a money field absent, JSON null, or "" — a shape the spec's own examples depict for the ledger (partial objects, docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:5826-5843) and which the streaming twin of the same ledger source demonstrably emits every 10s as blank rows (StreamingModels.cs:174-177). Because Position's seven money/quantity fields and every LedgerEntry balance field are non-nullable decimal (IIbkrPortfolioApiModels.cs:177-183, 267-297), the globally-registered EmptyTolerantDecimalConverter (EmptyTolerantNumberConverters.cs:23-24) materializes the absent value as 0m: the consumer's reconciliation loop reads Quantity=0 ("flat" when a position exists) or CashBalance=0/NetLiquidationValue=0 and acts on it. The null/"" arms are completely silent; the full-omission arm is caught by the VCR-10 schema net but, with StrictResponseValidation defaulting to false (IbkrClientOptions.cs:102), only as a log warning while the fabricated zero is still returned. The code-side mechanism is fully traced; only the live-wire frequency of IBKR sending sparse REST portfolio payloads is unpinned by recordings, which is what keeps this PLAUSIBLE rather than CONFIRMED.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Retrofit Position and LedgerEntry money fields to nullable decimal per ADR-0001 (breaking, feat!:, semver review per backlog-format 📦 rules), mirroring the VCR-01 treatment of LiveOrder/Trade.
- **Suggested regression test:** Position_EmptyStringOrOmittedMoneyField_DeserializesToNull — fixture position row with "mktPrice":"" and omitted "unrealizedPnl" asserts null (not 0m) through the DI/WireMock stack.

### AUT-2 — medium — IbkrOAuthCredentials (public credential record)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Auth/IbkrOAuthCredentials.cs:19` · lens: OAuth signing & credential hygiene
- **Claim:** IbkrOAuthCredentials is a public positional record with no ToString override, so the compiler-generated ToString()/PrintMembers prints AccessToken and EncryptedAccessTokenSecret (and ConsumerKey) in cleartext. The record is held on the public options surface (IbkrClientOptions.Credentials, src/IbkrConduit/Session/IbkrClientOptions.cs:17), so any consumer-side structured-logging destructure ('{@Options}' / '{Creds}'), exception-context dump, or string interpolation of the options/credentials object writes live OAuth credential material to logs — directly violating the repo's own credential-sanitization rule (.claude/rules/security.md).
- **Consequence:** OAuth access token and encrypted access-token secret leak into consumer log sinks/observability backends; combined with the consumer key (also printed) this materially aids credential compromise of a real-money trading tenant.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Auth/IbkrOAuthCredentials.cs:19-26 (public record, string AccessToken / string EncryptedAccessTokenSecret positional params, no ToString override anywhere in the file), src/IbkrConduit/Session/IbkrClientOptions.cs:17 (public IbkrOAuthCredentials? Credentials { get; set; } — reachable public surface)
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Independently traced end-to-end. (1) src/IbkrConduit/Auth/IbkrOAuthCredentials.cs:19-26 declares a public, non-partial positional record with string ConsumerKey, string AccessToken, string EncryptedAccessTokenSecret as positional parameters; the record body (lines 27-37) contains only Dispose() — no ToString or PrintMembers override, and being non-partial none can exist elsewhere (grep across src/IbkrConduit/Auth confirmed). The compiler-generated ToString therefore renders all public properties, printing AccessToken and EncryptedAccessTokenSecret in cleartext plus ConsumerKey and the full decimal DhPrime (RSA properties print only the CLR type name). (2) Reachable public surface: IbkrClientOptions.Credentials (src/IbkrConduit/Session/IbkrClientOptions.cs:17), IIbkrClientManager.AddAsync parameter (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IIbkrClientManager.cs:24), and OAuthCredentialsFactory.FromEnvironment/FromJson/FromFile returns (src/IbkrConduit/Auth/OAuthCredentialsFactory.cs:21,51,80). (3) Counter-evidence hunt found none: the library itself never logs/interpolates the object (only ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(options.Credentials, ...) at src/IbkrConduit/Http/ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:180, which emits the param name only); no test pins redaction; the repo's sanitization posture is deliberate elsewhere (AuditLogHandler redacts Authorization headers and sanitizes URLs, src/IbkrConduit/Http/AuditLogHandler.cs:11,58,104), so the unredacted record contradicts .claude/rules/security.md rather than reflecting a considered choice; the VCR-01..11 fix stream (docs/findings/2026-07-04-rtos-venue-consumer-review.md, PRs #238-#249) never touched this. (4) Severity re-judged: the sweep's compromise claim is partially overstated — RSA.ToString prints only the type name so neither private key leaks, and EncryptedAccessTokenSecret is ciphertext useless without EncryptionPrivateKey; OAuth 1.0a signing to IBKR additionally requires the signature private key, so the printed material alone cannot authenticate a session. What leaks is the long-lived cleartext AccessToken plus ConsumerKey — real credential material into consumer log sinks on a very common accident (string interpolation, non-destructured {Creds} message property, exception-context enricher), a genuine hardening defect on the public surface of a money boundary but requiring consumer-side logging of the object and yielding an incomplete credential set. That places it at medium (security/observability hardening gap on the public surface), not high.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer (e.g. RTOS) holds the IbkrOAuthCredentials record or IbkrClientOptions and, in ordinary diagnostic code, writes it to a log — logger.LogInformation("venue creds: {Creds}", creds), $"...{options.Credentials}" interpolation, a Serilog {@Options} destructure of the options graph, or an exception-context enricher that captures the options object. Because the record at src/IbkrConduit/Auth/IbkrOAuthCredentials.cs:19 has no ToString/PrintMembers override, the compiler-generated formatter emits "IbkrOAuthCredentials { TenantId = ..., ConsumerKey = <9-char key>, AccessToken = <live cleartext OAuth token>, EncryptedAccessTokenSecret = <base64 ciphertext>, ... }" into the consumer's log sink/observability backend, persisting the tenant's long-lived IBKR access token and consumer key in cleartext — violating the library's own credential-sanitization rule. The RSA private keys and the decrypted secret do not print, so the leak alone cannot forge IBKR requests, but it durably exposes two of the four credential components of a real-money trading tenant to anyone with log access.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Override ToString() (sealed) to print only TenantId and redact all token fields (e.g. "IbkrOAuthCredentials { TenantId = x, ConsumerKey = ***, ... }"), mirroring the Flex-token redaction discipline already applied in AuditLogHandler.
- **Suggested regression test:** IbkrOAuthCredentials_ToString_DoesNotContainSecrets — construct with synthetic token values and assert ToString() contains neither the AccessToken nor the EncryptedAccessTokenSecret substrings.

### CON-2 — medium — IbkrWebSocketClient (subscribe vs dispose channel completion)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:246` · lens: concurrency, thread safety & disposal
- **Claim:** SubscribeTopicAsync / RegisterUnsolicitedTopic / RegisterConnectionEvents check _disposed (lines 231, 270, 304) and then add a channel writer to the registries (246-254, 280-284, 310-313) with no synchronization against DisposeAsync's completion sweep (405-427). If DisposeAsync's writer-complete loop and _subscribers.Clear() (line 417) run between the check and the add — or RegisterUnsolicitedTopic's GetOrAdd re-creates a topic list after Clear — the newly added writer is never TryComplete'd. The subscription's pump (ChannelObservable.PumpAsync, ChannelObservable.cs:53) then awaits ReadAllAsync on a channel that will never complete and never receive frames: the observer gets neither OnCompleted nor OnError.
- **Consequence:** A consumer that subscribes concurrently with tenant teardown (e.g. RTOS stream setup racing IbkrClientManager.RemoveAsync) gets a stream that silently never emits and never terminates — a wedge with no error signal, hiding that the subscription is dead.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:231-254 (check-then-add, no lock shared with dispose), :268-298, :302-325, :387-427 (dispose sets _disposed, completes a snapshot of writers, Clear at 417); src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/ChannelObservable.cs:49-95 (pump only terminates on channel completion, cancellation, or a frame)
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced end-to-end at HEAD. SubscribeTopicAsync checks _disposed (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:231) then adds a writer via _subscribers.GetOrAdd + writers.Add under _subscriptionLock/lock(writers) (:246-254); RegisterUnsolicitedTopic (:270, :280-284) and RegisterConnectionEvents (:304, :310-313) follow the same check-then-add shape. DisposeAsync (:387-435) sets _disposed=true (:394), awaits DisconnectAsync (:403 — an await that widens the window), sweeps _subscribers completing writers (:406-415), then _subscribers.Clear() (:417) — WITHOUT taking _subscriptionLock, and the subscribe paths never re-check _disposed inside their locks. A subscriber passing the :231 check just before :394 can GetOrAdd after :417's Clear, recreating a topic list whose writer nothing ever TryCompletes (ReconnectAsync early-returns on _disposed at :681-684; nothing else revisits the registry). The orphaned reader feeds ChannelObservable.PumpAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/ChannelObservable.cs:49-95), which terminates only on channel completion, observer throw, or the subscription's own dispose token — that token comes from SingleObserverChannelObservable.Subscribe (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/SingleObserverChannelObservable.cs:30-32) and is NOT linked to the client's _disposeCts, so the observer receives neither OnCompleted nor OnError. Reachability is public: IIbkrClient.Streaming.OrderUpdatesAsync/MarketDataAsync → SubscribeTopicAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:100, :113, :141); IIbkrClientManager.RemoveAsync → ManagedTenant.DisposeAsync → provider.DisposeAsync disposing the WS client (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IbkrClientManager.cs:133-143; src/IbkrConduit/Client/ManagedTenant.cs:46). No manager guard serializes in-flight streaming calls against teardown (MGR-3 at IbkrClientManager.cs:82-97 covers only the Add-vs-Dispose slot race). Counter-evidence hunt: tests pin only sequential orderings — DisposeAsync_CompletesSubscriberChannels (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClientTests.cs:337-347) and SubscribeAfterDispose_ThrowsObjectDisposedException (:352-361) — neither interleaves the window; volatile _disposed (:87) cannot close a check-then-act; the returned unsubscribe delegate would complete the orphan (:813) but only if the consumer proactively disposes a subscription it has no signal is dead. Purely code-side race, no live-wire dependency. Severity re-judged down from the wedge-of-pipeline framing: the race requires the consumer's own teardown of that tenant to overlap an in-flight subscribe on the same tenant; the tenant is going away anyway, so the damage is a leaked never-terminating pump and a silent dead subscription handle (hang risk only for code awaiting stream completion during shutdown/recycle) — a robustness/observability gap per the rubric, not silently wrong data.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer holds an IIbkrClient for tenant T and, on one thread, calls client.Streaming.OrderUpdatesAsync (or MarketDataAsync / SubscribeSessionStatus / SubscribeConnectionEvents) while another thread concurrently calls IIbkrClientManager.RemoveAsync("T") (or disposes the manager). The subscribe call passes the _disposed check at IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:231 just before DisposeAsync sets _disposed at :394; DisposeAsync then awaits DisconnectAsync, completes every registered writer (:406-415), and clears _subscribers (:417) — after which the subscribe's GetOrAdd (:246) re-creates the topic entry and registers a writer that no code will ever complete. The returned IIbkrSubscription's pump (ChannelObservable.cs:53) then awaits ReadAllAsync on a channel that will never receive a frame (message pump dead, reconnect disabled by _disposed) and never completes: the observer gets no OnNext, no OnError, and no OnCompleted. The consumer is left with a silently dead stream handle and a leaked pump task; any shutdown coordinator awaiting the stream's terminal event hangs indefinitely, with no error signal that the subscription died with the disposed tenant.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Take a registration lock shared with DisposeAsync (or re-check _disposed after adding and complete+remove the writer if set), mirroring the MGR-3 post-install re-check pattern used in IbkrClientManager.AddAsync.
- **Suggested regression test:** SubscribeTopicAsync_RacingDispose_CompletesReader — interleave DisposeAsync between the disposed-check and registry add (test seam or tight loop), then assert the returned ChannelReader completes rather than hanging.

### ERR-2 — medium — IbkrClient.ValidateFlexTokenAsync (startup flex-token validation classification)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Client/IbkrClient.cs:174` · lens: error taxonomy & result mapping
- **Claim:** Two misclassifications in ValidateConnectionAsync's flex probe: (a) a transient transport failure (FlexClient maps HttpRequestException to IbkrApiError, FlexClient.cs:93-100) falls to the non-IbkrFlexError branch and is thrown as IbkrConfigurationException("FlexToken") — the taxonomy's permanent fix-your-config type (IbkrConfigurationException doc vs IbkrTransientException doc, src/IbkrConduit/Errors/IbkrTransientException.cs:6-10) — for a wait-and-retry condition; (b) when IbkrClientOptions.ThrowOnApiError=true, FlexOperations.ExecuteQueryAsync's WithThrowSetting (FlexOperations.cs:119,236-237) throws IbkrApiException before control reaches the 1015/1012/1013 → IbkrConfigurationException mapping at IbkrClient.cs:147-171, so an invalid/expired flex token loses its actionable configuration classification.
- **Consequence:** A consumer startup orchestrator that retries on IbkrTransientException but halts-and-alerts on IbkrConfigurationException wedges its pipeline start on a network blip (permanent-looking error for a transient fault); conversely, under ThrowOnApiError=true a genuinely invalid token surfaces as a generic IbkrApiException, so the documented config-error handling path never fires.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Client/IbkrClient.cs:138-177 (mapping and fall-through throw at 174-176); src/IbkrConduit/Flex/FlexClient.cs:93-100 (transport → IbkrApiError, not IbkrFlexError); src/IbkrConduit/Client/FlexOperations.cs:236-237 (WithThrowSetting EnsureSuccess) and 119 (applied inside ExecuteQueryAsync before the facade's mapping); src/IbkrConduit/Session/IbkrClientOptions.cs:108 (ThrowOnApiError is a supported public option).
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced from the public surface: IIbkrClient.ValidateConnectionAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IIbkrClient.cs:88) -> IbkrClient.ValidateConnectionAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IbkrClient.cs:111-129) -> ValidateFlexTokenAsync (IbkrClient.cs:138-177). Branch (a): FlexClient.GetXmlAsync catches HttpRequestException and returns an IbkrApiError, not an IbkrFlexError (src/IbkrConduit/Flex/FlexClient.cs:93-100; ditto non-XML bodies at 108-115). FlexOperations.ExecuteInternalAsync returns that transport failure immediately with no retry ("don't retry network errors", src/IbkrConduit/Client/FlexOperations.cs:151-156), and ExecuteQueryAsync's Map preserves the error (FlexOperations.cs:117-119). Back in ValidateFlexTokenAsync, `result.Error is IbkrFlexError` fails for IbkrApiError, so control falls to the terminal throw of IbkrConfigurationException(..., "FlexToken") at IbkrClient.cs:174-176. IbkrConfigurationException is documented as "the consumer needs to fix credentials or options before any retry can succeed" (src/IbkrConduit/Errors/IbkrTransientException.cs:6-10 draws exactly this distinction; IbkrConfigurationException.cs:5-8). Crucially, the session-init leg of the SAME method (IbkrClient.cs:113 -> SessionManager.EnsureInitializedAsync) classifies the identical fault class the other way: SessionManager.WrapCredentialException maps a generic HttpRequestException and TaskCanceledException timeout to IbkrTransientException "retry expected" (src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:670-678), reserving IbkrConfigurationException for 401/403 and crypto/config faults (647-668). So within one ValidateConnectionAsync call, a network blip during LST acquisition surfaces as transient, while the same blip 200ms later during the flex probe surfaces as a permanent config error. Counter-evidence found: this is pinned by unit test ValidateConnectionAsync_TransportError_ThrowsConfigurationException (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Client/IbkrClientTests.cs:262-281, IbkrApiError "Connection refused" -> IbkrConfigurationException with CredentialHint "FlexToken") — so it is intended-as-implemented, but the pin encodes the taxonomy contradiction (the thrown message itself says "check network connectivity" while the type and CredentialHint say fix FlexToken). Branch (b): with IbkrClientOptions.ThrowOnApiError=true (public option, src/IbkrConduit/Session/IbkrClientOptions.cs:104-108), FlexOperations.ExecuteQueryAsync applies WithThrowSetting (FlexOperations.cs:119, 236-237), which calls Result.EnsureSuccess -> throws IbkrApiException wrapping the IbkrFlexError (src/IbkrConduit/Errors/Result.cs:55-57). ValidateFlexTokenAsync awaits Flex.ExecuteQueryAsync with no try/catch (IbkrClient.cs:140), so the exception propagates before the 1015/1012/1013 -> IbkrConfigurationException mapping (IbkrClient.cs:147-168) and before the tolerant "token valid but query failed" log-and-return path (IbkrClient.cs:170-171) can run — both are dead code under that option. 1015/1012/1013 as non-retryable token errors is pinned locally (src/IbkrConduit/Flex/FlexErrorCodes.cs:32-35), so no live-wire dependency. Reachability: direct public facade call; no internal callers alter the path; git log shows the flex-validation code predates and was untouched by the VCR-01..11 fix stream (last touched 0b8d2ea), and the RTOS findings doc does not cover it — not a re-report. Severity re-judged: no money/order/fill corruption, no auth-integrity failure, no wedge of a running pipeline — it is a startup-time error-classification gap that misdirects automated retry-vs-halt handling and hides the transient/permanent distinction: medium per the rubric (robustness/observability gap that hides a liveness condition).
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer (e.g., an RTOS-style startup orchestrator) calls client.ValidateConnectionAsync() at boot and branches on the library's documented taxonomy — retry-with-backoff on IbkrTransientException, halt-and-page on IbkrConfigurationException. (a) Session init succeeds, but the Flex Web Service probe hits a DNS blip/connection refused/5xx: FlexClient maps the HttpRequestException to IbkrApiError (FlexClient.cs:93-100), FlexOperations returns it without retry (FlexOperations.cs:151-156), and IbkrClient.ValidateFlexTokenAsync's non-IbkrFlexError fallthrough throws IbkrConfigurationException("...check network connectivity", CredentialHint:"FlexToken") (IbkrClient.cs:174-176) — the permanent fix-your-config type — so the orchestrator halts and pages a human for a fault that the session-init leg of the same method would have classified IbkrTransientException (SessionManager.cs:670-678) and the orchestrator would have retried through. (b) Conversely, with ThrowOnApiError=true, a genuinely invalid/expired flex token (Flex error 1015/1012) makes FlexOperations.ExecuteQueryAsync throw a generic IbkrApiException from WithThrowSetting (FlexOperations.cs:119,236-237) before IbkrClient.cs:147-168 can map it to IbkrConfigurationException — the documented config-error handler (and the tolerant non-token-error path at IbkrClient.cs:170-171) never fires, so the actionable "regenerate your FlexToken" classification is lost and the startup validation contract silently changes shape based on an unrelated option.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** In ValidateFlexTokenAsync, map non-IbkrFlexError transport failures to IbkrTransientException; call the internal non-throwing flex path (bypass WithThrowSetting) so the 1012/1013/1015 mapping runs regardless of ThrowOnApiError.
- **Suggested regression test:** ValidateConnection_FlexTransportFailure_ThrowsIbkrTransientException + ValidateConnection_ThrowOnApiError_InvalidFlexToken_ThrowsIbkrConfigurationException — WireMock flex endpoint down / returning code 1015 with ThrowOnApiError=true.

### ORD-2 — medium — OrderOperations.GetLiveOrdersAsync (VCR-05 auto force-clear follow-up) + EndpointRateLimitingHandler

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Client/OrderOperations.cs:234` · lens: orders & money path
- **Claim:** The §10.6 force=true follow-up after a filtered live-orders call is awaited inline BEFORE the already-computed result is returned to the caller (result built at OrderOperations.cs:223-224, follow-up awaited at 232-235, return at 237), and the follow-up targets the same '/iserver/account/orders' endpoint whose token-bucket limiter is 1 request per 5 seconds (RateLimitingAndResilienceRegistration.cs:55). The filtered call consumes the only token, so the follow-up queues in EndpointRateLimitingHandler.AcquireAsync (EndpointRateLimitingHandler.cs:71) for up to ~5s — every filtered GetLiveOrdersAsync call inline-blocks ~5 extra seconds and burns the next token.
- **Consequence:** The consumer's order-reconciliation path — the exact loop IbkrAmbiguousOrderError instructs consumers to run after a gated 401 ('Reconcile via live-order/trade queries') — gains a guaranteed ~5s stall per filtered call, and a poll loop's effective period doubles (each cycle needs 2 tokens from a 1-per-5s budget), delaying detection of lost/duplicate order state exactly when it is time-critical. It also extends the §10.6 sor-detail-suppression window to 5+ seconds after every filtered call.
- **Evidence:** OrderOperations.cs:219-237 (result computed, then `await IssueForceClearFollowUpAsync` before return), OrderOperations.cs:246-257 (follow-up calls _orderApi.GetLiveOrdersAsync(null, true)); ConsumerPipelineRegistration.cs:49,115-119 (IIbkrOrderApi goes through EndpointRateLimitingHandler); EndpointRateLimitingHandler.cs:63-71,124-141 (Contains match on '/iserver/account/orders', blocking AcquireAsync); RateLimitingAndResilienceRegistration.cs:55 (CreateEndpointLimiter(1, 5s, 50)); the DI-stack integration test GetLiveOrders_Filtered_IssuesExactlyOneForceFollowUp (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Integration/Orders/OrderTests.cs:321-364) exercises this exact double-call path through the real limiter.
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced end-to-end from the public surface at HEAD (18c6a23). (1) Public entry: IIbkrClient.Orders (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IIbkrClient.cs:23, IbkrClient.cs:79) resolves to OrderOperations, registered as the IOrderOperations singleton (src/IbkrConduit/Http/ConsumerPipelineRegistration.cs:60). (2) GetLiveOrdersAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/OrderOperations.cs:209-238): the filtered result is fully computed at lines 223-224, then — when `filtered && force != true` (line 232) — the §10.6 follow-up is AWAITED INLINE at line 234 before the return at line 237. IssueForceClearFollowUpAsync (lines 246-257) calls `_orderApi.GetLiveOrdersAsync(null, true, cancellationToken)` (line 251), i.e. the same Refit client through the same pipeline. (3) That pipeline includes EndpointRateLimitingHandler (ConsumerPipelineRegistration.cs:115-119); FindLimiter does a case-insensitive Contains match (src/IbkrConduit/Http/EndpointRateLimitingHandler.cs:132-138), and both the filtered GET and the follow-up GET hit path `/v1/api/iserver/account/orders...` (src/IbkrConduit/Orders/IIbkrOrderApi.cs:38), matching the `/iserver/account/orders` limiter. (4) That limiter is TokenLimit=1, TokensPerPeriod=1, ReplenishmentPeriod=5s, QueueLimit=50 (src/IbkrConduit/Http/RateLimitingAndResilienceRegistration.cs:55,68-78). The filtered call consumes the sole token; the follow-up's AcquireAsync (EndpointRateLimitingHandler.cs:71) queues until the next auto-replenishment tick — up to ~5s — and the caller's await blocks on it. The follow-up then burns the replenished token, so the next orders-endpoint call queues another full period: a filtered poll loop needs 2 tokens/cycle from a 1-per-5s budget (effective floor ~10s/cycle). No counter-evidence found: the follow-up does not bypass or use a separate limiter; nothing releases the result early. Two aggravators the sweep did not fully state: (a) the merged VCR-05 spec explicitly specified the follow-up as "fire-and-forget through the same rate-limited pipeline" (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-07-vcr-05-live-orders-priming.md:14) and rejected re-poll precisely because it "adds latency/rate cost to every call" (line 19) — the inline await is an implementation deviation from the merged spec, confirming this is a residual VCR-05 gap, not a recorded trade-off (design doc §10.6, docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md:749, is silent on await-vs-detach); (b) if the caller's CancellationToken fires during the queued wait, OperationCanceledException deliberately escapes the catch filter (OrderOperations.cs:253) and propagates out of GetLiveOrdersAsync, discarding the already-successfully-fetched result — a consumer with a per-call timeout under ~5s can fail every filtered call despite the data having arrived. The DI-stack test GetLiveOrders_Filtered_IssuesExactlyOneForceFollowUp (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Integration/Orders/OrderTests.cs:322-364) runs both calls through the real limiters (TestHarness.cs:107-120 builds the unmodified AddIbkrClient pipeline; OrderTests.cs:18-21 applies no limiter override), pinning only call-count, not latency — nothing pins or prevents the stall. Reachability of order placement is NOT affected (POST path `/iserver/account/{acct}/orders` does not Contains-match `/iserver/account/orders`). Severity: bounded ~5s delay + doubled poll period + timeout-induced result loss on the order-reconciliation read path — a robustness gap that delays detection of lost/duplicate order state (the IbkrAmbiguousOrderError obligation at src/IbkrConduit/Errors/IbkrError.cs:93-95 sends consumers to GetLiveOrdersAsync), but it does not itself lose money, corrupt data, or wedge the pipeline; the reconciliation loop only pays it when it passes filters. Medium.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer calls IIbkrClient.Orders.GetLiveOrdersAsync(filters: [...]) without force:true — e.g. polling cancelled/filled status while reconciling an IbkrAmbiguousOrderError after a gated 401. The filtered GET consumes the single token of the /iserver/account/orders limiter (1 per 5s), the result is computed, and then OrderOperations.cs:234 inline-awaits the §10.6 force=true follow-up, which queues in EndpointRateLimitingHandler.AcquireAsync for up to ~5s before the already-available result is returned — despite the merged VCR-05 spec specifying the follow-up as fire-and-forget. The follow-up also burns the next token, so a filtered poll loop's effective period floors at ~10s (2 tokens/cycle from a 1-per-5s budget), delaying detection of lost/duplicate order state exactly when it is time-critical; and if the caller's CancellationToken (e.g. a 3s per-call timeout) fires during the queued wait, OperationCanceledException propagates out of GetLiveOrdersAsync and the successfully fetched snapshot is discarded, so every filtered call can fail under a sub-5s timeout even though IBKR answered.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Return the caller's filtered result immediately and run the force-clear follow-up as a tracked fire-and-forget task (logged on failure, awaited/cancelled on dispose), or let library-initiated follow-ups bypass/deprioritize the endpoint limiter; keep the exactly-once semantics.
- **Suggested regression test:** GetLiveOrdersAsync_Filtered_ReturnsBeforeFollowUpCompletes — DI-stack WireMock test with a delayed force=true stub asserting the filtered call's Result is returned in well under the endpoint-limiter replenishment period while exactly one follow-up still reaches the server.

### ORD-3 — medium — OrderOperations question/reply flow (pending-confirmation invalidation window)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Client/OrderOperations.cs:99` · lens: orders & money path
- **Claim:** The captured spec pins that a pending order confirmation is invalidated by any subsequent order submission and the reply then 503s (docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:4559 'The reply must be confirmed before sending any further orders. Otherwise, the order will be invalidated and attempts to confirm invalid replies will result in a timeout (503)'; also :4612). The per-account semaphore that claims to 'prevent concurrent order submissions' (OrderOperations.cs:18) is released when PlaceOrderAsync returns the OrderConfirmationRequired (finally at :97-100), so a second PlaceOrderAsync can run inside the question→ReplyAsync window; ReplyAsync takes no lock at all (:318-367). Neither IOrderOperations.PlaceOrderAsync, ReplyAsync, nor OrderConfirmationRequired documents the reply-before-any-further-orders obligation, and the resulting 503 is surfaced as a generic IbkrApiError (ResultFactory.ParseError) that reads as a transient server error.
- **Consequence:** In a multi-strategy consumer, order A's pending confirmation is invalidated by order B's submission; order A silently dies at the broker and A's ReplyAsync yields IbkrApiError(503) that a consumer's transient-error policy will retry fruitlessly — the order loss is only discoverable by out-of-band reconciliation. A library that owns IBKR quirks neither guards the window nor states the consumer obligation.
- **Evidence:** docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:4559 and :4612 (invalidation + 503 pinned); OrderOperations.cs:69-70,97-100 (lock scope ends at method return), :318-367 (ReplyAsync lock-free); src/IbkrConduit/Client/IOrderOperations.cs:12-17,100-105 and src/IbkrConduit/Orders/OrderConfirmationRequired.cs:6-9 (no immediacy obligation documented); OrderOperationsAmbiguityTests.cs:140-150 pins the 503 as plain IbkrApiError.
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Trigger pinned by the captured spec: docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:4559 ('The reply must be confirmed before sending any further orders. Otherwise, the order will be invalidated and attempts to confirm invalid replies will result in a timeout (503)') and :4612 ('Orders must be replied to immediately... Submitting other orders or other requests will cancel the order and attempts to acknowledge the reply will result in a 503 error'). Code side traced end-to-end from the public surface (IIbkrClient.cs:23 Orders -> IOrderOperations): OrderOperations.cs:69-70 acquires the per-account SemaphoreSlim, the finally at :97-100 releases it when PlaceOrderAsync returns — including when it returns OrderConfirmationRequired (ClassifyOrderResponses/:417-469); ModifyOrderAsync has the same scope (:283-314); ReplyAsync (:318-367) takes no lock. So a second PlaceOrderAsync on the same account (or any other request, per spec :4612) can run inside order A's question->reply window and invalidate A. No documentation of the obligation anywhere on the public surface: IOrderOperations.cs:12-17 ('caller must handle via ReplyAsync'), :100-105 (chained confirmations only), OrderConfirmationRequired.cs:6-12 ('Caller must decide'), README.md:144-166 (example with no warning). The resulting 503 has no special classification — grep of src for 503/ServiceUnavailable is empty — and OrderOperationsAmbiguityTests.cs:141-149 pins reply-503 as a plain IbkrApiError(StatusCode=503). The library's own design doc actively misleads here: docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md:967-973 (§13.4) says 503s are 'common and expected... retry', while :591 acknowledges 'Reply IDs are stateful server-side and expire quickly. Do not delay' and :633 says the per-account semaphore exists 'due to the stateful question/reply flow' — a scope the implementation does not actually deliver (lock ends before the reply leg). Counter-evidence weighed: IbkrClientOptions.SuppressMessageIds (src/IbkrConduit/Session/IbkrClientOptions.cs:26-28, README.md:305, design :591 'primary defense') can close the window by pre-suppressing questions, but it is opt-in and incomplete (docs/ibkr-suppressible-message-ids.md covers only suppressible IDs), and the caller-handled OrderConfirmationRequired flow remains a first-class documented path — mitigation, not refutation. Verdict is CONFIRMED rather than PLAUSIBLE because the invalidation+503 behavior is pinned by docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md itself (:4559, :4612); note the captured spec is transcribed IBKR documentation, not a wire recording, so the exact invalidation scope (per-account vs per-session; whether non-order requests also invalidate) is documented-but-not-recorded. Severity re-judged as medium, not high: the loss is not fully silent — the consumer holds OrderConfirmationRequired (knows A is not yet live) and ReplyAsync does surface an error — but the error is misclassified as a transient-looking generic 503 that the library's own design guidance says to retry, hiding a permanent order invalidation behind retry churn: an observability/robustness gap that hides order loss, the rubric's medium.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A multi-strategy consumer (e.g., RTOS running two strategies against one account) calls PlaceOrderAsync for order A; IBKR returns a confirmation question, so PlaceOrderAsync returns OrderConfirmationRequired and releases the per-account semaphore (OrderOperations.cs:97-100). Before the consumer's ReplyAsync for A executes, strategy B's PlaceOrderAsync acquires the now-free semaphore and submits order B — which, per the captured spec (docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:4559, :4612), invalidates A's pending confirmation and cancels order A at the broker. A's ReplyAsync (lock-free, :318-367) then gets a 503 that surfaces as a generic IbkrApiError(ServiceUnavailable) (pinned by OrderOperationsAmbiguityTests.cs:141-149), which the design doc §13.4 tells consumers to treat as a normal transient condition and retry — so the consumer's retry policy loops fruitlessly against a permanently-dead replyId while the intended order A never reaches the market; the loss is only discoverable by out-of-band reconciliation against live orders. Nothing on the public surface (IOrderOperations.cs:12-17, :100-105; OrderConfirmationRequired.cs:6-12; README) states the reply-before-any-further-orders obligation, and the semaphore the class header claims serializes the 'stateful question/reply flow' (design doc :633) does not span the reply window.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Contract-gap design pass: either document the reply-immediately obligation on PlaceOrderAsync/ReplyAsync/OrderConfirmationRequired (and classify a reply 503 as a typed invalidated-confirmation refusal rather than a generic 503), or offer an option that holds the per-account lock across the question/reply round (auto-reply or scoped confirmation handle).
- **Suggested regression test:** ReplyAsync_503_ClassifiesAsInvalidatedConfirmation — stub reply 503 and assert a typed refusal (not a retryable-looking generic IbkrApiError), plus doc assertions per the chosen design.

### ORD-4 — medium — OrderRequest / OrderWireModel (public order-ticket surface)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Orders/IIbkrOrderApiModels.cs:27` · lens: orders & money path
- **Claim:** OrderRequest.OrderType documents 'TRAIL' and 'TRAILLMT' as supported values (IIbkrOrderApiModels.cs:23-27, reaffirmed by VCR-11 #246), but the captured spec makes trailingAmt and trailingType REQUIRED for TRAIL/TRAILLMT orders (docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:4516-4517 'You must specify both trailingType and trailingAmt ... Required for TRAIL and TRAILLMT orders'), and neither OrderRequest nor OrderWireModel (IIbkrOrderApiModels.cs:112-141) has any way to express them — ToWireModel (OrderOperations.cs:505-513) maps only conid/side/quantity/orderType/price/auxPrice/tif/manualIndicator/cOID/parentId/isSingleGroup/outsideRTH.
- **Consequence:** A consumer following the library's own documented enum cannot place a valid trailing-stop order: the submission will be refused by IBKR (or, if IBKR tolerates the omission, submitted with broker-defaulted trailing parameters the consumer never chose — a wrong-order risk). Trailing stops are a common risk-management order type for automated systems like RTOS.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Orders/IIbkrOrderApiModels.cs:23-27 (OrderType doc), :33-39 (Price/AuxPrice only), :112-141 (wire model field set); src/IbkrConduit/Client/OrderOperations.cs:505-513 (ToWireModel); docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:4516-4517 (required-ness pinned in the captured spec).
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Independent trace at HEAD (18c6a23): (1) OrderRequest's XML docs list "TRAIL" and "TRAILLMT" as supported OrderType values (src/IbkrConduit/Orders/IIbkrOrderApiModels.cs:24), and AuxPrice's doc even references TRAILLMT (IIbkrOrderApiModels.cs:36). (2) The full OrderRequest field set (IIbkrOrderApiModels.cs:12-74) is Conid, Side, Quantity, OrderType, Price, AuxPrice, Tif, ManualIndicator, CustomerOrderId, ParentId, IsSingleGroup, OutsideRth — no trailingAmt or trailingType, and no extension/passthrough mechanism (JsonExtensionData appears only on response DTOs, never on the outbound wire model). (3) OrderWireModel (IIbkrOrderApiModels.cs:112-141) serializes exactly conid/side/quantity/orderType/price/auxPrice/tif/manualIndicator plus cOID/parentId/isSingleGroup/outsideRTH — no trailing fields. (4) ToWireModel (src/IbkrConduit/Client/OrderOperations.cs:505-513) maps only those fields, and every submission path — PlaceOrderAsync (OrderOperations.cs:74), bulk placement (:120), ModifyOrderAsync (:288), and what-if (:379) — goes through it; there is no raw-payload escape hatch on the public surface. (5) The captured spec pins the requirement: docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:4516-4517 — "Required for TRAIL and TRAILLMT orders" / "You must specify both trailingType and trailingAmt for TRAIL and TRAILLMT orders". (6) The library's own contract claims trailing-stop support: docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md:109 (order-type scope includes "Trailing") and :675 ("Trailing Stop | DAY, GTC | Dynamic stop management"). (7) Not fixed by the VCR stream: VCR-11 (#246) was scoped to correcting the OrderType XML-doc enum only (docs/backlog.md:153) — it reaffirmed TRAIL/TRAILLMT in the docs without adding the fields; VCR-12 covers only ExtOperator; grep for "trailing" across src/ and tests/ finds no handling and no pinning test. Reachability is direct from IIbkrClient's order operations. The only unpinned aspect is IBKR's exact behavior on receiving a TRAIL order missing the fields (reject vs. broker-defaulted trailing params) — but the required-ness itself is pinned by the captured spec, so the failure (a documented order type that cannot be validly constructed through the public surface) is concrete either way.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer (e.g. RTOS) reads OrderRequest.OrderType's documented enum (IIbkrOrderApiModels.cs:24, reaffirmed by VCR-11 #246) and the design doc's claimed trailing-stop support (ibkr_conduit_design.md:675), builds an OrderRequest with OrderType = "TRAIL" plus Quantity/Side/AuxPrice, and calls PlaceOrderAsync. ToWireModel (OrderOperations.cs:505-513) emits a payload with no trailingAmt/trailingType — fields the captured spec marks REQUIRED for TRAIL/TRAILLMT (ibkr-web-api-spec.md:4516-4517) and which neither OrderRequest nor OrderWireModel can express. The submission is refused by IBKR (surfaced as a Result failure), so an automated system's risk-management trailing stop is never placed — or, in the unpinned tolerant case, IBKR accepts the order with broker-defaulted trailing parameters the consumer never chose. Severity medium rather than high/critical: the primary failure mode is a visible placement-time reject of a documented-but-unimplementable order type (a capability/contract gap that can leave positions unprotected), not a silent corruption of money numerics or a session/liveness failure.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** 📦 additive surface story: add TrailingAmt (decimal?) and TrailingType (string?) to OrderRequest and OrderWireModel with WhenWritingNull omission, plus ValidateOrderGroup-style fail-fast when OrderType is TRAIL/TRAILLMT and they are absent; alternatively remove TRAIL/TRAILLMT from the documented enum until supported.
- **Suggested regression test:** PlaceOrderAsync_TrailLimitOrder_SendsTrailingAmtAndType — assert the serialized wire payload carries trailingAmt/trailingType for a TRAILLMT request and that they are omitted for other types.

### PRB-4.2 — medium — HealthStatusCollector (active-probe evidence flow)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Health/HealthStatusCollector.cs:102` · lens: gap probe: IbkrClient facade layer: active-probe health path bypasses the VCR-07 competing-evidence flow, and facade DisposeAsync vs container ownership in the plain AddIbkrClient path
- **Claim:** CollectActiveSessionHealthAsync returns the server-reported authenticated/competing/fail verdict only to the immediate caller and never writes it into SessionHealthState — it is the sole server-evidence path that bypasses the ADR-0004 health-evidence flow. Every other server response feeds the shared state (SessionManager.cs:216, 396, 533 for ssodh/init; TickleTimer.cs:167, 257, 273 for tickle; StreamingOperations.cs:62 MarkCompeting for sts frames), but a probe that observes competing=true never calls MarkCompeting and a probe that observes authenticated=false never calls SetFailed. Additionally, because the probe traverses LastSuccessfulCallHandler (SessionServiceRegistration.cs:81-83), a 200 response with authenticated=false still refreshes LastSuccessfulCall (LastSuccessfulCallHandler.cs:63-68), keeping the passive staleness check green.
- **Consequence:** Divergent health verdicts across the surface: a consumer probing actively sees Degraded/Unhealthy while every passive reader (IbkrHealthCheck with default ActiveProbe=false, plus direct GetHealthStatusAsync() callers like RTOS session-loss logic) keeps reporting Healthy until the next tickle cycle re-derives the evidence; probe-observed competing evidence is not sticky as ADR-0004 rule 4 requires for the equivalent sts evidence. Residual gap from VCR-07 (#244) — its scope covered ssodh/tickle/sts but not the active-probe path.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Health/HealthStatusCollector.cs:102-123 (no _sessionHealthState write; contrast CollectPassiveSessionHealth :91-100 which only reads); write sites enumerated by grep: src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:216,396,533; src/IbkrConduit/Session/TickleTimer.cs:167,257,273; src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:62; sticky-competing contract: src/IbkrConduit/Health/SessionHealthState.cs:68-86 and docs/adr/0004-competing-session-truth-and-health-evidence.md:13-16; liveness side-effect: src/IbkrConduit/Health/LastSuccessfulCallHandler.cs:63-68 with handler wiring at src/IbkrConduit/Http/SessionServiceRegistration.cs:81-83
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced end-to-end at HEAD 18c6a23. (1) Public reachability: IIbkrClient.GetHealthStatusAsync(activeProbe) at src/IbkrConduit/Client/IbkrClient.cs:106-108 delegates to HealthStatusCollector.GetHealthStatusAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Health/HealthStatusCollector.cs:60-68), and IbkrHealthCheck passes IbkrHealthCheckOptions.ActiveProbe (src/IbkrConduit.HealthChecks/IbkrHealthCheck.cs:34, default false at IbkrHealthCheckOptions.cs:15). (2) The defect: CollectActiveSessionHealthAsync (HealthStatusCollector.cs:102-123) calls _sessionApi.GetAuthStatusAsync and returns the server's authenticated/connected/competing/fail verdict directly — it never calls _sessionHealthState.Update, MarkCompeting, or SetFailed. A full grep of src/ confirms the only SessionHealthState write sites are TickleTimer.cs:167 (Update from tickle authStatus), :257 and :273 (SetFailed on 401/sustained transport failure), SessionManager.cs:216/396/533 (Update from ssodh responses), and StreamingOperations.cs:62 (MarkCompeting from sts frames) — the active probe is the sole server-evidence path that bypasses the shared state. (3) Liveness side-effect: the probe uses the DI-registered IIbkrSessionApi (ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:145-147) whose pipeline includes LastSuccessfulCallHandler (SessionServiceRegistration.cs:81-83), which records success on any 2xx (LastSuccessfulCallHandler.cs:63-68) — so a 200 auth/status with authenticated=false refreshes LastSuccessfulCall, keeping the passive staleness tripwire (HealthStatusCollector.cs:200-203) green while the auth verdict it carried is discarded. (4) Sticky-competing inconsistency: MarkCompeting's contract (SessionHealthState.cs:68-86) makes server-originated competing evidence sticky until positively cleared per ADR-0004 (docs/adr/0004-competing-session-truth-and-health-evidence.md:15, rule 4); a probe observing competing=true is evidence of the same quality and is dropped. (5) Residual-gap status: VCR-07's spec test scope (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-07-vcr-07-competing-session-truth.md:33) covers ssodh/tickle/sts paths only; no test in HealthStatusCollectorTests.cs pins active-probe state feeding either way. Counter-evidence that bounds (but does not refute) the consequence: the tickle loop re-derives state within TickleIntervalSeconds (default 60s healthy, 5s after failure — TickleTimer.cs:83-84,178) and still triggers reauth on an unauthenticated tickle (TickleTimer.cs:199-208), so the passive/active divergence window is at most one tickle interval and recovery is not blocked; and ADR-0004 rule 2 literally forbids hardcoded literals rather than mandating that every server response feed state, so this is an evidence-discipline gap rather than a black-letter ADR violation. The trigger (200 auth/status with authenticated=false or competing=true) is the same wire family ADR-0004's context pins for ssodh/tickle, so the mechanism does not depend on unpinned wire behavior — any probe response differing from cached state produces the divergence.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A competing session takes over (or the brokerage bridge dies) between two tickle cycles: a consumer that calls IIbkrClient.GetHealthStatusAsync(activeProbe: true) — e.g. a Kubernetes readiness probe via IbkrHealthCheck with ActiveProbe=true — receives Degraded (competing=true) or Unhealthy (authenticated=false) from the live auth/status call, but because CollectActiveSessionHealthAsync (HealthStatusCollector.cs:102-123) never writes the verdict into SessionHealthState, every passive reader in the same process (default-configured IbkrHealthCheck, RTOS session-loss logic calling GetHealthStatusAsync() with no probe) keeps reporting Healthy from the stale snapshot for up to one tickle interval (default 60s); worse, the probe's 200 response traverses LastSuccessfulCallHandler and refreshes LastSuccessfulCall even when authenticated=false, so the passive staleness check stays green on the strength of a response whose auth verdict was discarded, and probe-observed competing evidence is not made sticky as the equivalent sts evidence is (SessionHealthState.MarkCompeting per ADR-0004 rule 4). The window is bounded — the next tickle (60s healthy / 5s failure cadence) re-derives the truth and triggers reauth — so this hides, rather than causes, a session-integrity failure: a medium observability gap left open by VCR-07, whose scope covered ssodh/tickle/sts but not the active-probe path.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** In CollectActiveSessionHealthAsync, feed the AuthStatusResponse into SessionHealthState per ADR-0004: full Update() with the server-reported fields (or at minimum MarkCompeting on competing=true and SetFailed on authenticated=false), so probe evidence is as durable as tickle/sts evidence.
- **Suggested regression test:** GetHealthStatusAsync_ActiveProbeObservesCompeting_PassiveSnapshotReportsCompeting — probe returns competing=true, then a subsequent activeProbe:false call must report Session.Competing=true (sticky evidence) without any tickle in between.

### RST-2 — medium — IIbkrPortfolioApi.GetSubAccountsPagedAsync (subaccounts2 DTO shape)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Portfolio/IIbkrPortfolioApi.cs:125` · lens: REST breadth & periphery
- **Claim:** GetSubAccountsPagedAsync declares the /portfolio/subaccounts2 response as a bare List<SubAccount>, but the repo's own captured spec pins it as an object wrapper {metadata:{total,pageSize,pageNum}, subaccounts:[...]} (docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:5257-5270). Deserializing an object into List<T> makes Refit set Content=null on a 2xx, which ResultFactory converts to Result.Failure(IbkrApiError "Response body could not be deserialized") (src/IbkrConduit/Errors/ResultFactory.cs:52-54). The integration fixture (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Integration/Fixtures/Portfolio/GET-subaccounts2.json) is synthetic ("Test User") and pins a bare array matching the DTO rather than the spec, so tests false-green. Pagination metadata (total/pageSize/pageNum) — the only loop-termination signal — is also unexposed.
- **Consequence:** For FA/IBroker structures with >100 sub-accounts (the sole population this endpoint exists for), every call via IIbkrClient.Portfolio.GetSubAccountsPagedAsync fails with a misleading deserialization error, and there is no way to know the page count; the paged enumeration feature is unusable against the pinned wire shape.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Portfolio/IIbkrPortfolioApi.cs:124-126 (IApiResponse<List<SubAccount>>); src/IbkrConduit/Client/PortfolioOperations.cs:258-268 (public path); docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:5257-5270 (object wrapper with metadata); tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Integration/Fixtures/Portfolio/GET-subaccounts2.json (fabricated bare-array body); failure mechanism src/IbkrConduit/Errors/ResultFactory.cs:47-54.
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced end-to-end from the public surface. (1) Reachability: IIbkrClient.Portfolio (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IIbkrClient.cs:13) -> IPortfolioOperations.GetSubAccountsPagedAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IPortfolioOperations.cs:147) -> PortfolioOperations.GetSubAccountsPagedAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/PortfolioOperations.cs:258-268) -> _api.GetSubAccountsPagedAsync. (2) DTO shape: src/IbkrConduit/Portfolio/IIbkrPortfolioApi.cs:124-126 declares GET /v1/api/portfolio/subaccounts2 as Task<IApiResponse<List<SubAccount>>>. (3) Pinned wire shape: docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:5257-5270 pins the subaccounts2 response as an object wrapper {metadata:{total,pageSize,pageNum}, subaccounts:array<PortfolioAccount>} — explicitly different from the non-paged /portfolio/subaccounts, which the same spec pins as a bare array (line 5237) and for which the List<SubAccount> DTO at IIbkrPortfolioApi.cs:118-119 is correct. The paged DTO looks copy-pasted from the non-paged one. (4) Failure mechanism: System.Text.Json throws JsonException deserializing a JSON object into List<T>; Refit 11 with an IApiResponse<T> return type captures that as an ApiException in response.Error with Content=null while IsSuccessStatusCode stays true. In ResultFactory.FromResponse (src/IbkrConduit/Errors/ResultFactory.cs:22-58): ThrowOnSendFailure is a no-op (only rethrows ApiRequestException, src/IbkrConduit/Errors/RefitResponseExtensions.cs:24-30); DetectHiddenError (ResultFactory.cs:127-166) returns null for the wrapper (no 'error'/'success' fields, doesn't start with '['); then IsSuccessStatusCode==true with Content==null hits ResultFactory.cs:52-54 -> Result.Failure(IbkrApiError "Response body could not be deserialized") (or an IbkrException if ThrowOnApiError, PortfolioOperations.cs:267). (5) The VCR-10 schema-validation net (src/IbkrConduit/Http/ResponseSchemaValidationHandler.cs:97-168, EnumerateValidationTargets:176-210) does not rescue this: for a collection DTO with an object root it validates the wrapper itself against SubAccount fields, producing extra={metadata,subaccounts}/missing=all required fields — a log warning by default, an IbkrSchemaViolationException in strict mode; either way the call fails. (6) Tests false-green: tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Integration/Fixtures/Portfolio/GET-subaccounts2.json:6-39 stubs a bare array with synthetic data ("U1234567"/"Test User") matching the DTO, not the spec's wrapper; integration tests GetSubAccountsPaged_ReturnsAllFields and its 401-recovery test (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Integration/Portfolio/PortfolioTests.cs:724-776) pass against that fixture, and the unit test is a pure delegation fake (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Portfolio/PortfolioOperationsTests.cs:175-183). (7) No recording exists (recordings/ contains only order-related dirs: bracket, bulk, cleanup, oca, orderref, orders, priming), and no VCR story or backlog entry covers subaccounts (grep of docs/backlog.md and docs/findings/ found nothing) — this is not a residual of a merged fix; it was never reported. (8) Pagination metadata (total/pageSize/pageNum) is indeed unexposed anywhere on the public surface, so even the loop-termination signal is absent. Verdict boundary: the trigger IS pinned by the captured spec (5257-5270), so per the verdict rules this is CONFIRMED; the only caveat is that the pin is the captured documentation, not a recording, and the repo's own contract-design rule notes documented != verified — but by this review's stated rule (PLAUSIBLE only when behavior is NOT pinned by the captured spec or recordings) the pin stands. Severity re-judged as medium, not high: the failure is loud (a Result.Failure/exception with a misleading message, plus a schema-mismatch warning log), not silently wrong data; no money numerics, orders, fills, or auth/session integrity are involved; the affected population is FA/IBroker structures with >100 sub-accounts, and the primary known consumer (RTOS) has no evident dependency on it. It is an unusable feature plus an observability gap (misleading error, false-green fixture), which fits medium.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer with an FA/IBroker tiered account structure of more than 100 sub-accounts (the population IBKR's spec directs to /portfolio/subaccounts2) calls IIbkrClient.Portfolio.GetSubAccountsPagedAsync(page). Per the repo's own captured spec (docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:5257-5270) the server returns {"metadata":{"total":...,"pageSize":...,"pageNum":...},"subaccounts":[...]}, but the Refit method (src/IbkrConduit/Portfolio/IIbkrPortfolioApi.cs:125) declares IApiResponse<List<SubAccount>>, so System.Text.Json fails to bind the object into a list, Refit sets Content=null on the 2xx, and ResultFactory (src/IbkrConduit/Errors/ResultFactory.cs:52-54) returns Result.Failure(IbkrApiError "Response body could not be deserialized") — or throws IbkrException when ThrowOnApiError is set — on every call, every page. The endpoint is therefore unusable against the pinned wire shape, the error message misdirects diagnosis toward a transient serialization problem, the pagination metadata needed to terminate an enumeration loop is not exposed anywhere on the public surface, and the integration tests false-green because tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Integration/Fixtures/Portfolio/GET-subaccounts2.json fabricates a bare-array body that matches the DTO instead of the spec. Not covered by any merged VCR-01..11 fix (no backlog/findings entry mentions subaccounts).
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Introduce a SubAccountsPage DTO (metadata + subaccounts) matching the spec, return it (📦 surface change), and correct the WireMock fixture to the spec shape; verify with an attended probe if an FA test structure is available.
- **Suggested regression test:** GetSubAccountsPaged_SpecShape_DeserializesMetadataAndAccounts — WireMock returns the spec's {metadata,subaccounts} object and asserts accounts plus pageNum/total round-trip.

### RST-4 — medium — HealthStatusOptions registration (health staleness thresholds)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Http/ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:143` · lens: REST breadth & periphery
- **Claim:** HealthStatusOptions is registered as a hardcoded `new HealthStatusOptions()` with no configuration hook — IbkrClientOptions has no corresponding property and AddIbkrClient exposes no way to set it — even though IbkrHealthCheckOptions' XML doc (src/IbkrConduit.HealthChecks/IbkrHealthCheckOptions.cs:7-9) explicitly tells consumers these thresholds are "configured at AddIbkrClient() registration time". Consequently StalenessTimeout is pinned at 120s (Health/IbkrHealthStatus.cs:105) while ValidateOptions accepts any positive TickleIntervalSeconds (ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:182-188): a consumer who legally sets TickleIntervalSeconds > 120 with no other REST traffic sees lastCall age exceed StalenessTimeout between tickles, so EvaluateOverallStatus (Health/HealthStatusCollector.cs:200-203) reports Unhealthy on a perfectly live session, oscillating each tickle cycle — and the documented knob to fix it does not exist.
