- **Consequence:** ASP.NET health checks (IbkrHealthCheck) flap Healthy→Unhealthy on an idle-but-tickling session for any tickle interval over 120s; orchestrators restart pods or trading is gated on a false-negative liveness signal, with the advertised remediation (configure HealthStatusOptions) unreachable through the public surface.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Http/ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:143 (AddSingleton(new HealthStatusOptions()), no configure delegate); grep shows no other HealthStatusOptions construction/configuration path in src; src/IbkrConduit.HealthChecks/IbkrHealthCheckOptions.cs:7-9 (doc claims AddIbkrClient-time configurability); src/IbkrConduit/Health/HealthStatusCollector.cs:200-203 (staleness→Unhealthy); src/IbkrConduit/Health/IbkrHealthStatus.cs:105 (120s default); src/IbkrConduit/Http/ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:182-188 (interval only checked > 0).
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced end-to-end at HEAD. (1) src/IbkrConduit/Http/ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:143 registers `services.AddSingleton(new HealthStatusOptions());` — a hardcoded default instance, no configure delegate; the collector consumes it at :152. (2) IbkrClientOptions (src/IbkrConduit/Session/IbkrClientOptions.cs:11-170) has no health-threshold property, and ValidateOptions (ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:182-188) accepts any TickleIntervalSeconds > 0 (default 60, IbkrClientOptions.cs:61). (3) The advertised knob is a doc lie: src/IbkrConduit.HealthChecks/IbkrHealthCheckOptions.cs:7-9 says thresholds are "configured at AddIbkrClient() registration time", but AddIbkrClient exposes no such hook and AddIbkrHealthCheck (HealthChecksBuilderExtensions.cs:22-35) only configures ActiveProbe. (4) Mechanism: on a consumer-idle session, tickle is the only lastCall writer — the internal session pipeline deliberately includes LastSuccessfulCallHandler as liveness evidence (SessionServiceRegistration.cs:70-83, SES-4/ADR-0004); LastSuccessfulCallHandler records any 2xx (Health/LastSuccessfulCallHandler.cs:60-71). EvaluateOverallStatus returns Unhealthy when now - lastCall > StalenessTimeout (HealthStatusCollector.cs:200-203), pinned at 120s default (IbkrHealthStatus.cs:105). So TickleIntervalSeconds > 120 makes every health sample taken >120s after the last tickle report Unhealthy, resetting to Healthy after the next tickle — oscillation. Reachable through the public surface: IIbkrClient.GetHealthStatusAsync (Client/IIbkrClient.cs:72 → IbkrClient.cs:106-108) and IbkrHealthCheck maps it to HealthCheckResult.Unhealthy (IbkrHealthCheck.cs:34-41). No live-wire dependency — fully code-side. Counter-evidence hunted: single-client consumers have an UNDOCUMENTED workaround (HealthStatusOptions is public; line 143 is plain AddSingleton, not TryAdd, so a later consumer-registered singleton wins on GetRequiredService), but the manager path has none — TenantBuilder.cs:32-41 builds a private ServiceCollection/provider the consumer never touches. Tests (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Health/HealthStatusCollectorTests.cs:215-223) pin staleness→Unhealthy via constructor-injected options only; nothing pins DI configurability. Nothing in the merged VCR-01..11 stream touched this path. Severity re-judged medium, not high: the trigger requires an explicit non-default config (>120s tickle, itself risky against IBKR's ~1-minute keepalive expectation — default 60s config never trips), the failure is a false-negative liveness flap (observability gap) rather than silently wrong data or session corruption, and a DI-override workaround exists for the single-client path.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer legally sets IbkrClientOptions.TickleIntervalSeconds to a value over 120 (ValidateOptions only requires > 0, ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:182-188) and wires IbkrHealthCheck into ASP.NET health checks. On an otherwise-idle-but-authenticated session, the only writer of LastSuccessfulCallTracker is the tickle loop (SessionServiceRegistration.cs:70-83), so between tickles the lastCall age exceeds the hardcoded 120s StalenessTimeout (IbkrHealthStatus.cs:105) and EvaluateOverallStatus returns Unhealthy (HealthStatusCollector.cs:200-203); the next tickle resets it to Healthy. The health endpoint therefore flaps Healthy→Unhealthy each tickle cycle on a perfectly live session — orchestrators restart pods or trading gates trip on a false-negative liveness signal — and the remediation the XML docs advertise (configuring HealthStatusOptions at AddIbkrClient time, IbkrHealthCheckOptions.cs:7-9) does not exist: AddIbkrClient registers a hardcoded `new HealthStatusOptions()` (ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:143) with no configuration hook, and on the IIbkrClientManager path there is no workaround at all because the per-tenant provider is built from a private ServiceCollection (TenantBuilder.cs:32-41). Single-client consumers can only fix it via an undocumented DI trick (registering their own HealthStatusOptions singleton after AddIbkrClient, which wins by last-registration).
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Expose HealthStatusOptions on IbkrClientOptions (or an AddIbkrClient overload), and either derive the staleness default from TickleIntervalSeconds (e.g. max(2x interval, 120s)) or validate the interval against the staleness window.
- **Suggested regression test:** AddIbkrClient_HealthThresholds_Configurable — set StalenessTimeout via options and assert HealthStatusCollector honors it; plus GetHealthStatus_TickleIntervalAboveDefaultStaleness_NotUnhealthy.

### SES-3 — medium — TokenRefreshHandler (401 recovery — error classification of consumer cancellation)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Session/TokenRefreshHandler.cs:100` · lens: session lifecycle
- **Claim:** The reauth-failure handler `catch (Exception ex)` (TokenRefreshHandler.cs:100) has no OperationCanceledException exclusion, so when the consumer's own token cancels while ReauthenticateAsync is in flight (OCE from SemaphoreSlim.WaitAsync at SessionManager.cs:288 or the deliberate cancellation rethrows at SessionManager.cs:342-345), the non-order path converts the caller's cancellation into a thrown IbkrApiException(IbkrSessionError "Re-authentication failed — credentials may be invalidated") at TokenRefreshHandler.cs:122-129 — potentially with IsCompeting=true pulled from a stale health snapshot (line 118-120). This contradicts the library's own care to keep cancellation distinct from session errors elsewhere (SessionManager.cs:170-172, 342-345; TickleTimer.cs:236-239).
- **Consequence:** A routine request timeout/cancellation that lands during a 401 recovery window surfaces to the consumer as a definitive session-integrity failure instead of OperationCanceledException. RTOS maps IbkrSessionError (and especially IsCompeting=true) to SessionLost and fires its recovery saga (halt/alert/re-login) — a spurious trading halt triggered by the consumer's own timeout, and a broken cancellation contract (callers awaiting with a token never see OCE on this path).
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Session/TokenRefreshHandler.cs:95-133 (catch(Exception) at 100 with no OCE filter; non-order throw of IbkrSessionError at 122-129; competing evidence from snapshot at 118-120); OCE sources inside ReauthenticateAsync: src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:288 (WaitAsync(cancellationToken)), :342-345 (rethrow when cancellationToken.IsCancellationRequested); reachability to consumers: ResultFactory.FromResponse → ThrowOnSendFailure per docs/findings/2026-07-04-rtos-venue-consumer-review.md:121 tracing; no cancellation test exists in tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Session/TokenRefreshHandlerTests.cs (grep shows only TestContext tokens).
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced end-to-end at HEAD (18c6a23). (1) src/IbkrConduit/Session/TokenRefreshHandler.cs:100 is a bare `catch (Exception ex)` with no OperationCanceledException filter; the non-order branch (line 110 gate) throws IbkrApiException(IbkrSessionError(401, "Re-authentication failed — credentials may be invalidated", ..., isCompeting)) at lines 122-129, where isCompeting for an OCE always falls to `_sessionHealthState.GetSnapshot().Competing` (lines 118-120, since an OCE is not IbkrApiException). (2) OCE sources inside ReauthenticateAsync are real and escape: SessionManager.cs:288 `_semaphore.WaitAsync(cancellationToken)` sits before the try block so it propagates directly; the deliberate cancellation rethrow filter at SessionManager.cs:342-345 rethrows OCE when the caller's token is canceled, and the outer catch at SessionManager.cs:423-430 rethrows after resetting state. (3) Consumer reachability: IbkrApiException derives from plain Exception (src/IbkrConduit/Errors/IbkrApiException.cs:8), so HttpClient.HandleFailure does not re-wrap it as cancellation (only HttpRequestException gets that treatment); Refit 11 captures handler-thrown exceptions into IApiResponse.Error as ApiRequestException (documented at src/IbkrConduit/Errors/RefitResponseExtensions.cs:8-14), and ThrowOnSendFailure (RefitResponseExtensions.cs:24-30), called first at ResultFactory.cs:24/66 and PortfolioOperations.cs:161 / FyiOperations.cs:134, rethrows the IbkrApiException verbatim to the consumer — pinned by the existing test TokenRefreshHandlerTests.cs:164 for the genuine-failure case. (4) Counter-evidence hunt: no VCR PR touched this catch (file read in full at HEAD); no cancellation-aware unwrap exists between the handler and the consumer for this exception shape; grep of tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Session/TokenRefreshHandlerTests.cs shows only TestContext.Current.CancellationToken — no test pins OCE-during-reauth. The inconsistency claim is confirmed: SessionManager.cs:170-172 and 342-345 and TickleTimer.cs:227-239 all deliberately keep cancellation distinct from session/credential errors; this catch is the one path that launders it. The trigger requires no unpinned IBKR wire behavior — only a 401 response (the handler's normal activation) plus a caller-token cancellation during the multi-round-trip reauth window (or while queued behind another caller's reauth on the semaphore).
- **Failure scenario (refined):** RTOS issues a non-order request (e.g. GET live orders) with its own per-request timeout token; IBKR returns 401 (routine session expiry or competing login), so TokenRefreshHandler calls SessionManager.ReauthenticateAsync(callerToken) (TokenRefreshHandler.cs:98). The reauth spans multiple round-trips (LST DH refresh + ssodh/init), or the caller is queued on the reauth semaphore behind a concurrent 401'd request (SessionManager.cs:288). The consumer's timeout fires; OCE propagates out of ReauthenticateAsync (WaitAsync at :288 or the deliberate rethrow at :342-345) into the bare catch(Exception) at TokenRefreshHandler.cs:100, whose non-order path converts it into IbkrApiException(IbkrSessionError 401 "Re-authentication failed — credentials may be invalidated") at :122-129, with IsCompeting pulled from the health snapshot (:118-120) — true if any prior tickle/sts recorded a competing verdict. Refit 11 captures the exception and ThrowOnSendFailure (ResultFactory.cs:24) rethrows it to the consumer, who sees a definitive session-integrity error instead of OperationCanceledException — even while a concurrent caller's reauth is actually succeeding. A consumer that maps IbkrSessionError (especially IsCompeting=true) to session-lost recovery fires a spurious halt/alert/re-login saga off its own timeout, and the library's cancellation contract (callers awaiting with a token see OCE) is broken on this path. No test in TokenRefreshHandlerTests.cs covers cancellation during reauth.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Before the general catch, add `catch (OperationCanceledException) when (cancellationToken.IsCancellationRequested)` that disposes the 401 response and rethrows for non-order requests; keep the ADR-0003 ambiguous-outcome marking for order-mutating POSTs (cancellation still leaves the outcome genuinely ambiguous there).
- **Suggested regression test:** SendAsync_ConsumerCancelsDuringReauth_PropagatesOperationCanceled — stub ISessionManager.ReauthenticateAsync to throw OCE for the request's cancelled token after a 401 on a GET; assert OperationCanceledException (not IbkrApiException/IbkrSessionError) propagates.

### STR-4 — medium — IbkrWebSocketClient.SubscribeTopicAsync (registration rollback on failed subscribe send)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:261` · lens: streaming delivery & backpressure
- **Claim:** The subscription entry is committed to _subscriptions/_subscribers (lines 245-254) before the subscribe message is sent (line 261); if SendTextAsync throws (WebSocketException on a socket that is Open at the check but dying at the send, or the concurrent-send InvalidOperationException from the send-path finding), the exception propagates to the consumer and the method never returns the unsubscribe delegate — the registration is orphaned with no rollback and no handle to remove it.
- **Consequence:** The ghost entry is replayed on every reconnect (line 728), re-establishing a wire subscription whose channel nobody reads: after 2048 frames every further frame increments the overflow drop counter forever (misleading ops signal on the topic), and because the ghost keeps its CancelMessage referenced in the refcount check (lines 815-816), a later legitimate unsubscribe of the same topic never sends the wire cancel — IBKR keeps streaming the topic indefinitely. A consumer retry of the failed subscribe doubles the registrations.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:245-254 (registration before send), :259-262 (send may throw after commit; SendTextAsync :771-793 throws WebSocketException when state races past the :774 guard), :723-735 (ghost replayed each reconnect), :795-821 (UnsubscribeSolicitedAsync refcount at :815-816 counts the ghost, suppressing cancelToSend), :243 (orphaned channel evicts via OnFrameDropped once full). Reachable from every solicited facade call (src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:100,113,141,159,174,189).
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced end-to-end at HEAD (18c6a23). (1) Commit-before-send, no rollback: src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:245-254 adds the TopicSubscription to _subscriptions and its writer to _subscribers under _subscriptionLock; :259-262 then awaits SendTextAsync with no try/catch; the unsubscribe delegate is only returned at :264, so any send throw orphans the registration with no removal handle. (2) Concrete in-process triggers: SendTextAsync:774-777 silently no-ops on a non-Open socket, but ws.SendAsync at :785-789 receives the caller's CancellationToken (OperationCanceledException if it fires mid-send) and throws WebSocketException if the socket races Open->Aborted between the :259/:774 checks and the send — neither depends on unpinned IBKR behavior. (The sweep's third trigger, concurrent-send InvalidOperationException vs the heartbeat 'tic' at :542, is weaker: modern .NET ManagedWebSocket serializes sends internally, so I do not rely on it.) (3) Ghost persistence: DisconnectAsync (:489-524) never clears _subscriptions/_subscribers (by design, for replay); only DisposeAsync (:406-417) completes writers. ConnectCoreAsync:486 -> ReplayActiveSubscriptionsAsync (:723-735) re-sends the ghost's SubscribeMessage on every reconnect, re-establishing an unread wire subscription. (4) Perpetual overflow drops: ProcessMessage:667-676 writes every matching-prefix frame to ALL writers including the ghost; the bounded channel (default 2048, src/IbkrConduit/Session/IbkrClientOptions.cs:89) with DropOldest + itemDropped (:237-243) invokes OnFrameDropped (:333-346), incrementing the cause=overflow drop counter on every frame once full — permanently polluting the FIL-1/VCR-02 no-silent-loss signal for that topic. (5) Refcount suppression: UnsubscribeSolicitedAsync:815-816 counts the ghost entry (same CancelMessage — constant 'uor+{}'/'utr'/'upl+{}' for sor/str/spl, per-conid for smd) as stillReferenced, so a later legitimate unsubscribe never sends the wire cancel; IBKR keeps streaming into the ghost indefinitely. (6) Reachability: every solicited facade method awaits SubscribeTopicAsync directly with no catch (src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:100,113,141,159,174,189); a consumer retry of the failed call adds a second registration (Distinct() at :728 dedups only the replayed wire message, not the entries). No test pins rollback-on-failed-subscribe-send (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClientTests.cs covers happy-path send/replay/refcount only; FailSendAfterCount is used only by the heartbeat test at :306-312), and no VCR-01..11 fix touched this path. Severity re-judged as medium, not higher: the consumer observes the exception (nothing silently wrong is delivered to a reader), so this is a robustness/observability defect — but the permanently-incrementing overflow counter and the suppressed wire cancel actively mask real delivery loss on that topic, exactly the rubric's medium ('observability gap that hides one of the above').
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer calls e.g. client.Streaming.OrderUpdatesAsync(ct) while the WebSocket is open; the registration is committed (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:245-254) and then the subscribe send at :261 throws — either the consumer's timeout token fires mid-SendAsync (:785-789) or the socket dies between the Open check (:259/:774) and the send. The exception propagates, the unsubscribe delegate is never returned, and the entry is orphaned in _subscriptions/_subscribers with no rollback. On the next reconnect (and every one after), ReplayActiveSubscriptionsAsync (:728-733) re-sends the ghost's 'sor+{}' subscribe, so IBKR streams order updates into a channel nobody reads; after 2048 frames every further sor frame increments the cause=overflow drop counter forever (:237-243, :333-346), corrupting the drop-observability signal for the topic. If the consumer retries and later legitimately unsubscribes, the ghost's identical CancelMessage 'uor+{}' keeps stillReferenced true (:815-816), so the wire cancel is never sent and IBKR streams sor indefinitely into the ghost — an unkillable leak plus a permanently misleading drop metric that persists until the client is disposed.
- **Corroboration:** CON (its send-path finding independently described the same orphaned registration — entry added at :246-254 with no rollback when the subscribe-path send throws, replayed on every reconnect with a never-drained channel)
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Wrap the immediate send in try/catch: on failure remove the entry from _subscriptions and writers, complete the channel, then rethrow — so a failed SubscribeTopicAsync leaves no registered state (the consumer's retry becomes the sole registration).
- **Suggested regression test:** SubscribeTopicAsync_SubscribeSendThrows_RollsBackRegistration — FakeWebSocketAdapter throws WebSocketException on the subscribe send; assert the call throws, ActiveSubscriptionCount is 0, a subsequent reconnect replays nothing for that topic, and a sibling subscription's unsubscribe still sends the wire cancel.

### STR-5 — medium — IbkrWebSocketClient.ReconnectAsync (stale trigger tears down a healthy connection)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:700` · lens: streaming delivery & backpressure
- **Claim:** ReconnectAsync unconditionally publishes ConnectionDisconnected and tears the socket down (lines 700-702) after acquiring _connectLock, without re-checking whether the connection was already re-established by the reconnect that just released the lock. Multiple triggers routinely queue for one outage: heartbeat-failure + receive-error fire together on an abrupt reset (lines 549, 596, 616), and OnTickleSucceededAsync's is-open check (line 755) runs before waiting on the lock — during an in-progress reconnect _webSocket is null (line 498), so a tickle landing in that window queues a second reconnect that then destroys the freshly recovered connection.
- **Consequence:** Each stale trigger doubles the streaming coverage gap (extra teardown + 1s delay + tickle REST call + reconnect) on the fill/order channel and emits an extra Disconnected/Reconnected pair, causing consumers that key REST reconciliation off ConnectionDisconnected (the VCR-02/FIL-4 contract) to run redundant reconciliation exactly when the session is struggling. The existing test explicitly tolerates '1 or 2' connects rather than pinning single-cycle recovery.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:679-721 (no healthy-socket re-check after WaitAsync; unconditional PublishConnectionEvent + DisconnectAsync at 700-702), :549/:596/:616 (three independent fire-and-forget triggers per socket), :748-763 (watchdog state check at 755 precedes lock wait; _webSocket nulled during reconnect at 498); tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClientTests.cs:942-945 (comment: 'could be 1 or 2 depending on timing').
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced independently from the public surface. Reachability: IIbkrClient.Streaming -> IStreamingOperations (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IStreamingOperations.cs:49 SubscribeConnectionEvents; src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:42-46) -> IbkrWebSocketClient; all sor/str/smd subscriptions ride this client's reconnect path, and the interface doc (IStreamingOperations.cs:40-42) explicitly contracts that every reconnect emits a Disconnected/Reconnected pair consumers key REST reconciliation off (the VCR-02/FIL-4 contract). Mechanism confirmed: ReconnectAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:679-721) checks _disposed at :681 BEFORE the lock wait at :686, and after acquiring _connectLock performs NO re-check of _webSocket health — lines 700-702 unconditionally PublishConnectionEvent(new ConnectionDisconnected(...)) and await DisconnectAsync(), then 1s delay (:706, _reconnectDelayMs=1000 at :24) + ConnectCoreAsync (:707). The healthy-socket early-return in ConnectCoreAsync (:439-442) cannot save a queued stale trigger because the teardown at :702 has already nulled the socket (DisconnectAsync :497-498). Multiple triggers per outage are real: (a) message-pump WebSocketException -> Task.Run(ReconnectAsync("receive_error")) (:613-617) plus an in-flight heartbeat SendAsync failing concurrently -> ReconnectAsync("heartbeat_failure") (:546-550) — the SendTextAsync state guard (:773-776) only suppresses the heartbeat trigger when the send has not yet started; (b) more routinely, the tickle watchdog: OnTickleSucceededAsync (:748-769) checks _webSocket is Open at :755 BEFORE waiting on the lock inside ReconnectAsync; during an in-progress reconnect _webSocket is null (:498) for a window of >=1s reconnect delay + tickle REST RTT (:454) + WS connect (:472), and TickleTimer fires NotifyTickleSucceededAsync on every successful tickle (src/IbkrConduit/Session/TickleTimer.cs:219, 60s healthy cadence) — a tickle landing in that window queues a stale reconnect that, on acquiring the lock, tears down the freshly recovered socket, emits a second Disconnected/Reconnected pair, and repeats the full 1s+tickle+connect+replay cycle. Counter-evidence hunted and bounded: (1) no infinite cascade — the stale teardown cancels the new pump/heartbeat CTSes, which exit via OperationCanceledException without re-triggering (:554-557, :609-611, :621-624); (2) no self-feeding loop — the tickle inside ConnectCoreAsync (:454) does NOT raise the notification (NotifyTickleSucceededAsync is called only from TickleTimer.RunAsync:219, verified via SessionLifecycleNotifier.cs:70-89); (3) no test pins single-cycle recovery — tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClientTests.cs:912-946 (Reconnect_MessagePumpAndTickleFireConcurrently_BothSerializedViaConnectLock) asserts only ConnectCallCount greater-than-before, with the comment at :942-944 "could be 1 or 2 depending on timing" — a smoke test for no-deadlock, not a pin against the double cycle. Trigger is purely code-side timing (generic transport failure or a tickle landing during the reconnect window), not unpinned IBKR wire semantics, so CONFIRMED. Severity re-judged as medium, not higher: the extra Disconnected/Reconnected pair is published correctly, so fills arriving during the redundant gap are recovered by the documented reconcile-on-Disconnected contract — nothing is silently lost or corrupted (not critical), and the amplification is bounded to one extra cycle per stale trigger with no wedge or session-integrity failure (not high). It is a genuine robustness gap on the fill/order money channel: it roughly doubles the streaming coverage gap and reconciliation load exactly when the session is struggling.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A single transient outage fires two reconnect triggers: the message pump's ReceiveAsync throws (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:616) while either an in-flight heartbeat "tic" send also throws (:549) or — the routine path — the TickleTimer's next successful tickle (TickleTimer.cs:219) lands during the first reconnect's >=1s-plus-two-RTT window in which _webSocket is null (:498), passing OnTickleSucceededAsync's pre-lock is-open check (:755). Both triggers serialize on _connectLock; the first completes a full teardown/reconnect and replays all subscriptions, then the second acquires the lock and — with no post-lock health re-check — unconditionally publishes a second ConnectionDisconnected and destroys the freshly recovered socket (:700-702), imposing a second 1s delay + tickle REST call + reconnect + replay on the sor/str fill channel. The consumer (e.g. realtest-order-steward) subscribed via SubscribeConnectionEvents sees two Disconnected/Reconnected pairs for one outage and runs REST reconciliation twice, and the streaming coverage gap on the order/fill channel is roughly doubled precisely when the session is unstable; the existing test (IbkrWebSocketClientTests.cs:942-945) explicitly tolerates "1 or 2" connects rather than pinning single-cycle recovery. Amplification is bounded (the stale teardown's cancelled pump/heartbeat do not re-trigger, and the in-reconnect tickle does not raise the watchdog notification), and all events are published, so the loss window is observable and reconciled — a robustness gap, not silent data loss.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Inside the lock, skip the cycle (return) when _webSocket is Open and was (re)established after the trigger was raised — e.g. capture a connection epoch/generation at trigger time and compare under the lock.
- **Suggested regression test:** ReconnectAsync_TriggerQueuedBehindSuccessfulReconnect_SkipsRedundantTeardown — queue a watchdog reconnect while a pump-triggered reconnect holds the lock; after both settle assert exactly one Disconnected/Reconnected pair and that the second trigger performed no CloseAsync on the new socket.

### TEN-1 — medium — TenantBuilder (manager eager-init failure path)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Client/TenantBuilder.cs:27` · lens: DI pipeline & multi-tenancy
- **Claim:** SkipLogoutOnDispose=true is set unconditionally before the build (TenantBuilder.cs:27), and the failure path only disposes the child provider and credentials (TenantBuilder.cs:50-62) — SessionManager.DisposeAsync then skips its logout (SessionManager.cs:473) and ManagedTenant (the sole owner of the 'exactly one bounded logout', VCR-08/MGR-1) was never created. So an AddAsync that successfully authenticates the brokerage session (EnsureInitializedAsync, TenantBuilder.cs:44-45) but then fails at IIbkrWebSocketClient.ConnectAsync (TenantBuilder.cs:46-47) — or is cancelled between the two — tears down with ZERO logouts, orphaning a live authenticated IBKR server-side session.
- **Consequence:** A failed AddAsync (transient WS outage, bad WebSocketBaseUrl override, caller cancellation) leaves an authenticated competing brokerage session on IBKR's side with no tickle and no logout; until IBKR's inactivity timeout expires it can 401/compete the same credentials' other live session (the consumer-obligation 'single live session' is violated by the library itself). Residual gap in the VCR-08/MGR-1 exactly-one-logout design — the failure path is exactly zero.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Client/TenantBuilder.cs:27 (flag set pre-build), 44-47 (init then connect), 50-62 (catch: provider+credentials dispose only); src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:470-483 (logout skipped when SkipLogoutOnDispose); src/IbkrConduit/Client/ManagedTenant.cs:33-44 (the only logout, requires successful build)
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced independently from the public surface. (1) Reachability: IIbkrClientManager.AddAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IbkrClientManager.cs:28) calls builder.BuildAsync at IbkrClientManager.cs:74; the real ITenantBuilder is registered in DI at src/IbkrConduit/Http/ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:87. (2) Flag: TenantBuilder.BuildAsync sets effectiveOptions.SkipLogoutOnDispose = true unconditionally BEFORE the build (src/IbkrConduit/Client/TenantBuilder.cs:27), on a per-tenant Clone (IbkrClientManager.cs:62, IbkrClientOptions.cs:167). (3) Trigger window: EnsureInitializedAsync (TenantBuilder.cs:44-45) performs the real ssodh/init, establishing the server-side brokerage session; IIbkrWebSocketClient.ConnectAsync (TenantBuilder.cs:46-47) can then throw — ws.ConnectAsync at src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:472 propagates WebSocketException on a WS outage or bad WebSocketBaseUrl, and cancellation of the caller token between the two calls throws OCE from IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:191/193. (4) Failure path: TenantBuilder's catch (TenantBuilder.cs:50-63) only disposes the child provider and credentials. Provider disposal runs SessionManager.DisposeAsync, which skips its logout because wasInitialized && !SkipLogoutOnDispose is false (src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:473-483) and stops the tickle keepalive (SessionManager.cs:463-466). ManagedTenant — the sole other logout call site — was never constructed (TenantBuilder.cs:48 is unreached). Grep confirms exactly two production LogoutAsync call sites: SessionManager.cs:477 (suppressed) and ManagedTenant.cs:38 (never created). Net: zero logout requests, no tickle — an authenticated IBKR server-side session is abandoned until IBKR's inactivity timeout. (5) Residual-gap attribution: pre-VCR-08, this same failure path DID log out — the original MGR-1 finding (docs/findings/2026-07-04-rtos-venue-consumer-review.md:133) documents SessionManager's unconditional dispose-time LogoutAsync; the VCR-08 spec (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-07-vcr-08-manager-lifecycle.md:15) only says 'the redundant second logout ... is deduplicated to one' and never addresses the build-failure path, so PR #245/#249-era code removed the only logout on this path without a replacement. (6) No test pins it: ManagerTeardownTests covers RemoveAsync logout counting (RemoveAsync_IssuesExactlyOneLogout, tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Integration/MultiTenant/ManagerTeardownTests.cs:94); unit tests cover builder-throw credential disposal only (FakeTenantBuilder, no real session). The other partial-failure paths inside AddAsync (TryUpdate loss at IbkrClientManager.cs:75-79, disposed re-check at :90-97) DO call tenant.DisposeAsync and get the ManagedTenant logout — only the TenantBuilder-internal window is zero-logout. Severity: medium, not high — the orphan is bounded (IBKR inactivity timeout kills an untickled session), Compete defaults to true (IbkrClientOptions.cs:23) so a retry's ssodh/init reclaims the slot, and harm requires both an eager-init failure and either concurrent same-credential usage or a Compete=false retry loop during the window. It is a robustness gap that transiently violates the single-live-session guarantee the library itself enforces elsewhere.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer calls IIbkrClientManager.AddAsync with a transiently unreachable WS endpoint, a bad WebSocketBaseUrl override, or a token that gets cancelled mid-add. TenantBuilder.BuildAsync has already set SkipLogoutOnDispose=true (TenantBuilder.cs:27) and successfully completed EnsureInitializedAsync (TenantBuilder.cs:44-45), so a live authenticated brokerage session now exists on IBKR's side; ConnectAsync (TenantBuilder.cs:46-47) then throws. The catch block (TenantBuilder.cs:50-63) disposes the child provider — SessionManager.DisposeAsync skips its logout because the flag is set (SessionManager.cs:473) and stops the tickle keepalive — and ManagedTenant, the only other LogoutAsync call site (ManagedTenant.cs:38), was never created. AddAsync rethrows having issued zero logout requests, orphaning an authenticated, untickled IBKR server-side session until IBKR's inactivity timeout expires; during that window it can compete with / 401 another live session on the same credentials, and a Compete=false retry loop keeps failing init against the orphan. Pre-VCR-08 this path logged out via SessionManager's dispose; the VCR-08 exactly-one-logout dedup (spec 2026-07-07-vcr-08-manager-lifecycle.md:15) suppressed it without adding a failure-path replacement, and no test covers logout on a failed eager init.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** In TenantBuilder's catch, when EnsureInitializedAsync had succeeded, issue the same bounded best-effort LogoutAsync (linked CTS with LogoutTimeout) before disposing the provider — mirroring ManagedTenant.DisposeAsync; or set SkipLogoutOnDispose only on success just before constructing ManagedTenant.
- **Suggested regression test:** BuildAsync_WebSocketConnectFails_IssuesBoundedLogout — TenantBuilder (or manager-level WireMock test) where ssodh/init succeeds and WS connect throws; assert /logout was called exactly once and the provider/credentials are disposed.

### TEN-2 — medium — ResponseSchemaValidationHandler + RefitEndpointMap (strict mode, string-returning endpoints)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Http/ResponseSchemaValidationHandler.cs:53` · lens: DI pipeline & multi-tenancy
- **Claim:** RefitEndpointMap.Build deliberately omits IApiResponse<string> endpoints (RefitEndpointMap.cs:115-119) but ResponseSchemaValidationHandler treats a null map entry as a violation in strict mode and throws IbkrSchemaViolationException 'No DTO mapping' (ResponseSchemaValidationHandler.cs:50-57). Four mapped-interface endpoints hit this on every successful call, including POST /iserver/reply/{replyId} (IIbkrOrderApi.cs:22) — the order-confirmation reply invoked by Orders.ReplyAsync (OrderOperations.cs:330) through the consumer pipeline that carries the handler (ConsumerPipelineRegistration.cs:49, 104-108). The throw happens AFTER base.SendAsync returns 200, i.e. after IBKR already processed the reply.
- **Consequence:** With StrictResponseValidation=true (the documented dev/paper fail-fast mode), every order-confirmation reply deterministically throws even though the order WAS confirmed server-side — the consumer sees an exception for a filled/working order and may resubmit (duplicate order in strict-enabled paper/dev environments). DeleteDeviceAsync, DismissNotificationAsync and InvalidatePortfolioCacheAsync are likewise unusable in strict mode. Residual gap from the VCR-10 (#248) validation-net hardening: the strict net cannot distinguish 'unknown endpoint' from 'known raw-string endpoint'.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Http/RefitEndpointMap.cs:115-119 (IApiResponse<string> skipped from map); src/IbkrConduit/Http/ResponseSchemaValidationHandler.cs:50-61 (strict throws on null dtoInfo); src/IbkrConduit/Orders/IIbkrOrderApi.cs:22 (ReplyAsync returns IApiResponse<string>); src/IbkrConduit/Client/OrderOperations.cs:318-330 (public reply flow); src/IbkrConduit/Http/ConsumerPipelineRegistration.cs:49,104-108 (handler on IIbkrOrderApi pipeline); tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Http/ResponseSchemaValidationHandlerTests.cs:180-191 pins only the genuinely-unknown-path case
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced independently at main@18c6a23. (1) Map omission: RefitEndpointMap.UnwrapReturnType returns null for Task<IApiResponse<string>> (src/IbkrConduit/Http/RefitEndpointMap.cs:115-119, "IApiResponse<string> -- raw string response, skip"), so Build() adds no entry (RefitEndpointMap.cs:35-39) and TryGetDtoType returns null (RefitEndpointMap.cs:57-68). (2) Strict throw: ResponseSchemaValidationHandler.SendAsync awaits the inner send first (ResponseSchemaValidationHandler.cs:37), gates on IsSuccessStatusCode (:39), then on a null dtoInfo throws IbkrSchemaViolationException("No DTO mapping...") when StrictResponseValidation is true (ResponseSchemaValidationHandler.cs:50-57) — this throw fires BEFORE the JSON content-type/empty-body skips (:63-77), so it triggers on any 2xx regardless of body; no wire dependency at all. (3) Four affected endpoints, all IApiResponse<string> on mapped interfaces: ReplyAsync POST /v1/api/iserver/reply/{replyId} (src/IbkrConduit/Orders/IIbkrOrderApi.cs:21-23), DismissNotificationAsync (IIbkrOrderApi.cs:78-80), DeleteDeviceAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Fyi/IIbkrFyiApi.cs:67), InvalidatePortfolioCacheAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Portfolio/IIbkrPortfolioApi.cs:74) — all four interfaces are in the map build (src/IbkrConduit/Http/ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:116-126) and all four pipelines carry the handler (src/IbkrConduit/Http/ConsumerPipelineRegistration.cs:47-55, 104-108). (4) Public reachability: IIbkrClient.Orders.ReplyAsync -> OrderOperations.ReplyAsync calls _orderApi.ReplyAsync with no try/catch around the send (src/IbkrConduit/Client/OrderOperations.cs:330-331; the try at :348 only catches JsonException from deserialization), and Refit's IApiResponse wrapper only captures ApiException from status/deserialization — a handler-thrown IbkrSchemaViolationException propagates out of the public method. Same pattern for FyiOperations.DeleteDeviceAsync (:133), OrderOperations.DismissNotificationAsync (:406), PortfolioOperations.InvalidatePortfolioCacheAsync (:160). (5) Strict mode is reachable config: IbkrClientOptions.StrictResponseValidation, default false, documented "Enable in dev/test environments for fail-fast behavior" (src/IbkrConduit/Session/IbkrClientOptions.cs:97-102). (6) No counter-evidence: unit tests pin only the genuinely-unknown-path case (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Http/ResponseSchemaValidationHandlerTests.cs:180-191) and the test Refit interface has no IApiResponse<string> method (:40-53); integration ResponseSchemaValidationTests never exercise the reply/notification/cache/device endpoints (grep for "reply" in tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Integration/Pipeline/ResponseSchemaValidationTests.cs: no hits). (7) Residual-gap attribution holds: the strict throw-on-unmapped predates VCR-10 (commit fac1222) and VCR-10/#248 (e8a665c) hardened collection/extension-data validation without touching the null-map branch or RefitEndpointMap's string skip (git log on RefitEndpointMap.cs shows no VCR-10 commit) — the net still cannot distinguish "unknown endpoint" from "known raw-string endpoint". Severity re-judged medium, not high: the failure is loud (a typed exception, not silently wrong data), confined to the opt-in StrictResponseValidation=true mode which defaults off and is documented as dev/test-only, so the default production money path is unaffected. The sting is that the throw lands after IBKR already processed the confirmation reply server-side, so in strict-enabled paper/dev runs a naive retry-on-exception can resubmit and duplicate an order, and the fail-fast net itself is deterministically broken on four endpoints — a robustness/observability defect in the safety tool rather than a direct production money-loss path.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer enables the documented dev/paper fail-fast mode (IbkrClientOptions.StrictResponseValidation = true), places an order that returns OrderConfirmationRequired, and calls client.Orders.ReplyAsync(replyId, confirmed: true). IBKR accepts the reply and returns 2xx — the order is now confirmed/working server-side — but ResponseSchemaValidationHandler, sitting on the IIbkrOrderApi pipeline, looks up POST /v1/api/iserver/reply/{replyId} in RefitEndpointMap, finds no entry (Build deliberately skips IApiResponse<string> endpoints), and because the null-map strict branch runs before any content-type or body check, deterministically throws IbkrSchemaViolationException("No DTO mapping for POST /v1/api/iserver/reply/...") on every successful call. The exception escapes the public ReplyAsync (no catch on that path), so the consumer sees a hard failure for an order that was actually confirmed — retry logic resubmits and duplicates the order in the strict-enabled paper/dev environment, and the confirmation flow (plus DismissNotificationAsync, DeleteDeviceAsync, InvalidatePortfolioCacheAsync) is unusable whenever strict validation is on. Residual gap left open by VCR-10/#248, which hardened element and extension-data validation but never taught the strict net to distinguish a known raw-string endpoint from a genuinely unmapped one.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Have UnwrapReturnType return a 'known, validation-skipped' sentinel entry for IApiResponse<string>/OneOf/Task returns so TryGetDtoType can distinguish known-raw endpoints (pass through) from truly unmapped ones (strict throw).
- **Suggested regression test:** StrictMode_StringReturningMappedEndpoint_PassesThrough — build the map over IIbkrOrderApi, strict handler, POST /v1/api/iserver/reply/123 returning 200 'o451' JSON; assert no throw (today: IbkrSchemaViolationException).

### TEN-3 — medium — ServiceCollectionExtensions.ValidateOptions (shared by AddIbkrClient and manager AddAsync)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Http/ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:182` · lens: DI pipeline & multi-tenancy
- **Claim:** ValidateOptions — documented as 'Validates all IbkrClientOptions fields to fail fast on misconfiguration' (ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:170-178) — checks TickleIntervalSeconds (line 182) but not TickleFailureIntervalSeconds, WebSocketHeartbeatIntervalSeconds, or StreamingBufferSize. A negative TickleFailureIntervalSeconds passes registration; then, after the FIRST failed tickle flips _lastTickleSucceeded (TickleTimer.cs:206/246), Task.Delay(TimeSpan.FromSeconds(negative)) at TickleTimer.cs:178-179 throws ArgumentOutOfRangeException, faulting RunAsync unobserved — StopAsync's catch handles only OperationCanceledException (TickleTimer.cs:130-140), so the fault also rethrows during SessionManager dispose (SessionManager.cs:465).
- **Consequence:** Session keep-alive and the tickle-succeeded WebSocket reconnect watchdog silently stop at the first transient tickle failure (possibly days after startup): the session expires, streaming never reconnects, and each REST call pays a 401+re-auth — a delayed, silent liveness degradation the fail-fast validation contract exists to prevent, plus a crash out of DisposeAsync at teardown. Same gap in both registration paths (parity holds; the omission is shared). Zero TickleFailureIntervalSeconds similarly produces a zero-delay tickle hot loop during outages.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Http/ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:178-227 (validated set excludes the three fields; XML doc at 170-173 claims all fields); src/IbkrConduit/Http/SessionServiceRegistration.cs:66-67 (unvalidated value flows to TickleTimerFactory); src/IbkrConduit/Session/TickleTimer.cs:178-179 (Task.Delay on the raw value), 130-140 (StopAsync swallows only OCE); src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:463-466 (StopAsync awaited in dispose)
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced end-to-end at HEAD. (1) ValidateOptions (src/IbkrConduit/Http/ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:178-227) — XML doc at 169-174 claims it validates all IbkrClientOptions fields — checks TickleIntervalSeconds (line 182), PreflightCacheDuration (190), ProactiveRefreshMargin (198), FlexPollTimeout (206), BaseUrl/WebSocketBaseUrl (214/221), but NOT TickleFailureIntervalSeconds, WebSocketHeartbeatIntervalSeconds, or StreamingBufferSize (defined at src/IbkrConduit/Session/IbkrClientOptions.cs:71/78/89, cloned at 159-161, no other guard anywhere — grep confirmed). Both public registration paths share the validator: AddIbkrClient at ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:56 and IbkrClientManager.AddAsync at src/IbkrConduit/Client/IbkrClientManager.cs:69. (2) The raw TickleFailureIntervalSeconds flows to TickleTimerFactory (src/IbkrConduit/Http/SessionServiceRegistration.cs:67) and into TickleTimer (TickleTimer.cs:95, factory Create at 318). (3) RunAsync selects _failureIntervalSeconds once _lastTickleSucceeded is false (TickleTimer.cs:178); any single failed tickle sets it false (line 206 for an unauthenticated payload, line 246 for any transport/5xx exception). The next iteration calls Task.Delay(TimeSpan.FromSeconds(negativeValue), _timeProvider, ct) at line 179 — a negative TimeSpan other than InfiniteTimeSpan throws ArgumentOutOfRangeException (framework contract), faulting the background task stored by StartAsync (lines 104-105) with nothing observing it. Keep-alive tickles and the NotifyTickleSucceededAsync WebSocket-reconnect-watchdog signal (line 219) stop permanently and silently. (4) StopAsync awaits the faulted task catching only OperationCanceledException (TickleTimer.cs:130-140), and SessionManager.DisposeAsync awaits StopAsync unguarded (src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:463-466), so the AOORE escapes DisposeAsync and skips the logout/semaphore-dispose tail (473-486). (5) Parity of the shared omission verified: negative WebSocketHeartbeatIntervalSeconds hits the identical pattern at src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:539 (fire-and-forget loop catching only OCE at 554), silently killing the required 'tic' keep-alive; zero TickleFailureIntervalSeconds is legal for Task.Delay and yields a zero-delay tickle loop during failure bursts, throttled only by the rate-limiting handlers (SessionServiceRegistration.cs:84-94). Counter-evidence hunt: no clamp/Math.Max/fallback anywhere; no VCR PR (#238-#249) touched the validated set; no test pins rejection of these fields (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Session/SessionModelsTests.cs:117 pins only the default; integration tests set the value to 1). Severity: trigger requires the consumer to configure an invalid value, so not high under valid config — but the documented fail-fast net exists precisely to catch this, and what it hides is a delayed silent session/streaming liveness loss plus a dispose-path crash: a robustness gap hiding a high-class failure → medium per rubric.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer sets IbkrClientOptions.TickleFailureIntervalSeconds to a negative value (e.g. a typo or a bad config binding) in AddIbkrClient or a per-tenant override via IIbkrClientManager.AddAsync; registration succeeds because ValidateOptions (ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:182-227) never checks that field despite documenting fail-fast validation of all fields. The system runs normally on the validated healthy interval until the FIRST transient tickle failure — a network blip or IBKR 5xx, possibly days later — flips _lastTickleSucceeded (TickleTimer.cs:206/246); the next loop iteration's Task.Delay(TimeSpan.FromSeconds(negative)) (TickleTimer.cs:178-179) throws ArgumentOutOfRangeException, faulting RunAsync unobserved. From that moment session keep-alive tickles and the tickle-succeeded WebSocket reconnect watchdog stop silently: the IBKR session ages out, a dropped WebSocket never reconnects (streaming order/fill events stop), and every REST call pays a 401 + full re-auth round trip. At shutdown, SessionManager.DisposeAsync (SessionManager.cs:465) awaits TickleTimer.StopAsync, which rethrows the stored AOORE (only OCE is caught, TickleTimer.cs:130-140), crashing provider disposal and skipping logout. Zero instead of negative produces a zero-delay tickle hot loop during outages; the same validation omission applies to WebSocketHeartbeatIntervalSeconds (silent heartbeat death at IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:539) and StreamingBufferSize.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Add range checks in ValidateOptions: TickleFailureIntervalSeconds > 0, WebSocketHeartbeatIntervalSeconds > 0, StreamingBufferSize > 0, throwing the same ArgumentOutOfRangeException shapes as the existing fields.
- **Suggested regression test:** ValidateOptions_NonPositiveTickleFailureInterval_Throws — AddIbkrClient (and manager AddAsync) with TickleFailureIntervalSeconds = -1 and 0 must throw ArgumentOutOfRangeException at registration, not fault the tickle loop later.

### WIR-2 — medium — ResponseSchemaValidationHandler + DtoFieldMap (REST validation net, VCR-10 residual)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Http/ResponseSchemaValidationHandler.cs:121` · lens: wire fidelity & serialization
- **Claim:** The validation net never descends into nested DTO fields or wrapped collections: ValidateResponseBody diffs only the top-level FieldNames of each validation target (ResponseSchemaValidationHandler.cs:121-153), DtoFieldMap.NestedMaps is computed (DtoFieldMap.cs:47-52) but never consulted by any caller, and GetNestedDtoType deliberately skips List<>/Dictionary<,> element types (DtoFieldMap.cs:163-171). EnumerateValidationTargets fans out only when the Refit RETURN type is a List/Dictionary (RefitEndpointMap.cs:124-147) — so GET /iserver/account/orders, whose return type is the wrapper OrdersResponse{orders:List<LiveOrder>, snapshot} (IIbkrOrderApi.cs:39, IIbkrOrderApiModels.cs:200-202), has only the two wrapper keys validated: LiveOrder rows — the money-critical payload VCR-10's own scope text cites ("live-orders caps at 1000") — are never schema-checked, and neither are nested objects like WhatIfResponse.Amount/Initial/Maintenance.
- **Consequence:** An IBKR rename/removal of a money field on live-order rows (price, filledQuantity, totalSize) or what-if margin/commission objects is invisible to the drift net the consumer relies on: the field silently deserializes to null (nullable, absorbed by [JsonExtensionData]) with no schema-mismatch warning or strict-mode throw — exactly the silent-drift class WIR-5/VCR-10 was built to surface.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Http/ResponseSchemaValidationHandler.cs:99,121-153,176-210 (only fieldMap.FieldNames used; enumeration driven solely by EndpointDtoInfo); src/IbkrConduit/Http/DtoFieldMap.cs:47-52,163-171,195 (NestedMaps built, List skip); grep shows NestedMaps referenced only by DtoFieldMapTests; src/IbkrConduit/Orders/IIbkrOrderApi.cs:39; src/IbkrConduit/Orders/IIbkrOrderApiModels.cs:200-202; docs/backlog.md VCR-10 entry
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced end-to-end at HEAD 18c6a23. (1) Reachability: ResponseSchemaValidationHandler is wired into the default consumer pipeline (src/IbkrConduit/Http/ConsumerPipelineRegistration.cs:105-108), so all IIbkrClient REST traffic passes through it. (2) GetLiveOrdersAsync returns Task<IApiResponse<OrdersResponse>> (src/IbkrConduit/Orders/IIbkrOrderApi.cs:38-39); OrdersResponse maps only "orders" and "snapshot" (src/IbkrConduit/Orders/IIbkrOrderApiModels.cs:200-202). RefitEndpointMap.UnwrapReturnType classifies it as plain-T (src/IbkrConduit/Http/RefitEndpointMap.cs:156, IsCollection:false, IsDictionary:false), so EnumerateValidationTargets yields only the root wrapper object (src/IbkrConduit/Http/ResponseSchemaValidationHandler.cs:209); the List/Dictionary fan-out (RefitEndpointMap.cs:124-147, handler lines 176-207) applies only to Refit RETURN-type collections, never to collections nested inside a wrapper DTO. (3) ValidateResponseBody diffs only the top-level fieldMap.FieldNames (ResponseSchemaValidationHandler.cs:111, 137-152) and never consults DtoFieldInfo.NestedMaps — grep confirms NestedMaps is referenced only by DtoFieldMap.cs itself (lines 51, 195, 208) and DtoFieldMapTests.cs:106-107; no production caller. Even the built map would lack LiveOrder under "orders" because GetNestedDtoType skips List<>/Dictionary<,> (src/IbkrConduit/Http/DtoFieldMap.cs:163-171). (4) Consequence verified: a rename/removal of price/filledQuantity/totalSize on LiveOrder rows leaves the wrapper's two keys unchanged, producing zero extra/missing findings — no warning, no strict-mode IbkrSchemaViolationException. The renamed field is silently absorbed by LiveOrder's [JsonExtensionData] AdditionalData (IIbkrOrderApiModels.cs:287-288) and the nullable money fields (lines 266-276, VCR-01 null-as-presence semantics) read null — indistinguishable from a legitimately sparse row. Identical blind spot for WhatIfResponse's nested WhatIfAmount/WhatIfMargin money objects (IIbkrOrderApiModels.cs:421-427, 441-449, 475-478): their NestedMaps entries exist but are never consulted. (5) No counter-evidence in tests: integration schema tests exercise only GetAccountsAsync (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Integration/Pipeline/ResponseSchemaValidationTests.cs:38,63,92,117,136); nothing pins nested descent. (6) Residual, not deliberate deferral: the VCR-10 entry (docs/backlog.md:141-147, PR #248) scoped every-element collection validation + extension-data extra-field diffs + streaming money census, never mentioning wrapper/nested descent — yet its scope text justifies full-collection validation with "live-orders caps at 1000", an endpoint whose rows the mechanism never actually visits. The net's failure is deterministic and offline-demonstrable given a drifted body, so CONFIRMED rather than PLAUSIBLE; the only live-wire dependency is when IBKR drifts, which is the feature's premise, not the defect's. Severity medium per rubric: an observability gap that hides a would-be silently-wrong-data event; it does not itself corrupt data today, and non-strict consumers were never promised more than a warning — but strict-mode consumers (StrictResponseValidation, src/IbkrConduit/Session/IbkrClientOptions.cs:102) get a false sense of coverage on the money-critical live-orders and what-if payloads.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer (e.g. realtest-order-steward) enables the response-schema drift net — or just relies on its warning logs — and IBKR ships a Client Portal change that renames or drops a money field on live-order rows (e.g. "filledQuantity" -> "filled_qty") or inside the what-if "amount"/"initial"/"maintenance" objects. Because GET /iserver/account/orders returns the OrdersResponse wrapper, the handler validates only the top-level keys "orders" and "snapshot" (ResponseSchemaValidationHandler.cs:209 yields the root; only FieldNames at line 111 are diffed; NestedMaps is never consulted and GetNestedDtoType skips List<> at DtoFieldMap.cs:163-171), so the drifted row produces zero extra/missing findings: no schema-mismatch warning, no strict-mode throw. The renamed field is silently swallowed by LiveOrder.AdditionalData ([JsonExtensionData], IIbkrOrderApiModels.cs:287-288) and FilledQuantity/Price/TotalSize deserialize to null — which VCR-01 semantics tell the consumer to read as "absent from this row", so a permanent upstream schema break is indistinguishable from routine sparse data and the consumer's fill/price tracking degrades with no signal from the net built specifically to catch this (VCR-10/WIR-5 residual: the collection fan-out shipped in #248 covers only Refit return-type collections, not wrapped ones).
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Recurse validation through DtoFieldInfo.NestedMaps (object-valued fields) and extend GetNestedDtoType/EnumerateValidationTargets to descend into List<T>-typed DTO properties, so wrapper-shaped endpoints validate their row elements; bound recursion depth against cyclic DTOs.
- **Suggested regression test:** ResponseSchemaValidation_LiveOrderRowFieldDrift_ReportsMismatch — WireMock GET /iserver/account/orders returning {orders:[{...price renamed to px...}],snapshot:true}; asserts a schema-mismatch warning (and strict-mode IbkrSchemaViolationException) naming the drifted field.

### WIR-4 — medium — AccountSummaryOverview / AccountSummaryCashBalance (account summary REST — money typed double)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Accounts/IIbkrAccountApiModels.cs:194` · lens: wire fidelity & serialization
- **Claim:** All sixteen money fields of AccountSummaryOverview (balance, SMA, buyingPower, availableFunds, excessLiquidity, netLiquidationValue, equityWithLoanValue, regTLoan, securitiesGVP, totalCashValue, accruedInterest, regTMargin, initialMargin, maintenanceMargin — IIbkrAccountApiModels.cs:194-207) and AccountSummaryCashBalance.Balance/SettledCash (:166-167) are typed double?, unlike every other money surface in the library (decimal per the prior review's census, which covered only Orders/Trades/LiveOrder/streaming/Portfolio — Accounts was outside it). EventContract Payout/Strike ride non-nullable double too (IIbkrEventContractApiModels.cs:81,107,190,199). Binary double cannot exactly represent decimal wire values, so balances round-trip with float artifacts.
- **Consequence:** A consumer using GetAccountSummaryAsync for pre-trade risk gates (buying power, available funds, margin headroom) receives silently imprecise money numerics (e.g. 1234567.89 -> 1234567.8899999999) that break exact-comparison logic and re-conversion to decimal, inconsistently with the decimal guarantee the rest of the facade provides; sub-cent today, but it is the only money surface where the library itself degrades wire precision.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Accounts/IIbkrAccountApiModels.cs:164-167,190-208; src/IbkrConduit/Client/AccountOperations.cs:95 (public reachability via IIbkrClient.Accounts.GetAccountSummaryAsync); src/IbkrConduit/EventContracts/IIbkrEventContractApiModels.cs:81,107,190,199; docs/findings/2026-07-04-rtos-venue-consumer-review.md:561 (decimal census scoped to Orders/Trades/LiveOrder/streaming/Portfolio only)
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Mechanism verified end-to-end at HEAD. (1) Types: AccountSummaryOverview declares 14 money fields as double? — balance/SMA/buyingPower/availableFunds/excessLiquidity/netLiquidationValue/equityWithLoanValue/regTLoan/securitiesGVP/totalCashValue/accruedInterest/regTMargin/initialMargin/maintenanceMargin (src/IbkrConduit/Accounts/IIbkrAccountApiModels.cs:194-207); AccountSummaryCashBalance.Balance/SettledCash are double? (:166-167). EventContract Payout/Strike are non-nullable double (src/IbkrConduit/EventContracts/IIbkrEventContractApiModels.cs:81,107,190,199), exposed via IIbkrClient.EventContracts (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IIbkrClient.cs:63). (2) Reachability: IIbkrClient.Accounts.GetAccountSummaryAsync — IAccountOperations.cs:54, implemented at src/IbkrConduit/Client/AccountOperations.cs:95-104, exercised through the full DI stack in tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Integration/Accounts/AccountsTests.cs:303-330 via _harness.Client.Accounts. (3) Inconsistency: the parallel /portfolio/{acct}/summary surface types the same figures decimal? (AccountSummaryEntry.Amount, src/IbkrConduit/Portfolio/IIbkrPortfolioApiModels.cs:215), and the prior review's census statement "no money field rides double" (docs/findings/2026-07-04-rtos-venue-consumer-review.md:561) explicitly scoped only Orders/Trades/LiveOrder/streaming/Portfolio — Accounts and EventContracts were never censused, and no VCR story (VCR-01 spec covers OrderUpdate/TradeExecution/Trade/LiveOrder/status events; VCR-10 is the validation net) touched these files. No recorded policy exists: grep of docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md for "decimal" returns nothing, and ADR-0001 covers nullability only (docs/adr/0001-nullable-as-presence-wire-fidelity.md:8). (4) Trigger grounded: the endpoint is absent from docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md (only /portfolio/{acct}/summary at :5722) and the Accounts fixture is whole-dollar (tests/.../Fixtures/Accounts/GET-account-summary.json, all *.0), but the captured portfolio-summary fixture for the same account figures shows fractional cents on the wire (tests/.../Fixtures/Portfolio/GET-portfolio-summary.json:9 "buyingpower": 3984328.97, :11 "initmarginreq": 15276.38), so cent-precision values on these fields are real, and any such value parsed into double carries a binary representation error by mathematical necessity — no live probe needed. (5) Counter-evidence that softens the claimed consequence: the artifact is largely masked below 16 significant digits — .NET's Decimal(Double) conversion rounds to 15 significant digits and double.ToString uses shortest-round-trip formatting, so (decimal)3984328.97d == 3984328.97m exactly; the claimed observable "1234567.89 -> 1234567.8899999999" does not surface on decimal re-conversion or formatting for realistic balances. Observable wrongness requires >15-significant-digit values or consumer-side double arithmetic at an exact comparison boundary (~1e-10 relative error). Also the type is visible in the public signature (double?, not silent coercion), and a decimal-typed alternative for the same risk figures exists on the facade (IIbkrClient.Portfolio.GetAccountSummaryAsync). Severity re-judged medium: not itself money-losing at observable magnitudes, but a reachable robustness/consistency gap on a money surface that can hide sub-cent wrong data and invites consumer double-space money math, and it falsifies the census the 0.9.0 breaking set was cut against.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer builds pre-trade risk gates on IIbkrClient.Accounts.GetAccountSummaryAsync (AccountOperations.cs:95) — the natural "account summary" facade method — reading AvailableFunds/BuyingPower/MaintenanceMargin as double? (IIbkrAccountApiModels.cs:196-207). IBKR sends these figures with cents (pinned for the same account figures on the parallel portfolio surface: GET-portfolio-summary.json "buyingpower": 3984328.97), so every fractional value lands in the DTO with a binary double artifact the rest of the library's decimal-typed money surface never introduces. The artifact is masked on ToString/decimal re-conversion below 16 significant digits, so the practical exposure is narrower than claimed: consumer-side arithmetic or comparisons performed in double space (e.g. availableFunds - requiredMargin >= 0 exactly at the boundary), mixed decimal/double money math forced by the type inconsistency, or >15-significant-digit balances. The defect is a residual contract gap the VCR stream left open — the decimal census (findings doc :561) never covered Accounts or EventContracts (Payout/Strike also ride non-nullable double, IIbkrEventContractApiModels.cs:81,107) — and no design-doc/ADR numeric-type policy exists to catch the next off-convention DTO.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Retype the AccountSummaryOverview/AccountSummaryCashBalance (and event-contract strike/payout) money fields to decimal? (breaking, 📦 semver review); the shared empty-tolerant decimal converters already cover the wire shapes.
- **Suggested regression test:** AccountSummaryOverview_MoneyFields_RoundTripDecimalExactly — WireMock fixture with "buyingPower":1234567.89 asserts the DTO surfaces exactly 1234567.89m with no binary-float drift.

### ORD-1 — medium — OrderOperations.ReplyAsync (question/reply classification net)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Client/OrderOperations.cs:354` · lens: orders & money path
- **Claim:** The WIR-4 classification net in ReplyAsync catches only JsonException, but DeserializeReplyResponse throws InvalidOperationException for a 2xx reply body that is empty/whitespace (OrderOperations.cs:477-481), a body that is neither array nor object such as plain text or HTML (OrderOperations.cs:501-502), or a body that deserializes to JSON null (OrderOperations.cs:489-491, 495-497). Those InvalidOperationExceptions escape ReplyAsync uncaught instead of surfacing as a classified Result failure.
- **Consequence:** After the consumer transmitted confirmed=true (the order may now be live at the broker), a 200-with-empty/plain-text body — body shapes the library itself documents as real IBKR error formats (ResultFactory.cs:10-11 'empty bodies, and plain text/HTML') — crashes the consumer's Result-based order pipeline with an untyped InvalidOperationException, or gets misread as a definitive refusal, while the confirmation may actually have gone through. Inconsistent with the sibling path: a malformed body starting with '[' or '{' IS classified (JsonException → IbkrApiError with StatusCode OK, OrderOperations.cs:354-364).
- **Evidence:** OrderOperations.cs:347-364 (try/catch catches only JsonException); OrderOperations.cs:474-503 (DeserializeReplyResponse throws InvalidOperationException on empty/non-JSON/null-deserialize bodies); ResultFactory.cs:127-137 (DetectHiddenError returns null for whitespace body, so the empty body reaches DeserializeReplyResponse); tests pin the throw only at helper level, never at ReplyAsync level: tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Orders/OrderOperationsReplyTests.cs:122-143; the only ReplyAsync-level unparseable test uses a '['-prefixed body (JsonException path): tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Orders/OrderOperationsAmbiguityTests.cs:164-176. Residual of VCR-04 (WIR-4/AMB-4).
- **Skeptic verdict:** **PLAUSIBLE** — Independent end-to-end trace from the public surface. (1) Reachability: IIbkrClient.Orders (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IIbkrClient.cs:23) -> IOrderOperations.ReplyAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IOrderOperations.cs:104) -> OrderOperations.ReplyAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/OrderOperations.cs:318-367). (2) The reply POST transmits confirmed=true (OrderOperations.cs:330-331), then the response routes through the identity-parser overload ResultFactory.FromResponse (OrderOperations.cs:340 -> src/IbkrConduit/Errors/ResultFactory.cs:64-91). For a 2xx with empty/whitespace body, DetectHiddenError returns null (ResultFactory.cs:129-132) and FromResponse returns Success("") (ResultFactory.cs:87); for a 2xx plain-text/HTML body, DetectHiddenError's JsonException catch also returns null (ResultFactory.cs:142-148) and the raw text is passed through as Success. (3) DeserializeReplyResponse then throws InvalidOperationException for an empty/whitespace body (OrderOperations.cs:477-481) and for a body whose first non-space char is neither '[' nor '{' (OrderOperations.cs:501-502); ClassifyResponse additionally throws InvalidOperationException for a residual JSON object with no order_id/no question/no error (OrderOperations.cs:466-468, reached via ClassifyOrderResponses at 439 for e.g. a bare "{}" 200 body). (4) The WIR-4 net at OrderOperations.cs:348-364 catches ONLY JsonException, so all of those InvalidOperationExceptions escape ReplyAsync uncaught — even for ThrowOnApiError=false Result-pattern consumers. This contradicts the merged VCR-04 spec's own decision, item 5: "A 2xx body that still fails deserialization surfaces as a classified error type (not a raw exception) so consumers can map it to their ambiguous leg by type" (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-07-vcr-04-order-outcome-replay-gate.md, Scope item 5) — so this is a genuine residual of VCR-04/WIR-4, not deliberate design (the only deliberately-kept throw is the ClassifyResponse residual, per the AMB-4 comment at OrderOperations.cs:411-415). (5) Tests confirm rather than refute: the throws are pinned at helper level only (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Orders/OrderOperationsReplyTests.cs:121-146 pin InvalidOperationException for ""/" "/"not-json"), and the only ReplyAsync-level unparseable-body test uses a '['-prefixed body that takes the JsonException path (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Orders/OrderOperationsAmbiguityTests.cs:164-176). (6) Sibling inconsistency confirmed: on place/modify the same shapes classify — a 2xx with null deserialized Content becomes IbkrApiError "Response body could not be deserialized" (ResultFactory.cs:47-54), never an escape. (7) Trigger is NOT pinned: the captured spec documents the reply 200 response only as an array of objects with a 503 error mode (docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md, /iserver/reply section, response-body table and example around lines 4617-4661), recordings/ contains no reply captures (grep for "reply" over recordings/ returns nothing; dirs are bracket/bulk/cleanup/oca/orderref/orders/priming), and the documented empty/plain-text 200 bodies belong to other endpoints (/iserver/notification, DELETE /fyi/deliveryoptions — docs/ibkr-error-patterns-report.md:197-209). Hence PLAUSIBLE, not CONFIRMED. Two corrections to the sweep's claim: (a) the "deserializes to JSON null" branches (OrderOperations.cs:489-490, 496-497) are effectively unreachable — a payload starting with '[' or '{' cannot deserialize to null, and the literal "null" body hits line 501 instead; (b) there is no "misread as definitive refusal" path — the failure mode is always the uncaught throw. Severity: medium — the consequence if triggered is an untyped exception crossing the Result contract at the post-confirmation money boundary (the consumer cannot map it to its ambiguous leg by type, exactly what VCR-04 set out to guarantee), but the failure is loud rather than silent, non-2xx error pages ARE classified (ResultFactory.cs:90, 93-125), and the trigger shape is unpinned and outside every IBKR-documented reply response; this matches the medium rating the original review gave the sibling WIR-4 deserialization-escape defect.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer places an order, receives OrderConfirmationRequired, and calls client.Orders.ReplyAsync(replyId, confirmed: true) — the confirmation POST reaches IBKR and the order may now be transmitting. If the broker (or an intermediary gateway) answers that POST with HTTP 200 and a body that is empty, whitespace, a bare unrecognized JSON object like {}, or non-JSON text/HTML — shapes IBKR documents on other endpoints but that are not pinned for /iserver/reply by the captured spec or any recording — ResultFactory.FromResponse passes the raw body through as Success (DetectHiddenError returns null for whitespace and for JSON-unparseable text, ResultFactory.cs:129-148), DeserializeReplyResponse or ClassifyResponse throws InvalidOperationException (OrderOperations.cs:477-481, 501-502, 466-468), and the catch at OrderOperations.cs:354 — which only nets JsonException — lets it escape. The consumer's Result-based order pipeline, contracted (VCR-04 spec item 5) to receive a classified error type it can map to its ambiguous-outcome leg, instead gets an untyped InvalidOperationException from a method that promises Result even with ThrowOnApiError=false; a generic exception handler that treats it as "reply failed" may resubmit an order whose confirmation may already have gone through, while the equivalent malformed body on the place/modify path (ResultFactory.cs:47-54) or a '['/'{'-prefixed malformed reply body (JsonException path) would have been classified.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Widen the catch in ReplyAsync to also convert InvalidOperationException from DeserializeReplyResponse into the same classified IbkrApiError(StatusCode.OK, ..., rawBody, requestPath) failure, or make DeserializeReplyResponse return a classification instead of throwing for the empty/non-JSON shapes.
- **Suggested regression test:** ReplyAsync_Empty2xxBody_ReturnsClassifiedErrorNotThrow — stub /iserver/reply returning 200 with '' (and a second case with 'plain text'), assert ReplyAsync returns Result failure of IbkrApiError with StatusCode OK carrying the raw body, and does not throw.

### PRB-2.1 — medium — SessionManager (question-suppression step in init/reauth)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:205` · lens: gap probe: Question-suppression flow: SuppressQuestionsAsync failure inside EnsureInitializedAsync/ReauthenticateAsync — an SES x ORD interaction nobody mentioned
- **Claim:** A non-2xx response from POST /iserver/questions/suppress escapes EnsureInitializedAsync/ReauthenticateAsync as a raw Refit.ApiException, unclassified by the library's exception taxonomy: the suppress try/catch catches only ApiRequestException (SessionManager.cs:205, :384), and in Refit 12 ApiException (thrown for an HTTP-error response on a raw Task<T> method) is a SIBLING of ApiRequestException under ApiExceptionBase, not a subclass — so it matches neither the inner catch nor any WrapCredentialException wrapping (which only covers the LST+ssodh block at :163-188/:334-360). Every other init failure is translated to IbkrConfigurationException/IbkrTransientException; a suppress HTTP error (or, via the deliberate RethrowOriginal at :209/:388, a raw HttpRequestException transport blip that WrapCredentialException would have classified IbkrTransientException at :670-673) surfaces raw to the consumer.
- **Consequence:** Because init is lazy inside OAuthSigningHandler.SendAsync (OAuthSigningHandler.cs:68) and consumer Result<T> operations rethrow send-failure inner exceptions via ThrowOnSendFailure (ResultFactory.cs:24), a consumer's PlaceOrderAsync/GetLiveOrdersAsync throws a raw Refit.ApiException whose request URI is /iserver/questions/suppress — a type nowhere in the library's documented surface. A transient IBKR 5xx on suppress during re-init on the order path crashes an RTOS-style pipeline keyed on the IbkrException taxonomy; a permanent trigger (unsupported message ID or >51 IDs — spec-pinned 'system error', docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:5079, unvalidated by ValidateOptions) wedges EVERY subsequent API call this way, with an extra ssodh/init round-trip per call since the server session is actually live but init keeps aborting at the suppress step.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:198-212 and :377-391 (catch (ApiRequestException) only); :163-188 and :646-698 (WrapCredentialException applied only to LST+ssodh, HttpRequestException→IbkrTransientException at :670-673); :252-257 (outer catch is bare rethrow); src/IbkrConduit/Errors/RefitResponseExtensions.cs:58-64 (RethrowOriginal); ~/.nuget/packages/refit/12.1.0 XML docs: ApiException ('after a response was received') and ApiRequestException ('before a response') are siblings under ApiExceptionBase; src/IbkrConduit/Serialization/IbkrRefitSettings.cs (default ExceptionFactory, so non-2xx raw Task<T> throws ApiException); src/IbkrConduit/Auth/OAuthSigningHandler.cs:68 (lazy init on every consumer call); src/IbkrConduit/Errors/ResultFactory.cs:24 (ThrowOnSendFailure rethrows to consumer); docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:5079 (>51 IDs / unsupported IDs → system error). Only the transport-wrapped leg is test-pinned (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Session/SessionManagerTests.cs:118-149); no test covers a non-2xx suppress response.
- **Skeptic verdict:** **PLAUSIBLE** — Code-side mechanism independently re-traced and holds end-to-end at HEAD 18c6a23. (1) SuppressQuestionsAsync is a raw Task<SuppressResponse> Refit method (src/IbkrConduit/Session/IIbkrSessionApi.cs:27-29) on the session pipeline (src/IbkrConduit/Http/SessionServiceRegistration.cs:75-100 — handlers are LastSuccessfulCall/GlobalRateLimit/EndpointRateLimit/OAuthSigning only; no TokenRefreshHandler, no error-classification handler). IbkrRefitSettings.Create() sets no ExceptionFactory (src/IbkrConduit/Serialization/IbkrRefitSettings.cs:32-37), so a non-2xx throws Refit's ApiException. (2) ApiException and ApiRequestException are siblings under ApiExceptionBase — pinned in-repo by the migration plan verified against Refit source (docs/superpowers/plans/2026-06-10-refit-11-migration.md:17,22-25: "ApiException : ApiExceptionBase", "ApiRequestException : ApiExceptionBase") and functionally by TickleTimer.cs:248-251, which type-tests ApiException{StatusCode:401} from this same pipeline as "distinct from the ApiRequestException that wraps transport faults". So the suppress catch blocks, `catch (ApiRequestException)` at SessionManager.cs:205 and :384, do NOT match a non-2xx ApiException. (3) No other classification exists: WrapCredentialException covers only the LST+ssodh try (SessionManager.cs:163-188 init, :334-360 reauth); the outer catch (:252-257 init, :423-430 reauth) only resets _state to Uninitialized and bare-rethrows. The raw ApiException escapes EnsureInitializedAsync/ReauthenticateAsync. (4) Consumer reachability confirmed: every consumer Refit client's OAuthSigningHandler is constructed with ISessionManager (ConsumerPipelineRegistration.cs:120-126) and calls EnsureInitializedAsync inside SendAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Auth/OAuthSigningHandler.cs:66-69); Refit 12 folds this handler-thrown exception into ApiResponse.Error as ApiRequestException(inner=ApiException), and ResultFactory.FromResponse's ThrowOnSendFailure (src/IbkrConduit/Errors/ResultFactory.cs:24 → RefitResponseExtensions.cs:24-30) rethrows the inner — so PlaceOrderAsync/GetLiveOrdersAsync throws a raw Refit.ApiException whose URI is /iserver/questions/suppress, a type outside the library taxonomy. (5) Wedge leg: SES-3 reset (:256/:428) means a persistently failing suppress aborts every subsequent init after a successful ssodh/init, adding an LST+ssodh round-trip per consumer call. ValidateOptions performs no SuppressMessageIds validation (src/IbkrConduit/Http/ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:178-227). (6) Test census: only the transport leg is pinned (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Session/SessionManagerTests.cs:117-149, deliberately rethrows the raw inner HttpRequestException as Refit-10 parity); no test covers a non-2xx suppress. No VCR story (PRs #238-#249) touched suppress classification — this is not a residual of a fixed story. WHY NOT CONFIRMED: the trigger requires IBKR to actually return a non-2xx HTTP status on POST /iserver/questions/suppress, and that is not pinned anywhere — recordings/ contains only success captures (recordings/oca|orderref|bulk|bracket/001-POST-iserver-questions-suppress.json), and the spec's ">51 IDs / unsupported IDs will result in a system error" (docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:5079) does not pin the HTTP representation. If that "system error" is a 200-with-error-body, this defect does not fire at all — SessionManager never inspects SuppressResponse.Status, so a body-level error passes silently (a different, quieter gap). Gating: only consumers with SuppressMessageIds configured (SessionManager.cs:198, :377) reach the suppress step at all.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** An RTOS-style consumer configures IbkrClientOptions.SuppressMessageIds (as the captured spec itself advises for automated order flows) and runs order operations. During a lazy init or post-401 re-auth, ssodh/init succeeds but IBKR answers POST /iserver/questions/suppress with a non-2xx (a transient gateway 5xx, a competing-session 401 in the init window, or — if IBKR renders it as non-2xx — the spec-pinned "system error" for an unsupported or >51-entry SuppressMessageIds list, which ValidateOptions never checks). Refit throws ApiException, which the suppress catch (ApiRequestException-only, SessionManager.cs:205/:384) cannot match and WrapCredentialException never sees, so the consumer's PlaceOrderAsync/GetLiveOrdersAsync call throws a raw Refit.ApiException pointing at /iserver/questions/suppress — a type outside the IbkrException/Result taxonomy the pipeline is keyed on, crashing or misrouting its error handling; and because SES-3 resets state to Uninitialized, a persistent suppress error makes every subsequent API call repeat LST+ssodh/init and fail the same way, wedging the client loudly despite a live server session. Trigger caveat: the non-2xx shape of a suppress failure is unverified live behavior (recordings hold only success captures); if IBKR's "system error" is instead a 200-with-error-body, SessionManager ignores it silently and this path never fires.
