- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Catch ApiExceptionBase (or Exception) around the suppress call and route through WrapCredentialException — or a dedicated classifier mapping suppress 4xx to IbkrConfigurationException("SuppressMessageIds") and 5xx/transport to IbkrTransientException — so suppress failures land in the same taxonomy as every other init failure. Optionally validate SuppressMessageIds (count <= 51, membership in SuppressibleMessages) in ValidateOptions to fail fast at registration.
- **Suggested regression test:** EnsureInitializedAsync_SuppressReturnsHttpError_ThrowsClassifiedIbkrException — stub SuppressQuestionsAsync to throw a Refit ApiException (503 and 400 cases); assert IbkrTransientException / IbkrConfigurationException respectively surfaces from EnsureInitializedAsync, never raw Refit.ApiException.

### PRB-2.2 — medium — SessionManager + session HTTP pipeline (suppress result verification)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:202` · lens: gap probe: Question-suppression flow: SuppressQuestionsAsync failure inside EnsureInitializedAsync/ReauthenticateAsync — an SES x ORD interaction nobody mentioned
- **Claim:** The SuppressResponse returned by both suppress calls is discarded (SessionManager.cs:202-203, :381-382) — Status is never compared to the spec-pinned "submitted" — and the internal session pipeline has no hidden-error net: no ResponseBodyCaptureHandler, no ResponseSchemaValidationHandler, no DetectHiddenError (SessionServiceRegistration.cs:70-100; DetectHiddenError lives only in the consumer-pipeline ResultFactory, ResultFactory.cs:127). A 200 body shaped {"error":"…"} — the exact IBKR failure shape DetectHiddenError exists for — deserializes to SuppressResponse(Status: null) (missing ctor param defaults to null; "error" lands in JsonExtensionData AdditionalData) and init proceeds to Ready with suppression silently unapplied.
- **Consequence:** Silent suppression no-op on the order-flow-blocking path: the design doc calls pre-suppression 'the primary defense' against the question/reply round-trip (docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md:591). An unattended consumer whose suppression silently failed gets an unexpected OrderConfirmationRequired on order placement, stalling automated submission until reply logic (with its own hazards — reply IDs expire quickly and are invalidated by subsequent submissions, ORD-3) kicks in, with zero observability that suppression was the cause.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:202-203 and :381-382 (await result discarded); src/IbkrConduit/Session/IIbkrSessionApiModels.cs:132-140 (SuppressResponse.Status, JsonExtensionData); src/IbkrConduit/Http/SessionServiceRegistration.cs:70-100 (session pipeline = LastSuccessfulCallHandler → rate limiters → OAuthSigningHandler only); src/IbkrConduit/Errors/ResultFactory.cs:127-166 (DetectHiddenError consumer-pipeline-only); docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:5090-5100 (response = {"status":"submitted"}, 'Verifies that the request has been sent'); docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md:591. No recording in recordings/ pins a failure-shaped 200 on this endpoint.
- **Skeptic verdict:** **PLAUSIBLE** — Code-side mechanism verified end-to-end at HEAD. (1) SessionManager.cs:198-212 (EnsureInitializedAsync) and :377-391 (ReauthenticateAsync) await SuppressQuestionsAsync and discard the SuppressResponse; Status is never inspected before the health update (:216-220, :396-400) and the Ready transition (:237, :410). Only thrown exceptions are handled (:205-211, :384-390). (2) SuppressResponse (src/IbkrConduit/Session/IIbkrSessionApiModels.cs:132-140) is a positional record with no required/[JsonRequired] on Status, so a 200 body {"error":"..."} deserializes to Status=null with the error text absorbed into the [JsonExtensionData] AdditionalData dictionary — no deserialization failure. (3) The internal session pipeline (src/IbkrConduit/Http/SessionServiceRegistration.cs:75-100) is LastSuccessfulCallHandler -> GlobalRateLimitingHandler -> EndpointRateLimitingHandler -> OAuthSigningHandler with no ResponseBodyCaptureHandler and no ResponseSchemaValidationHandler; those are wired only into the consumer pipeline (src/IbkrConduit/Http/ConsumerPipelineRegistration.cs:95, :105-108), and DetectHiddenError exists only in the consumer-pipeline ResultFactory (src/IbkrConduit/Errors/ResultFactory.cs:127-166). IbkrRefitSettings.Create (src/IbkrConduit/Serialization/IbkrRefitSettings.cs:32-37) adds no ExceptionFactory. VCR-10 (#248) hardened only the consumer pipeline; grep of docs/findings/2026-07-04-rtos-venue-consumer-review.md and docs/backlog.md shows no VCR story covering suppress-result verification, so nothing at HEAD fixes this. (4) Reachable from the public surface: AddIbkrClient with opts.SuppressMessageIds populated (src/IbkrConduit/Session/IbkrClientOptions.cs:28; opt-in, default empty — but the design doc docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md:591 calls pre-suppression 'the primary defense' and real captures show 11 IDs suppressed); any consumer call drives EnsureInitializedAsync. Counter-evidence that BOUNDS the finding: non-2xx suppress failures fail init loudly (the exception propagates through the catch at SessionManager.cs:252-258 / :423-430; SessionManagerTests.cs:1561-1614 pins the throwing path), so the silent path is exclusively a 2xx with a non-success body. That trigger is NOT pinned: all four recordings (e.g. recordings/oca/001-POST-iserver-questions-suppress.json) capture only 200 {"status":"submitted"}, and docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:5090-5100 pins only the success shape; :5079 documents failure modes (>51 messageIds -> 'system error', unsupported IDs rejected) but not whether they arrive as non-2xx or as a 200 error body. The hidden-error-in-200 pattern is real for IBKR (it is why DetectHiddenError exists for the consumer pipeline) but is unverified on this specific endpoint — hence PLAUSIBLE, not CONFIRMED. Severity re-judged medium: the consequence is not silent money/order loss — a failed suppression later surfaces as visible question objects in the order-submission response — but it is an observability gap that hides a session-setup integrity failure (zero log/health/error signal attributing the stall to suppression), on the order-flow path where reply IDs expire quickly (design doc :591), matching the rubric's medium ('robustness or observability gap that hides one of the above').
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer configures IbkrClientOptions.SuppressMessageIds (as the design doc recommends and real recordings show, e.g. 11 order-warning IDs) and calls any IIbkrClient operation, driving SessionManager.EnsureInitializedAsync. IBKR answers POST /iserver/questions/suppress with HTTP 200 whose body is a failure shape — e.g. {"error":"..."} (the hidden-error shape IBKR uses elsewhere) or any body lacking status:"submitted", such as from an unsupported message ID or the documented >51-ID 'system error' if delivered as a 200. SuppressResponse deserializes tolerantly (Status=null, error text swallowed into AdditionalData), SessionManager.cs:202/:381 discards the result without checking Status, no handler in the session pipeline inspects the body, and init proceeds to Ready with suppression silently unapplied and health reported clean. The first automated order placement then unexpectedly returns question objects instead of a confirmation, stalling unattended submission into the question/reply round-trip (whose reply IDs expire quickly), with no log line, health signal, or error anywhere attributing the stall to the failed suppression. Trigger depends on IBKR returning a failure-shaped 200 on this endpoint, which no recording or captured-spec entry pins.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Check the returned SuppressResponse: treat Status == null or an AdditionalData 'error' key as a failed suppression (throw the same classified error as finding 1, or at minimum log a warning and expose it via SessionHealthState) instead of discarding the body.
- **Suggested regression test:** EnsureInitializedAsync_SuppressStatusMissing_SurfacesFailure — stub SuppressQuestionsAsync returning SuppressResponse(Status: null) with an 'error' extension-data entry; assert init does not silently reach Ready (throws or records a health/log signal).

### PRB-3.2 — medium — AccountLedgerUpdateMapper / AccountSummaryUpdateMapper (ssd/sld row deserialization)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/AccountLedgerUpdateMapper.cs:20` · lens: gap probe: ssd/sld account-money streaming mappers (AccountSummaryUpdateMapper, AccountLedgerUpdateMapper) — money-carrying mappers absent from every WIR clean note
- **Claim:** Residual of the WIR-1 / VCR-03 (FIL-2) defect class in the two account-money mappers: AccountLedgerUpdateMapper.Map deserializes each result row with no per-element isolation (AccountLedgerUpdateMapper.cs:18-25), and AccountSummaryUpdateMapper.Map is identical (AccountSummaryUpdateMapper.cs:18-25), so one row that throws (a decimal-mapped field arriving as an object/array/bool on wire drift, an unparseable non-empty string — EmptyTolerantNumberParsing throws JsonException at EmptyTolerantNumberConverters.cs:264-267 — or a value overflowing decimal via the exponent notation the reference doc pins at docs/ibkr-websocket-api-reference.md:93) escapes Map and is caught at the frame level by ChannelObservable (ChannelObservable.cs:56-66), dropping the WHOLE frame. Because every 10-second sld frame re-carries the full currency set (docs/ibkr-websocket-api-reference.md:93-95), a persistently malformed row on ONE exotic currency drops EVERY subsequent ledger frame — the consumer's entire cash-balance stream goes permanently stale, not just one row or one frame's tail. VCR-03 fixed this for str (TradeExecutionMapper.cs:39-54) and WIR-1 re-found it for sor/spl; ssd/sld were left out of both.
- **Consequence:** The consumer's cash-balance/summary truth silently stops updating for ALL currencies and keys while the subscription stays alive and appears healthy; the loss is observable only via the drops metric/log (cause=mapper), not on the stream itself, so an RTOS-style consumer trades against a frozen balance snapshot.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/AccountLedgerUpdateMapper.cs:18-25 (no try/catch per element); src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/AccountSummaryUpdateMapper.cs:18-25 (same); src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/ChannelObservable.cs:56-66 (whole-frame drop on mapper throw); src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/TradeExecutionMapper.cs:39-54 (the per-element isolation pattern applied only to str); src/IbkrConduit/Serialization/EmptyTolerantNumberConverters.cs:258-268 (JsonException on unparseable non-empty string); docs/ibkr-websocket-api-reference.md:93-95 (10s full-currency-set cadence that makes a bad row persistent).
- **Skeptic verdict:** **PLAUSIBLE** — Traced end-to-end from the public surface. (1) Reachability: IIbkrClient streaming ops expose AccountSummaryAsync and AccountLedgerAsync, which wire AccountSummaryUpdateMapper.Map / AccountLedgerUpdateMapper.Map into ChannelObservable for the ssd/sld topics (src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:165-192, specifically :176 and :191). (2) No per-element isolation: both mappers eagerly loop `result` and call element.Deserialize<Row>(StreamingSerialization.Options) with no per-row try/catch (AccountLedgerUpdateMapper.cs:18-25, AccountSummaryUpdateMapper.cs:18-25); because the row list is built eagerly before the update record is returned, one throwing row aborts Map entirely — ALL rows in that frame are lost, not just the tail. (3) Frame-level drop: ChannelObservable.PumpAsync catches the mapper throw, records a drop (cause=mapper), logs, and continues (ChannelObservable.cs:56-66) — the subscription stays alive but the whole frame is discarded. (4) Throw paths are real: EmptyTolerantNullableDecimalConverter throws JsonException on any token other than Number/String/Null (EmptyTolerantNumberConverters.cs:41-48) and EmptyTolerantNumberParsing.Parse rethrows FormatException/OverflowException as JsonException for unparseable non-empty strings (EmptyTolerantNumberConverters.cs:258-268); plain string properties (Key, Currency, AccountCode — StreamingModels.cs:151-156, 192-205) throw per STJ defaults on non-string tokens. (5) VCR-03 scope: the backlog entry (docs/backlog.md:85-92) and TradeExecutionMapper.cs:39-54 confirm per-element isolation was applied ONLY to str (FIL-2) plus sts tolerant-bool; ssd/sld (and also sor/spl — OrderUpdateMapper.cs:29-44, PnlUpdateMapper.cs:24-31) were never in scope, so this is a genuine residual of the FIL-2 defect class, not something #247 fixed. (6) No refuting test: AccountLedgerUpdateMapperTests.cs and AccountSummaryUpdateMapperTests.cs cover only well-formed frames, blank rows, missing result — no malformed-row case. (7) Cadence: docs/ibkr-websocket-api-reference.md (sld section, ~line 95) pins a message every 10 seconds with one entry per currency, blank (key+timestamp only) when unchanged — so a persistently malformed, actively-changing row recurs and drops every data-bearing frame. HOWEVER, the trigger itself is not pinned: the converters already tolerate every quirk the captured spec and recordings document — empty strings, quoted numbers, exponent notation (decimal.Parse with NumberStyles.Float and Utf8JsonReader.GetDecimal both accept the E-component the reference doc pins, and real balance magnitudes like 2.02E8 are far below decimal's ~7.9E28 range). The live recording in the mapper test (AccountLedgerUpdateMapperTests.cs:12-14) shows a fully well-formed row. A row that actually throws requires wire drift (bool/object/array on a decimal field, or a genuinely garbage string like "N/A") that no recording or spec entry evidences. Per the verdict rules that makes this PLAUSIBLE, not CONFIRMED. Severity re-judged: the loss is not silent at the library boundary — every dropped frame is counted (cause=mapper) and warn-logged (ChannelObservable.cs:64-65) per the deliberate drop-taxonomy design — but the stream itself gives no signal, so a consumer not watching the drops metric trades on a frozen balance snapshot. That is a robustness/observability gap that can hide staleness (frozen data, not wrongly-valued data), with a speculative trigger: medium.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer subscribes via IIbkrClient.Streaming.AccountLedgerAsync (or AccountSummaryAsync). IBKR's sld frame re-carries one row per currency every 10 seconds. If wire drift ever makes one row's decimal-mapped field un-deserializable — a bool/object/array token, or a non-empty unparseable string such as "N/A" on an exotic currency's exchangeRate (no such shape is pinned by the captured spec or recordings; the converters already tolerate empty strings, quoted numbers, and exponent notation) — then AccountLedgerUpdateMapper.Map (AccountLedgerUpdateMapper.cs:18-25), which has no per-row try/catch and builds its row list eagerly, throws out of the whole frame, and ChannelObservable (ChannelObservable.cs:56-66) drops the ENTIRE frame — every currency's balances, not just the bad row — while keeping the subscription alive. Because the malformed row recurs on every data-bearing frame, the consumer's whole cash-balance/summary stream freezes indefinitely; the only signal is the drops metric (cause=mapper) and a warning log, nothing on the stream itself, so an RTOS-style consumer that doesn't alert on that metric trades against a stale balance snapshot. This is the same FIL-2 defect class VCR-03 (#247) fixed for str only — its backlog scope (docs/backlog.md:85-92) never included ssd/sld (nor sor/spl).
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Apply the FIL-2 pattern to both mappers: wrap each element.Deserialize in try/catch, skip and report the bad row through the same onElementDropped hook StreamingOperations wires for str (StreamingOperations.cs:226-230), delivering the frame's surviving rows. Note this extends the already-reported WIR-1 (sor/spl) to the ssd/sld account-money mappers, which WIR-1 did not name.
- **Suggested regression test:** AccountLedgerUpdateMapper_OneMalformedRow_DeliversRemainingRows — frame with three ledger rows where the middle row has {"cashbalance":{}}; assert two rows delivered, one drop reported, exception not propagated (mirror test for AccountSummaryUpdateMapper).

### PRB-3.3 — medium — AccountSummaryRow (ssd streaming DTO)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/StreamingModels.cs:151` · lens: gap probe: ssd/sld account-money streaming mappers (AccountSummaryUpdateMapper, AccountLedgerUpdateMapper) — money-carrying mappers absent from every WIR clean note
- **Claim:** AccountSummaryRow maps only key/currency/monetaryValue/severity/timestamp (StreamingModels.cs:151-156) and — unlike AccountLedgerRow, which has a [JsonExtensionData] escape hatch (StreamingModels.cs:208-209) — has no `value` property and no JsonExtensionData, while the repo's own WebSocket reference pins a sixth documented row field: `value`: String, "A non-monetary value (dates, account titles, etc.)" returned for non-monetary keys (docs/ibkr-websocket-api-reference.md:50, example row at line 58). System.Text.Json silently ignores the unmapped property, so for any non-monetary summary key the row arrives as Key+Timestamp(+Severity) only, indistinguishable from IBKR having sent nothing — the interface even invites consumers to filter fields, listing only "currency","monetaryValue" as examples (IStreamingOperations.cs:127).
- **Consequence:** A consumer subscribing to ssd with default (all) keys or non-monetary keys silently loses every documented non-monetary summary value with no way to recover it (no AdditionalData), and cannot distinguish 'absent from this frame' from 'unmapped by the library' — violating the nullability-as-presence contract the streaming DTOs otherwise follow.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/StreamingModels.cs:150-156 (AccountSummaryRow: five properties, no value, no extension data) vs StreamingModels.cs:191-210 (AccountLedgerRow has AdditionalData); docs/ibkr-websocket-api-reference.md:47-60 (documented `value` field and example row carrying it); tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Streaming/AccountSummaryUpdateMapperTests.cs:12-14 (the only captured ssd frame is a monetary row — the value shape is documented, not recorded).
- **Skeptic verdict:** **PLAUSIBLE** — Traced end-to-end from the public surface: IIbkrClient.Streaming (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IIbkrClient.cs:33) -> IStreamingOperations.AccountSummaryAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IStreamingOperations.cs:130-134; line 127 documents the fields filter with only "currency"/"monetaryValue" as examples) -> StreamingOperations.AccountSummaryAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:165-177) subscribes ssd and maps frames via AccountSummaryUpdateMapper.Map (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/AccountSummaryUpdateMapper.cs:13-29), which deserializes each result element to AccountSummaryRow using StreamingSerialization.Options. AccountSummaryRow (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/StreamingModels.cs:151-156) maps exactly key/currency/monetaryValue/severity/timestamp — no `value` property and no [JsonExtensionData] — while the sibling AccountLedgerRow (StreamingModels.cs:192-210) does carry an AdditionalData extension-data bag. StreamingSerialization.Options (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/StreamingSerialization.cs:17-31) configures only case-insensitivity and empty-tolerant numeric converters, so System.Text.Json's default UnmappedMemberHandling silently discards the documented sixth field `value` (docs/ibkr-websocket-api-reference.md:50, example row carrying it at line 58). Nothing merged in VCR-01..11 touches this: VCR-10's money-field census is str/sor-only (docs/backlog.md:145) and ResponseSchemaValidationHandler is an HTTP DelegatingHandler that never sees WebSocket frames; the prior venue-consumer review has no ssd findings (grep of docs/findings/ found none). The only pinned ssd frame anywhere is a monetary row (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Streaming/AccountSummaryUpdateMapperTests.cs:12-14). Code-side mechanism is therefore fully confirmed; however docs/ibkr-websocket-api-reference.md is a verbatim transcription of IBKR's documentation (provenance header, lines 3-7), not a wire capture, and no recordings/ entry contains an ssd frame at all — so whether IBKR actually emits `value` rows for non-monetary keys is documented but not recorded, which per the verdict rules caps this at PLAUSIBLE. Severity: medium — silently lost non-monetary summary data (dates, account titles, etc.) with no recovery path and a presence-contract violation, but no money numeric is corrupted, no order/fill loss, no session/liveness impact, and the data remains reachable via the REST account-summary endpoints.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer calls client.Streaming.AccountSummaryAsync("DU1234567") with no keys/fields filter (default: all keys, all fields). Per the repo's transcribed IBKR WebSocket reference (docs/ibkr-websocket-api-reference.md:47-58), non-monetary summary keys arrive as rows carrying a `value` string (dates, account titles, etc.) instead of monetaryValue/currency. AccountSummaryUpdateMapper deserializes each row into AccountSummaryRow (StreamingModels.cs:151-156), which has no `value` property and — unlike AccountLedgerRow (StreamingModels.cs:192-210) — no [JsonExtensionData] escape hatch; StreamingSerialization.Options sets no UnmappedMemberHandling, so System.Text.Json silently drops the field. The consumer receives a row containing only Key, Timestamp, and Severity with Currency and MonetaryValue null — bit-identical to a row where IBKR sent nothing — so every documented non-monetary summary value is unrecoverably lost and the consumer cannot distinguish "absent from this frame" from "unmapped by the library", violating the nullability-as-presence contract the streaming DTOs otherwise follow. The trigger depends on IBKR actually emitting `value` rows, which is pinned only by transcribed IBKR documentation, not by any recording or live-captured frame in the repo.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Add a `[JsonPropertyName("value")] string? Value` member to AccountSummaryRow and a [JsonExtensionData] AdditionalData dictionary matching AccountLedgerRow (additive, non-breaking); pin the non-monetary row shape with a live capture since no recording exists for ssd.
- **Suggested regression test:** AccountSummaryUpdateMapper_NonMonetaryRow_ValueIsMapped — frame row {"key":"AccountType","value":"INDIVIDUAL","severity":0,"timestamp":1700000000}; assert row.Value == "INDIVIDUAL" and unknown extra fields land in AdditionalData.

### RST-1 — medium — FlexResultParser.AttrDecimal (Flex money-field parsing)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Flex/FlexResultParser.cs:271` · lens: REST breadth & periphery
- **Claim:** AttrDecimal silently coerces any unparseable money attribute to 0m (decimal.TryParse ... ? d : 0m), and NumberStyles.Number+InvariantCulture misreads European-formatted values: for a Flex profile emitting decimal-comma numbers, "1.234,56" fails TryParse and becomes 0m, while grouped "1.234" (= 1234) parses successfully as 1.234m — a 1000x magnitude error with no error signal. This feeds every money field on FlexCashTransaction (Amount, FxRateToBase, FlexResultParser.cs:99,107), FlexTradeConfirmation (Quantity/Price/Amount/Proceeds/NetCash/Commission, lines 134-139), FlexSymbolSummary and FlexOrder, reachable via IIbkrClient.Flex.GetCashTransactionsAsync/GetTradeConfirmationsAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/FlexOperations.cs:79-110). The absent-attribute→0m case is deliberately pinned (FlexResultParserTests.cs:141), but the unparseable-value→0m/misparse case has no pin, no log, and no failure surface.
- **Consequence:** A consumer reconciling fills or cash movement from Flex (the design doc's designated cross-session reconciliation path, docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md §10) silently gets 0 or 1000x-wrong amounts/commissions for any account whose Flex number format is not US-invariant — corrupted money numerics with a success-shaped Result.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Flex/FlexResultParser.cs:271-272 (AttrDecimal returns 0m on TryParse failure, NumberStyles.Number allows thousands separators); :107 (Amount), :134-139 (trade money fields); non-nullable decimal DTO src/IbkrConduit/Flex/FlexCashTransaction.cs:19,43; reachability src/IbkrConduit/Client/FlexOperations.cs:89-91,107-109; only pin is absent→0m at tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Flex/FlexResultParserTests.cs:132-145 — no unparseable-value test exists.
- **Skeptic verdict:** **PLAUSIBLE** — Mechanism confirmed by direct read: src/IbkrConduit/Flex/FlexResultParser.cs:271-272 defines AttrDecimal as decimal.TryParse(value, NumberStyles.Number, InvariantCulture) ? d : 0m — any unparseable money attribute silently coerces to 0m with no log, no error, and no failure surface; NumberStyles.Number includes AllowThousands so European-grouped "1.234" parses as 1.234m (1000x) while "1.234,56" fails to 0m. It feeds every money field: FlexCashTransaction.FxRateToBase/Amount (FlexResultParser.cs:99,107), FlexTradeConfirmation Quantity/Price/Amount/Proceeds/NetCash/Commission (:134-139), FlexSymbolSummary (:160-165), FlexOrder (:186-191); DTO fields are non-nullable decimal (src/IbkrConduit/Flex/FlexCashTransaction.cs:19,43) so 0m is indistinguishable from a real zero. Reachability confirmed from the public surface: IIbkrClient.Flex.GetCashTransactionsAsync -> docResult.Map(FlexResultParser.ParseCashTransactions) (src/IbkrConduit/Client/FlexOperations.cs:89-91) and GetTradeConfirmationsAsync (:107-109), returning a success-shaped Result; no value-level validation net exists on the Flex XML path. The design doc confirms Flex is the designated cross-session reconciliation path (docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md:713-740). Not covered by the merged VCR-01..11 stream (docs/findings/2026-07-04-rtos-venue-consumer-review.md mentions Flex only in rate-limiter/registration findings), so this is not a re-report. However, the trigger is not pinned: docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md contains zero Flex content, recordings/ has no Flex captures, and the only wire evidence — real-capture fixtures tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Flex/Fixtures/*.xml — shows exclusively invariant period-decimal numerics with no thousands separators (amount="2331.45", price="583.313333333"). The absent-attribute->0m half is deliberately pinned behavior (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Flex/FlexResultParserTests.cs:132-145), and the design doc calls the Flex XML schema "well-defined and stable" (:830) while warning paper data "may be... formatted differently" (:986) — neither pins number locale. Whether any IBKR Flex profile/account ever emits decimal-comma, grouped, or otherwise-unparseable numerics in XML is live wire behavior not verified by anything in the repo, so CONFIRMED is not available; no counter-evidence refutes it either. Severity re-judged: the demonstrable defect at HEAD is a silent-coercion/observability gap (no error signal, no unparseable-value pin, non-nullable DTO erasing the distinction) that would hide a critical money-corruption outcome if the unpinned wire variance exists — medium per the rubric, escalating to critical only if the non-invariant trigger is ever demonstrated live.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer runs IIbkrClient.Flex.GetTradeConfirmationsAsync/GetCashTransactionsAsync as the design doc's designated cross-session fill/cash reconciliation path (docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md sec 10). If IBKR ever emits a Flex XML numeric attribute that InvariantCulture NumberStyles.Number cannot parse — e.g. a decimal-comma value like amount="1.234,56" from a non-US-formatted Flex profile, or any malformed/exotic token — AttrDecimal (FlexResultParser.cs:271-272) silently substitutes 0m into the non-nullable Amount/Price/Proceeds/NetCash/Commission fields, and a European-grouped "1.234" (meaning 1234) parses "successfully" as 1.234m, a 1000x magnitude error; the result arrives as a success-shaped Result with no log, error, or per-field presence signal, so the consumer reconciles fills and cash against zeroed or 1000x-wrong numbers. The code path is fully reachable and unguarded at HEAD, but the trigger rests on unpinned live IBKR Flex wire behavior: every captured fixture in the repo shows invariant period-decimal output, and no spec entry, recording, or test demonstrates IBKR ever emitting non-invariant or unparseable Flex numerics.
- **Corroboration:** ERR (medium variant, FlexResultParser.cs:272: same AttrDecimal TryParse-else-0m coercion across Amount/Price/Proceeds/NetCash/Commission/Quantity/FxRateToBase; adds the no-wire-anomaly trigger — a Flex query configured without a column emits elements lacking the attribute → authoritative-looking 0m — and the presence-contract context from docs/findings/2026-07-04-rtos-venue-consumer-review.md:496,572 with a nullable-decimal 📦 design-pass fix direction)
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Parse with an explicit strict style (no AllowThousands, or normalize per a documented expected format); on parse failure surface an error (nullable field + parse-failure signal, or fail the statement parse) instead of coercing to 0m. Pin the accepted wire number format against a real Flex recording.
- **Suggested regression test:** ParseCashTransactions_UnparseableAmount_SurfacesErrorNotZero — feed amount="1.234,56" and amount="abc" and assert the parse does not yield Amount==0m/1.234m silently.

### RST-3 — medium — FlexResultParser.ParseFlexDateTime (timezone handling)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Flex/FlexResultParser.cs:244` · lens: REST breadth & periphery
- **Claim:** The trailing-timezone fallback recognizes only 8 US abbreviations plus UTC/GMT (FlexResultParser.cs:244-255); any other suffix (CET, CEST, BST, HKT, JST, AEST — normal for non-US IBKR accounts, whose Flex timestamps carry the account's statement timezone like the pinned "EDT" example at :231) makes ParseFlexDateTime return null, silently nulling DateTime/OrderTime/whenGenerated on every row of an otherwise-successful statement. Separately, the bare "yyyyMMdd;HHmmss" branch (:222) applies DateTimeStyles.AssumeUniversal, stamping account-local Flex timestamps as UTC — a 4-9 hour offset error that shifts which trading day a fill/cash transaction appears to belong to.
- **Consequence:** A consumer on a non-US-timezone account loses all Flex timestamps (presence corrupted to null despite data being on the wire), and US-account bare timestamps are mislabeled UTC — both silently, corrupting time-based reconciliation (settle-date/trade-day bucketing) without any error.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Flex/FlexResultParser.cs:216-263 (whitelist switch :244-255 returns null for unknown tz; AssumeUniversal at :222,227); consumed at :61 (whenGenerated), :104,130-131,180 (row timestamps); only tz pin is "EDT" at tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Flex/FlexResultParserTests.cs:116 — no non-US-tz or bare-local-time recording exists.
- **Skeptic verdict:** **PLAUSIBLE** — Reachability confirmed from the public surface: IIbkrClient.Flex (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IIbkrClient.cs:38) -> FlexOperations maps FlexResultParser.ParseCashTransactions/ParseTradeConfirmations/ParseGeneric (src/IbkrConduit/Client/FlexOperations.cs:90,108,118,128) -> ParseFlexDateTime populates whenGenerated (src/IbkrConduit/Flex/FlexResultParser.cs:61,91), CashTransaction.DateTime (:104), TradeConfirmation.OrderTime/DateTime (:130-131), FlexOrder.OrderTime (:180). Mechanism confirmed in code: (1) the trailing-tz fallback whitelist at FlexResultParser.cs:244-255 maps only EDT/EST/CDT/CST/MDT/MST/PDT/PST/UTC/GMT; any other suffix hits `_ => (TimeSpan?)null` and the method returns null at :262 with no logging — the general DateTimeOffset.TryParse at :233 cannot rescue named abbreviations (.NET does not parse them; the code's own comment at :237 says so), so CET/CEST/BST/HKT/JST/AEST all yield null silently while the statement parses as a success. (2) The bare "yyyyMMdd;HHmmss" and "yyyyMMdd" branches at :222 and :227 use DateTimeStyles.AssumeUniversal, stamping the value as UTC regardless of its actual timezone. Tests pin neither behavior: tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Flex/FlexResultParserTests.cs:101-111 (compact) and :113-121 (EDT) assert only Y/M/D/H/M/S components, never the offset; the only tz exercised is EDT. However, the trigger depends on live IBKR wire behavior that is NOT pinned in this repo: docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md contains zero Flex entries (grep for whenGenerated/dateTime/flex returns nothing), recordings/ has no Flex captures (only bracket/bulk/cleanup/oca/orderref/orders/priming), and the parser landed in a single commit (c003ff7) untouched by the VCR fix stream. Whether non-US accounts receive non-US tz abbreviations, and whether bare compact timestamps are account-local rather than UTC, is upstream behavior with no recording or spec pin — so per the verdict rules this is PLAUSIBLE, not CONFIRMED. Consequence is narrower than the sweep claimed: day-bucketing fields TradeDate/SettleDate/ReportDate go through ParseFlexDate (DateOnly, :198-213) and are unaffected, as are all money numerics; the corruption is confined to the nullable DateTimeOffset timestamp fields. Severity re-judged medium: it is a silent data gap/mislabel on a reporting-reconciliation surface (design doc §11 names the Trade Confirmation flex query as the cross-session fill-history mechanism), but the authoritative day-bucketing dates and amounts survive intact, so it is an observability/robustness gap that can hide or skew time-based reconciliation rather than directly losing fills or money numerics.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer on an IBKR account whose Flex statement timezone is non-US (e.g. Europe/London or Asia/Hong_Kong) calls IIbkrClient.Flex.GetTradeConfirmationsAsync or GetCashTransactionsAsync; IBKR returns timestamps in the pinned-style format "2026-04-09;21:23:54 BST" (same shape as the captured "EDT" example, different abbreviation). The general parse at FlexResultParser.cs:233 fails (.NET does not parse named tz abbreviations), the whitelist at :244-255 has no entry for BST, and ParseFlexDateTime returns null at :262 — so every row's DateTime/OrderTime and the statement's WhenGenerated come back null despite the data being on the wire, with no error, log, or diagnostic; the statement otherwise reports success. Separately, if the account's Flex query is configured for the compact "yyyyMMdd;HHmmss" format, the branch at :222 stamps the account-local timestamp as UTC via AssumeUniversal, producing DateTimeOffsets that are off by the account's UTC offset (4-9+ hours), silently shifting intraday fill timestamps across day boundaries for consumers that bucket by the timestamp instead of the (unaffected) TradeDate/SettleDate DateOnly fields. Both failure modes are deterministic in the code, but no Flex recording or spec entry pins that IBKR actually emits non-US abbreviations or account-local compact timestamps, so the trigger rests on unverified live wire behavior.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Preserve the raw timestamp string on the DTO (or parse to a DateTime with explicit Unspecified kind) instead of guessing offsets; extend the abbreviation table or use the query's configured tz; never return null when the attribute is present — surface unparsed raw text.
- **Suggested regression test:** ParseFlexDateTime_NonUsTimezoneSuffix_DoesNotDropTimestamp — "2026-04-09;21:23:54 CET" must not yield null (and bare "20260409;212354" must not be asserted UTC without a pinned recording).

### AUT-3 — low — SessionTokenProvider (refresh dedupe vs lazy acquisition)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Auth/SessionTokenProvider.cs:74` · lens: OAuth signing & credential hygiene
- **Claim:** GetLiveSessionTokenAsync acquires a fresh LST under the semaphore (line 74) without incrementing _version, while RefreshAsync's dedupe (lines 90-102) only detects refreshes that bumped _version. At the LST expiry boundary, a Get-triggered acquisition (request A sees the cached token expired) and a 401-triggered RefreshAsync (request B signed just before expiry) serialize on the semaphore, and the refresh proceeds to perform a second back-to-back LST handshake even though A just installed a brand-new token.
- **Consequence:** A redundant full DH/LST handshake per expiry-boundary race; if IBKR invalidates the previous LST on new issuance (unpinned), in-flight requests signed with the seconds-old token 401 and trigger further refresh/retry churn.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Auth/SessionTokenProvider.cs:64-75 (Get path acquires without touching _version), src/IbkrConduit/Auth/SessionTokenProvider.cs:90-102 (RefreshAsync dedupe keyed solely on _version), src/IbkrConduit/Session/TokenRefreshHandler.cs:98 → SessionManager.cs:337 (401 path reaching RefreshAsync concurrently with signing-path Get)
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Independently traced from the public surface. (1) Every signed request reaches SessionTokenProvider.GetLiveSessionTokenAsync via OAuthSigningHandler.SendAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Auth/OAuthSigningHandler.cs:71), registered in the AddIbkrClient handler pipeline. (2) The Get path treats an expired cached token as a miss (src/IbkrConduit/Auth/SessionTokenProvider.cs:57-62, expiry check via IsExpired at :83), acquires the semaphore, re-checks (:67-71), and installs a fresh LST at :74 WITHOUT incrementing _version — confirmed by reading the whole file: _version is only touched in RefreshAsync (:102) and read at :90/:96. (3) The 401 path is TokenRefreshHandler.SendAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Session/TokenRefreshHandler.cs:98) → SessionManager.ReauthenticateAsync → _sessionTokenProvider.RefreshAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:337). RefreshAsync's dedupe (SessionTokenProvider.cs:90-102) snapshots _version before the semaphore wait and skips the handshake only if _version advanced — a Get-path acquisition that completed while the refresher waited leaves _version unchanged, so the refresher performs a second full DH/LST handshake back-to-back with the one the Get path just finished. SessionManager's own _reauthEpoch dedupe (SessionManager.cs:277-303) does not help: the Get path never touches _reauthEpoch either. Tests pin only refresh-vs-refresh dedupe (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Session/SessionTokenProviderRefreshTests.cs:60-93, RefreshAsync_ConcurrentCalls_OnlyRefreshesOnce); no test covers the Get-vs-Refresh interleave, so nothing pins the current behavior as intended. Mitigating context that caps severity: (a) the proactive-refresh scheduler (SessionManager.cs:548-576, RunProactiveRefreshAsync :578-629 with SES-5 retry backoff) re-auths ProactiveRefreshMargin before expiry, so the client-side expiry boundary is only reached after proactive refresh has failed for the entire margin window (e.g., a connectivity outage spanning expiry) — the trigger is concrete but rare; (b) even with a perfect dedupe, ReauthenticateAsync would still run ssodh/init (SessionManager.cs:339-340), so the only duplicated work is the one extra LST handshake round-trip; (c) the second handshake produces another valid token installed under the same semaphore — no corruption, no wrong data; (d) the escalated consequence (IBKR invalidating the previous LST on new issuance → 401 churn on in-flight requests) is NOT pinned: docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md contains no LST-issuance/invalidation semantics (grep for live-session-token/invalidate found only portfolio-cache and order-reply entries), and even under that hypothesis non-order in-flight 401s self-heal through TokenRefreshHandler's retry, while the same invalidation window already exists for the first (legitimate) handshake. Net: real dedupe gap, reachable, concrete trigger, but the verified consequence is one redundant handshake in a rare race — a nit by the rubric.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** The proactive LST refresh has been failing (e.g., network outage) long enough for the cached LST to reach its client-computed expiry. Connectivity returns: request B, signed seconds earlier with the now-dead LST, comes back 401 and enters TokenRefreshHandler → SessionManager.ReauthenticateAsync → SessionTokenProvider.RefreshAsync, snapshotting _version before waiting on the provider semaphore; concurrently request A signs, sees the cached token expired in GetLiveSessionTokenAsync, takes the semaphore first, and installs a brand-new LST at SessionTokenProvider.cs:74 without bumping _version. B then acquires the semaphore, finds _version unchanged (dedupe at :96 misses), and performs a second full DH/LST handshake immediately after A's — one redundant OAuth live_session_token round-trip per such race. ReauthenticateAsync proceeds to ssodh/init either way, so functional recovery is unaffected; the further escalation (old-LST invalidation on reissue causing 401 churn on in-flight requests) depends on unpinned IBKR wire behavior and, where it applies to non-order requests, self-heals via the 401 retry.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Increment _version inside GetLiveSessionTokenAsync's acquisition branch (or dedupe RefreshAsync on the cached token instance/expiry changing) so any acquisition completed while a refresher waited satisfies the refresh.
- **Suggested regression test:** RefreshAsync_QueuedBehindExpiredGetAcquisition_DoesNotAcquireTwice — fake LST client with a delay; expire the cached token, start Get and Refresh concurrently, assert client.CallCount == 1 after both complete.

### AUT-4 — low — SessionManager.WrapCredentialException (LST-validation misclassification)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:655` · lens: OAuth signing & credential hygiene
- **Claim:** LiveSessionTokenClient throws CryptographicException("Live Session Token validation failed: computed signature does not match server's signature.") when the derived LST fails HMAC validation (LiveSessionTokenClient.cs:101-102); WrapCredentialException's substring routing matches Contains("sign") on the word 'signature' and reports "RSA signature failed — verify SignaturePrivateKey" (SessionManager.cs:655-658). A wrong SignaturePrivateKey cannot produce this failure (the server rejects the LST request with 401 before responding); the real causes are a wrong ConsumerKey, DhPrime, or EncryptionPrivateKey/access-token-secret mismatch.
- **Consequence:** During credential setup or rotation, the operator of a trading system is directed to verify the one credential that is provably not at fault, hiding the true configuration error and prolonging an auth outage.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Auth/LiveSessionTokenClient.cs:98-103 (validation-failure CryptographicException message contains 'signature'), src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:650-663 (Contains("decrypt")/Contains("sign") substring routing; the 'sign' case fires first for this message), src/IbkrConduit/Auth/OAuthCrypto.cs:94-101 (what validation actually checks: HMAC(lst, consumerKey))
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced end-to-end at HEAD (18c6a23). (1) Trigger site: LiveSessionTokenClient.GetLiveSessionTokenAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Auth/LiveSessionTokenClient.cs:98-103) throws CryptographicException("Live Session Token validation failed: computed signature does not match server's signature.") when OAuthCrypto.ValidateLiveSessionToken fails; that validation is HMAC-SHA1(key=derived LST, data=UTF8(consumerKey)) compared to the server's live_session_token_signature (src/IbkrConduit/Auth/OAuthCrypto.cs:94-101). (2) Propagation: SessionManager.InitializeAsync calls _sessionTokenProvider.GetLiveSessionTokenAsync at src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:165 inside try/catch; the CryptographicException hits the generic catch at SessionManager.cs:185-187 and is passed to WrapCredentialException (same pattern in the re-auth path at SessionManager.cs:353-359). This path is reachable from the public surface: every consumer call goes through session initialization (AddIbkrClient DI -> EnsureInitializedAsync -> InitializeAsync). (3) Misrouting: WrapCredentialException's switch (SessionManager.cs:647-663) checks Contains("decrypt") first (no match — the message has no 'decrypt'), then Contains("sign", OrdinalIgnoreCase) at SessionManager.cs:655 — 'signature' matches — producing IbkrConfigurationException("RSA signature failed — verify SignaturePrivateKey matches the key registered in the IBKR portal", "SignaturePrivateKey", ce). (4) Wrong-credential attribution: SignaturePrivateKey is provably not the cause on this path — the LST request itself is RSA-SHA256-signed with SignaturePrivateKey (LiveSessionTokenClient.cs:54,66-67), and a signature the server rejects yields a non-2xx that EnsureSuccessStatusCode (LiveSessionTokenClient.cs:78) converts to HttpRequestException, which routes to the 401/403 ConsumerKey/AccessToken branch (SessionManager.cs:665-668) instead. Reaching step 10 validation requires a 200, i.e., an accepted RSA signature. Local RSA signing with a wrong-but-well-formed key also does not throw. The real causes of validation failure are a DhPrime mismatch (server computes its DH response under the prime registered in the portal and cannot detect a client-side mismatch, so it returns 200 with a signature over a different LST), a ConsumerKey mismatch in the HMAC data, or a garbled decrypted access-token secret. (5) Tests do not pin the message routing: tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Session/SessionManagerWrapCredentialExceptionTests.cs:102-110 only asserts a CryptographicException containing 'sign' becomes IbkrConfigurationException — it never asserts parameter-name accuracy for the LST-validation message, so no pin contradicts the finding. Severity: this is a wrong diagnostic hint, not a data/order/session-integrity defect. The exception is still correctly classified as a non-transient IbkrConfigurationException (correct retry semantics), and the original CryptographicException with the accurate 'Live Session Token validation failed' text is preserved as InnerException (SessionManager.cs:656-658 passes ce as inner), so the true cause remains visible in any logged exception chain. It prolongs operator diagnosis during setup/rotation but hides nothing from full logs — low per the rubric.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** An operator rotates credentials and misconfigures DhPrime (e.g., keeps the old prime after re-registering in the IBKR portal) or ConsumerKey while SignaturePrivateKey is correct. The first IIbkrClient call triggers session init; the LST request is accepted (200 — the server cannot detect a DH-prime mismatch and signs an LST derived under its own registered prime), the client derives a different LST, ValidateLiveSessionToken fails, and LiveSessionTokenClient throws CryptographicException("Live Session Token validation failed: computed signature does not match server's signature."). WrapCredentialException's Contains("sign") substring match (SessionManager.cs:655) fires on the word 'signature' and surfaces IbkrConfigurationException("RSA signature failed — verify SignaturePrivateKey...", parameter "SignaturePrivateKey") — directing the operator to the one credential the successful signed request just proved correct, and away from the actual DhPrime/ConsumerKey misconfiguration, prolonging the auth outage; the accurate root-cause text survives only in the InnerException.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Throw a distinct exception type (or a sentinel message checked before the 'sign' case) from the LST-validation failure and map it to "verify ConsumerKey / DhPrime / EncryptionPrivateKey" guidance.
- **Suggested regression test:** WrapCredentialException_LstValidationFailure_PointsAtConsumerKeyOrDhPrime — pass the exact CryptographicException LiveSessionTokenClient throws and assert the resulting IbkrConfigurationException does not name SignaturePrivateKey.

### AUT-5 — low — OAuthCredentialsFactory (TenantId defaults to ConsumerKey)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Auth/OAuthCredentialsFactory.cs:69` · lens: OAuth signing & credential hygiene
- **Claim:** FromJson unconditionally sets TenantId = consumerKey (line 69, no tenantId field is read and no override parameter exists), and FromEnvironment falls back to consumerKey when IBKR_TENANT_ID is unset (line 29). TenantId is then stamped as a tag on every log scope, metric, and Activity span (e.g. OAuthSigningHandler.cs:62,93; SessionManager.cs:132,145), so the IBKR consumer key — enumerated as credential material in the design doc's credential table — propagates verbatim into third-party observability backends for consumers using the documented factory path.
- **Consequence:** Consumer-key identifier leaks into logs/metrics/traces exported off-host, weakening the repo's log-sanitization guarantee (a partial credential-hygiene gap, though the consumer key alone cannot authenticate).
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Auth/OAuthCredentialsFactory.cs:29 (env fallback), src/IbkrConduit/Auth/OAuthCredentialsFactory.cs:68-70 (FromJson: TenantId = consumerKey with no alternative), src/IbkrConduit/Auth/OAuthSigningHandler.cs:62,93 (TenantId emitted on spans and metric tags), src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:132 (TenantId in every session log scope)
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Mechanism independently re-traced end-to-end. (1) Factory defaults: src/IbkrConduit/Auth/OAuthCredentialsFactory.cs:29 — FromEnvironment sets `tenantId = Environment.GetEnvironmentVariable("IBKR_TENANT_ID") ?? consumerKey`; OAuthCredentialsFactory.cs:68-70 — FromJson constructs `new IbkrOAuthCredentials(consumerKey, consumerKey, ...)`, i.e. TenantId = ConsumerKey unconditionally; no tenantId JSON field is read (lines 56-61 read only consumerKey/accessToken/accessTokenSecret/signaturePrivateKey/encryptionPrivateKey/dhPrime) and no override parameter exists; FromFile (line 80-88) delegates to FromJson. (2) Propagation: src/IbkrConduit/Http/ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:113 registers `TenantContext(credentials.TenantId)` for the single-tenant AddIbkrClient path; that TenantId is then stamped as `ibkr.tenant_id` (LogFields.cs:12) on every consumer-request span and metric tag (OAuthSigningHandler.cs:62 `activity?.SetTag(LogFields.TenantId, ...)`, :93 metric tag), on every rate-limiter log scope (GlobalRateLimitingHandler.cs:52, EndpointRateLimitingHandler.cs:56), tickle-loop scopes (TickleTimer.cs:93), and operations spans (OrderOperations.cs:62 etc.). So a consumer using the documented FromFile/FromJson path exports the 9-character IBKR consumer key verbatim to whatever OTEL/logging backend they wire up. Counter-evidence found that caps severity but does not kill the finding: (a) the env-path default is documented and deliberate — README.md:275 "IBKR_TENANT_ID | No | Tenant identifier (defaults to consumer key)"; (b) the multi-tenant path is unaffected — IbkrClientManager.cs:50 unconditionally normalizes `credentials with { TenantId = tenantId }` with the caller-supplied id; (c) single-tenant consumers have an escape hatch since IbkrOAuthCredentials is a public positional record (Auth/IbkrOAuthCredentials.cs:20) usable with a `with { TenantId = ... }` expression; (d) the consumer key is not secret material in any functional sense — it is a 9-char portal-chosen identifier (docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md:151,162) transmitted in cleartext as `oauth_consumer_key` in every request's Authorization header (design doc:212), and it cannot authenticate without the access token, encrypted secret, and both private RSA keys. The repo's redaction (AuditLogHandler.cs:11,104) targets Authorization headers/URLs, i.e. actual signatures — consistent with the identifier not being treated as a secret. Net: the trigger is concrete and reachable through the documented public factory + AddIbkrClient path, but the consequence is an identifier-exposure hygiene nit, not a credential leak — squarely `low` per the rubric ("nit"), matching the sweep's own claimed severity.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A single-tenant consumer follows the documented setup path — `OAuthCredentialsFactory.FromFile("ibkr-credentials.json")` (or FromJson, or FromEnvironment without IBKR_TENANT_ID set) passed to `AddIbkrClient(opts => opts.Credentials = creds)` — and wires standard OpenTelemetry/logging exporters. Because FromJson hard-codes TenantId = consumerKey (OAuthCredentialsFactory.cs:68-70) with no tenantId field or override parameter, the TenantContext (ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:113) carries the IBKR consumer key, and every HTTP request span, rate-limit metric tag, tickle log scope, and operations Activity emits it as `ibkr.tenant_id` (OAuthSigningHandler.cs:62,93; GlobalRateLimitingHandler.cs:52; TickleTimer.cs:93), so the consumer-key identifier lands verbatim in third-party observability backends. Impact is capped: the key is a non-secret 9-character identifier already sent in cleartext on every wire request, cannot authenticate alone, the env-var fallback is documented in README.md:275, the manager path overrides it (IbkrClientManager.cs:50), and consumers can `with { TenantId = ... }` on the public record — a minor telemetry-hygiene gap (the FromJson/FromFile path offers no tenant-id affordance), not a credential leak.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Read an optional tenantId field in the credential JSON (and add a tenantId parameter/overload), defaulting to an opaque derived id (e.g. short hash of the consumer key) rather than the raw key.
- **Suggested regression test:** FromJson_WithTenantIdField_UsesIt_AndDefaultIsNotRawConsumerKey — parse setup-tool JSON with and without tenantId and assert the raw consumer key never becomes the TenantId.

### CON-3 — low — IbkrWebSocketClient (subscribe replay duplication)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:259` · lens: concurrency, thread safety & disposal
- **Claim:** SubscribeTopicAsync adds its entry to _subscriptions (line 253) and then independently sends the subscribe message if _webSocket is Open (259-262), without holding _connectLock; ConnectCoreAsync assigns _webSocket (473) and later snapshots _subscriptions for replay (726-729). A subscribe racing ConnectAsync can both be included in the replay snapshot AND observe the open socket, sending the identical subscribe message twice (e.g. smd+conid twice).
- **Consequence:** Duplicate wire subscription message to IBKR; whether IBKR treats a repeated smd/sor/str subscribe as idempotent or double-delivers frames is not pinned by docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md or recordings/ — at worst duplicate frames on a money-bearing stream, at best a no-op.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:246-262 (registry add then unlocked open-check send), :437-487 (ConnectCoreAsync: _webSocket assigned at 473 before replay at 486), :723-735 (replay snapshot under _subscriptionLock only)
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — I re-traced the full path at HEAD (18c6a23). SubscribeTopicAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:225-265) adds its TopicSubscription to _subscriptions under _subscriptionLock (lines 247-254), then — WITHOUT holding _connectLock — independently checks `_webSocket?.State == WebSocketState.Open` (line 259) and sends the subscribe message (line 261). ConnectCoreAsync (lines 437-487), which runs under _connectLock from both ConnectAsync (line 196) and background ReconnectAsync (line 707, triggered by heartbeat failure line 549, server close line 596, receive error line 616, session refresh line 745, tickle watchdog line 763), assigns the open socket to _webSocket at line 473 and then replays via ReplayActiveSubscriptionsAsync (line 486), which snapshots _subscriptions under _subscriptionLock only (lines 723-729) and sends each distinct SubscribeMessage (lines 731-734). Concrete interleaving: thread A (consumer calling e.g. IIbkrClient.Streaming.MarketDataAsync → SubscribeTopicAsync directly, src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:100) adds its entry at line 253; thread B in ConnectCoreAsync assigns the open _webSocket at line 473 and snapshots at line 728 — the lock handoff on _subscriptionLock guarantees B sees A's entry, and A's subsequent unlocked read at line 259 sees the open socket; both line 261 and line 733 send the identical subscribe message. Nothing dedups: the TopicSubscription record (lines 888-892) carries no sent flag, SendTextAsync (lines 771-793) has no send-once guard, and replay's .Distinct() (line 728) only dedups within the replay set, not against the direct send. Counter-evidence hunted and not found: no VCR fix touched this (the merged stream addressed mapper robustness, metrics, disposal — not connect/subscribe locking); unit tests (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClientTests.cs:191 SubscribeTopicAsync_SendsSubscribeMessage) are sequential and pin nothing about the concurrent window; docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md contains no statement on repeated-subscribe idempotency (grep for duplicate/idempotent/resubscribe found only unrelated REST fields), and recordings/ holds only REST order captures (bracket/bulk/cleanup/oca/orderref/orders/priming) — the downstream IBKR reaction to a doubled smd/sor subscribe is unpinned. The duplicate wire send itself is confirmed code-side; only its amplification (no-op vs duplicate frames) is unpinned. Severity re-judged low: the window exists only while a connect/reconnect completes concurrently with a new subscribe; even if IBKR double-delivers, duplicate frames route into the same per-topic channel and sor/str consumers already carry a documented dedup obligation, while smd ticks are snapshot-like — no silent money corruption, no session/liveness failure, no pipeline wedge.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer's subscribe call (e.g. IIbkrClient.Streaming.MarketDataAsync for conid 265598) races a connect — most realistically a library-initiated background reconnect (heartbeat failure/server close/tickle watchdog → ReconnectAsync → ConnectCoreAsync under _connectLock), which the consumer cannot see or avoid. The subscribe adds its entry to _subscriptions (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:253) just before ConnectCoreAsync's replay snapshot (line 728), so the replay sends "smd+265598+{...}" (line 733); the subscribe's own unlocked open-socket check (line 259) then also observes the newly assigned open _webSocket (line 473) and sends the identical message again (line 261). IBKR receives the same subscribe twice on one connection; whether that is a no-op or double-delivers frames on a money-bearing stream (sor/str) is not pinned by the captured spec or recordings — at worst duplicate frames that a dedup-obligated consumer must absorb, at best nothing. Narrow window, bounded consequence: low.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Perform the open-check-and-send under _connectLock (or mark entries as 'sent' and have replay skip already-sent entries for the current connection generation).
- **Suggested regression test:** SubscribeTopicAsync_RacingConnect_SendsSubscribeExactlyOnce — fake adapter records sends; interleave SubscribeTopicAsync with ConnectAsync and assert exactly one copy of the subscribe message per connection.

### ERR-3 — low — FlexOperations.ExecuteInternalAsync (send-retry exhaustion classification)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Client/FlexOperations.cs:196` · lens: error taxonomy & result mapping
- **Claim:** When SendRequest exhausts its 3 in-library attempts on a retryable code (including 1018 rate-limit, canonical description 'Please try again'), the surfaced IbkrFlexError hard-codes IsRetryable:false while still carrying the retryable ErrorCode and its CodeDescription — a self-contradictory classification (ErrorCode 1018 + CodeDescription 'Too many requests…' + IsRetryable=false). The poll-timeout path returns IsRetryable:true for the analogous exhaustion (FlexOperations.cs:338-341), so the two exhaustion paths disagree.
- **Consequence:** A consumer retry scheduler keying on IsRetryable treats a transient rate-limit/generation-load condition as permanent and drops the query (e.g. skips a cash-transactions pull) after as little as ~20s of internal backoff; data quietly arrives a full schedule cycle late or never for one-shot jobs.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Client/FlexOperations.cs:186-199 (IsRetryable:false with lastSendError.ErrorCode/CodeDescription passed through), 177-179 (1018 gets only a 10s backoff, max 3 attempts vs. the 10-req/min limit), 338-346 (poll timeout path sets IsRetryable:true); src/IbkrConduit/Flex/FlexErrorCodes.cs:38 (1018 classified retryable).
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced end-to-end from the public surface at HEAD (18c6a23). Reachability: IIbkrClient.Flex (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IIbkrClient.cs:38) -> GetCashTransactionsAsync / GetTradeConfirmationsAsync / ExecuteQueryAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/FlexOperations.cs:79-130) all funnel into ExecuteInternalAsync (FlexOperations.cs:132). The send-retry loop (FlexOperations.cs:148-200) runs maxSendAttempts=3 (line 143); a retryable Flex error (e.g. 1018 rate limit, classified IsRetryable:true in src/IbkrConduit/Flex/FlexErrorCodes.cs:38, produced by BuildFlexErrorFromResponse at FlexOperations.cs:409 via ExtractReferenceCode:247) sets lastSendError (line 172) and retries with a 10s backoff for 1018 (lines 177-180, ±20% jitter via ApplyJitter:429-433). On the third consecutive retryable response, sendAttempt < maxSendAttempts is false (line 175), so control falls to the exhaustion block (lines 186-199), which constructs a new IbkrFlexError passing through lastSendError.ErrorCode (line 194) and lastSendError.CodeDescription (line 195: "Too many requests. Limited to one request per second, 10 requests per minute per token.") while hard-coding IsRetryable:false (line 196). The analogous poll-timeout exhaustion in PollForStatementAsync returns IsRetryable:true (FlexOperations.cs:338-341), pinned by test ExecuteQueryAsync_TimeoutExhausted_ReturnsRetryableFlexError (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Flex/FlexOperationsPollingTests.cs:232-256, asserting IsRetryable true at :254). So the two exhaustion paths genuinely disagree, and the surfaced error is internally contradictory (retryable code + "try again" description + IsRetryable=false). Counter-evidence hunt: (1) the send-exhaustion path IS exercised by test ExecuteQueryAsync_SendRequestAllAttemptsRetryable_FailsWithAttemptCount (FlexOperationsPollingTests.cs:467-489) — it asserts the message but does NOT assert IsRetryable, so nothing pins false as intentional; (2) no design-doc, ADR, or XML-doc statement of exhaustion-classification semantics exists (grep for IsRetryable across docs/ returns nothing outside findings; IbkrError.cs:61-75 documents only that the flag exists); (3) the VCR fix stream (#238-#249) did not touch this classification — no IsRetryable mention in docs/findings/; (4) one could argue IsRetryable:false is intentional ("library already retried"), but the poll-timeout path returning true (line 341) for the exact same in-library-exhaustion situation refutes intentionality — there is no consistent policy. The trigger does not depend on unpinned live wire behavior: any code in the library's own retryable table (1001, 1004-1009, 1018, 1019, 1021 — FlexErrorCodes.cs:22-41) returned three times in a row triggers it, and with 1018's documented 10-req/min/token limit plus only ~20s of total internal backoff (2 delays of ~10s, lines 177-180), three consecutive 1018s under token contention is concrete. Severity re-judged: the error is surfaced loudly with full message/code (not silent data corruption), Flex covers reporting/reconciliation pulls not order placement, and the consumer sees a failure — the harm is a misleading classification flag causing a retry scheduler to treat a transient condition as permanent, delaying (or for one-shot jobs, dropping) a data pull. That is a classification/robustness nit on a non-order path: low per the rubric.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer calls client.Flex.GetCashTransactionsAsync() (or any Flex query) while its Flex token is under rate-limit contention (e.g. another job on the same token already consumed the 10-req/min budget). IBKR answers SendRequest with code 1018 three times; FlexOperations retries internally with only ~10s backoff between attempts (FlexOperations.cs:177-180) — about 20s total, well inside the same rate-limit window — then exhausts and returns an IbkrFlexError carrying ErrorCode 1018 and the CodeDescription "Too many requests..." but IsRetryable hard-coded to false (FlexOperations.cs:193-199). A consumer retry scheduler that keys on IsRetryable — the natural contract, since the poll-timeout exhaustion path returns IsRetryable:true for the equivalent transient exhaustion (FlexOperations.cs:338-341) — classifies the rate-limited query as permanently failed and does not reschedule it; the cash-transactions/trade-confirmations pull silently waits a full schedule cycle, or never runs for a one-shot job, even though waiting under a minute would have succeeded. The same wrong-false classification fires for every other transient code in the retryable table (1001, 1009, 1019, ...) when generation load persists past 3 send attempts.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Propagate lastSendError.IsRetryable (true for exhausted-but-transient codes) so the field matches the code's canonical classification, mirroring the poll-timeout path.
- **Suggested regression test:** ExecuteQuery_SendRequestExhaustsOn1018_ErrorRemainsRetryable — fake FlexClient returning code 1018 three times; assert IbkrFlexError.IsRetryable is true.

### ERR-4 — low — ResultFactory.DetectHiddenError vs OrderOperations classification (order-rejection subtype)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Errors/ResultFactory.cs:157` · lens: error taxonomy & result mapping
- **Claim:** A bare-object 200 {"error":"…"} on an order-mutating endpoint is intercepted by DetectHiddenError inside FromResponse and surfaced as IbkrHiddenError before OrderOperations' classification runs (OrderOperations.cs:77-84 returns on !apiResult.IsSuccess), while only the array-wrapped [{"error":"…"}] shape reaches IbkrOrderRejectedError (OrderOperations.cs:431-437). This contradicts both types' published contracts: IbkrOrderRejectedError is documented as 'IBKR returned 200 OK with an error body on an order endpoint' and IbkrHiddenError as '…on a non-order endpoint' (IbkrError.cs:52-59, 78-85). The bare-object-on-orders shape is pinned as real by the repo's own integration test (ErrorResultTests.cs:172-199, which asserts only the message, not the type).
- **Consequence:** A consumer pattern-matching IbkrOrderRejectedError to detect definitive refusals (safe-to-resubmit-adjusted) misses bare-object rejects and falls into its generic/ambiguous handling — failure direction is safe (extra reconciliation, no double-submit), but the documented taxonomy is unreliable for the exact discrimination it exists to provide.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Errors/ResultFactory.cs:127-166 (bare object with error → IbkrHiddenError; arrays skipped at 134-138); src/IbkrConduit/Client/OrderOperations.cs:77-84 (hidden-error failure short-circuits before ClassifyOrderResponses), 429-437 (array-wrapped path yields IbkrOrderRejectedError); src/IbkrConduit/Errors/IbkrError.cs:52-59,78-85 (contradicted XML-doc contracts); tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Integration/Pipeline/ErrorResultTests.cs:172-199 (shape pinned, type unasserted).
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced end-to-end from the public surface. (1) Mechanism: IIbkrClient.Orders.PlaceOrderAsync -> OrderOperations.cs:75-77 calls ResultFactory.FromResponse; for a 200 the factory runs DetectHiddenError first (ResultFactory.cs:41-45). DetectHiddenError skips only bodies starting with '[' (ResultFactory.cs:134-138); a bare object {"error":"..."} deserializes into IbkrErrorBody and returns IbkrHiddenError at ResultFactory.cs:155-157. OrderOperations.cs:78-81 then short-circuits on !apiResult.IsSuccess and returns that IbkrHiddenError — ClassifyOrderResponses (the only constructor site of IbkrOrderRejectedError, OrderOperations.cs:424-426 and 433-436, verified by grep: no other src construction) never runs. Same routing on ModifyOrderAsync (OrderOperations.cs:291-296) and ReplyAsync (OrderOperations.cs:340-345). So IbkrOrderRejectedError is reachable ONLY for the empty-array [] and array-wrapped [{"error":...}] shapes; the bare-object shape on an order endpoint always yields IbkrHiddenError. (2) Trigger is pinned, not hypothetical: the captured spec docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:4585-4589 quotes IBKR's docs — an order that cannot be completed (permissions/funds) returns 200 OK with the bare-object {"error":"We cannot accept an order at the limit price..."} — and the repo's own full-DI-stack integration test ErrorResultTests.cs:172-199 exercises exactly this shape on POST .../orders (asserting only Message, not the subtype). (3) Contract contradiction is real at HEAD: IbkrError.cs:51-53 documents IbkrOrderRejectedError as 'IBKR returned 200 OK with an error body on an order endpoint' and IbkrError.cs:77-78 documents IbkrHiddenError as '...on a non-order endpoint'. The actual discriminator is bare-object vs array-wrapped, not order vs non-order — the XML docs describe a dichotomy the implementation does not follow, and the spec-pinned primary reject shape (bare object) lands on the type documented as impossible for order endpoints. (4) Counter-evidence hunted and weighed: the split is deliberate — the merged VCR-04 spec (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-07-vcr-04-order-outcome-replay-gate.md:15) blessed 'Failure(IbkrHiddenError/IbkrOrderRejectedError)' for the bare-object reply reject, and the unit test ReplyAsync_HiddenErrorBody_ReturnsFailureWithRejectReason (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Orders/OrderOperationsAmbiguityTests.cs:91-101) pins IbkrHiddenError for a bare-object 200 reject on an order path. This proves intent, but does not refute the finding: the XML docs at HEAD were not updated to match, so the published taxonomy is factually wrong about the discrimination it exists to provide. This is a residual gap the VCR-04/AMB-3/AMB-4 stream left open (it fixed empty-array and array-wrapped shapes and hardened routing, but left the two types' contracts contradicting the routing). Severity re-judged as low: both subtypes surface as Result.Failure carrying the reject message and raw body — nothing is silent, no crash, no wrong money numeric; the known consumer (RTOS IBV-05, per docs/findings/2026-07-04-rtos-venue-consumer-review.md:188) maps IbkrHiddenError and IbkrOrderRejectedError alike to definitive refusal; the failure direction for a doc-trusting consumer is extra reconciliation, never a double-submit. It does not rise to medium because it hides nothing — the misdirection is confined to the subtype label, and design doc / ADRs contain no competing taxonomy statement (grep of docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md finds neither type).
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer places an order via IIbkrClient.Orders.PlaceOrderAsync (or ModifyOrderAsync/ReplyAsync) and IBKR refuses it with the spec-pinned bare-object 200 body {"error":"We cannot accept an order at the limit price..."} (docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:4585-4589). DetectHiddenError (ResultFactory.cs:134-157) intercepts the body before order-specific classification, so the call returns Failure(IbkrHiddenError) — despite IbkrHiddenError's XML doc (IbkrError.cs:77-78) declaring it occurs 'on a non-order endpoint' and IbkrOrderRejectedError's doc (IbkrError.cs:51-53) claiming exactly this case. A consumer that pattern-matches IbkrOrderRejectedError to identify definitive order refusals (per the published docs) misses every bare-object reject — the most common, IBKR-documented reject shape — and drops into its generic/unknown-failure handling, typically triggering unnecessary reconcile-before-resubmit workflows. Failure direction is safe (the error is still a loud Failure carrying the reject text and raw body; no double-submit path exists), but the public error taxonomy is unreliable for the one discrimination it documents, a residual left open by the VCR-04 fix (whose spec and tests deliberately pinned IbkrHiddenError for the bare-object shape without updating the two types' contracts).
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** In the order-operations paths, remap an IbkrHiddenError failure from FromResponse to IbkrOrderRejectedError (the request path is known to be an order endpoint), or fix the XML docs to state the actual split.
- **Suggested regression test:** PlaceOrder_200BareObjectError_ReturnsIbkrOrderRejectedError — extend the existing ErrorResultTests.OrderError_200WithRejection to assert the error subtype.

### ERR-5 — low — Result<T> (default-struct hole)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Errors/Result.cs:26` · lens: error taxonomy & result mapping
- **Claim:** Result<T> is a public readonly struct, so `default(Result<T>)` / `new Result<T>()` is constructible by consumers and yields IsSuccess=false with _error=null; the Error property's null-forgiving return (line 26-28) then hands back a null IbkrError, and EnsureSuccess (line 55-57) throws NullReferenceException from inside IbkrApiException's constructor (IbkrApiException.cs:17-21 dereferences error.Message) instead of anything diagnosable.
- **Consequence:** A consumer that materializes a default Result (uninitialized field, array element, Task.FromResult(default)) gets a value that masquerades as a failure whose Error is null — result.Error.Message NREs and EnsureSuccess crashes with an unrelated NRE; the library itself never produces this state, so severity is limited to consumer misuse of the public surface.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Errors/Result.cs:8 (public readonly struct, implicit parameterless ctor), 26-28 (Error returns _error! which is null for default), 55-57 (EnsureSuccess passes null into IbkrApiException); src/IbkrConduit/Errors/IbkrApiException.cs:17-21 (error.Message dereference).
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced end-to-end at HEAD 18c6a23. src/IbkrConduit/Errors/Result.cs:8 declares `public readonly struct Result<T>` whose only constructors are private (lines 30-42), but as a struct it is default-constructible by any consumer (`default(Result<T>)` / `new Result<T>()`); such an instance has IsSuccess=false (line 14, bool default) and _error=null (line 11). Result.cs:26-28: `Error => !IsSuccess ? _error! : throw ...` — the null-forgiving `_error!` hands back a literal null IbkrError for the default instance, so `result.Error.Message` NREs at the consumer call site. Result.cs:55-57: `EnsureSuccess()` throws `new IbkrApiException(_error!)` with null; IbkrApiException.cs:17-18 immediately evaluates `base(error.Message)` in the constructor, so the consumer gets a raw NullReferenceException thrown from inside the exception ctor instead of any diagnosable IbkrApiException/InvalidOperationException. Counter-evidence hunt: repo-wide grep found zero occurrences of `default(Result` or `new Result<` in src/ or tests/; every library-produced Result flows through Result<T>.Success/Failure with non-null errors (ResultFactory.cs:31,44,53,57,73,84,90), and all public-surface Result<T> values are returned via Task<Result<T>> from operations methods — the library can never emit a default instance. No test pins the default-struct behavior, and no VCR fix (PRs #238-#249) touched Result.cs's null-forgiving paths. The mechanism and trigger are concrete, so CONFIRMED — but the trigger is exclusively consumer-side construction of a default struct, which the sweep itself acknowledged.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer (e.g., RTOS test or wiring code) materializes a default Result<T> — an uninitialized `Result<OrderSubmitted>` field, an element of a `Result<T>[]`, or `Task.FromResult(default(Result<T>))` in a stub — and treats it as the failure it claims to be (IsSuccess=false): reading `result.Error.Message` throws NullReferenceException at the consumer's call site because Result.cs:26-28 returns the null `_error` via `_error!`, and calling `result.EnsureSuccess()` throws a raw NullReferenceException from inside IbkrApiException's constructor (IbkrApiException.cs:18 dereferences `error.Message`) instead of a diagnosable IbkrApiException — masking the real problem (an uninitialized Result) behind an unrelated NRE. The library itself never produces this state (all Results flow through the private Success/Failure ctors with non-null errors), so impact is limited to confusing diagnostics on consumer misuse of the public struct; no money, order, or session integrity is at risk.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Guard the default state: have Error/EnsureSuccess/Match/Switch throw InvalidOperationException("uninitialized Result") when both _value and _error are null (or back IsSuccess with an _initialized flag).
- **Suggested regression test:** DefaultResult_ErrorAccess_ThrowsInvalidOperation — asserts default(Result<int>).Error and .EnsureSuccess() throw a descriptive InvalidOperationException, never NRE.

### PRB-1.3 — low — StreamingOperations subscribe-message construction (fields/keys/accountId interpolation)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:96` · lens: gap probe: Client/StreamingOperations.cs — the streaming facade no lens owned; prefix-only frame routing means cross-conid/cross-account fan-in on smd/ssd/sld
- **Claim:** Consumer-supplied strings are interpolated into the wire message with no escaping or validation: MarketDataAsync wraps each field in bare quotes (line 96: $"\"{f}\""), BuildKeysFieldsArgs does the same for keys/fields (lines 199, 203), and accountId is spliced raw into the topic-target segment (lines 171, 186). A field/key containing a quote or backslash produces malformed JSON args; an accountId containing '+' shifts the topic-target segment (and would also desync the umd/usd/uld cancel and StreamingFrameHelpers.ParseAccountIdFromTopic matching). No exception is thrown client-side — the malformed subscribe is sent and the subscription is returned as if live.
- **Consequence:** A subscription that silently never delivers (or subscribes to something other than requested) with no client-side error — a robustness gap that hides a dead feed; low because realistic inputs (MarketDataFields constants, IBKR-issued account ids, documented ledger/summary keys) contain none of these characters, so a trigger requires consumer garbage-in.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:96-98 (fields quoted unescaped into smd args), :171-172 and :186-187 (accountId raw into topic segment and cancel), :194-206 (BuildKeysFieldsArgs quotes keys/fields unescaped); src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:771-793 (SendTextAsync sends the string verbatim, no validation).
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced the full path from the public surface. IIbkrClient.Streaming (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IIbkrClient.cs:33, src/IbkrConduit/Client/IbkrClient.cs:85) exposes StreamingOperations. (1) MarketDataAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:94-98) wraps each consumer-supplied field in bare quotes — line 96: `string.Join(",", fields.Select(f => $"\"{f}\""))` — with no escaping or validation; a field containing `"` or `\` yields malformed JSON in the smd args. (2) BuildKeysFieldsArgs (StreamingOperations.cs:194-206, quoting at :199 and :203) does the same for AccountSummaryAsync/AccountLedgerAsync keys/fields. (3) accountId is spliced raw into the '+'-delimited topic segment at :171-172 (ssd/usd) and :186-187 (sld/uld).
