Downstream, SubscribeTopicAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:225-265) performs no validation: it registers the subscription (:245-254), sends the string verbatim via SendTextAsync (:261; :771-793 — UTF-8 encode and ws.SendAsync, no inspection) or queues it for replay (:723-735), and returns a live-looking (reader, unsubscribe) handle unconditionally (:264). No test pins any validation (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Streaming/StreamingOperationsTests.cs and integration tests all use well-formed inputs), and none of the merged VCR-01..11 fixes touched subscribe-message construction. Two corrections to the sweep's claimed consequence: (a) inbound frame routing splits the topic on the FIRST '+' only (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:660-661), so an accountId containing '+' does not desync local channel routing — the inbound topic string comes from IBKR, not from the consumer's string; (b) the cancel/refcount path stays internally consistent because subscribe and cancel are built from the same accountId string and refcounting compares CancelMessage strings verbatim (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:815-819). The real effect of a '+' in accountId is only how IBKR's server parses the outbound '+'-delimited message — unpinned by docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md or recordings/, as is IBKR's exact response to malformed smd/ssd args (silent ignore vs. error frame). The client-side defect (malformed message sent, no exception, subscription handle returned as if live) is fully confirmed in code. Severity is low per the rubric: the trigger requires consumer garbage-in — MarketDataFields constants (src/IbkrConduit/MarketData/MarketDataFields.cs:11) are numeric strings, IBKR-issued account ids (U/DU + digits) contain no '+', and documented ledger/summary keys contain no quotes/backslashes — so no realistic input reaches the failure.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer calls client.Streaming.MarketDataAsync(conid, new[] { "31\"" }) (or AccountSummaryAsync/AccountLedgerAsync with a key/field containing a quote or backslash, or an accountId containing '+'): StreamingOperations interpolates the string unescaped into the wire message (StreamingOperations.cs:96, :199, :203, :171, :186), producing malformed JSON args or a shifted '+'-delimited topic segment; IbkrWebSocketClient.SubscribeTopicAsync sends it verbatim (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:261, :784-789) and returns a normal subscription handle — no client-side exception, no validation failure — so the consumer holds a subscription that (depending on unpinned IBKR server handling of the malformed message) silently never delivers or subscribes to something other than requested, with the dead feed observable only by absence of data. Trigger requires consumer-supplied garbage: all realistic inputs (MarketDataFields numeric constants, IBKR-issued account ids, documented ledger/summary keys) contain none of these characters.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Build the args object with JsonSerializer.Serialize (escapes automatically) and validate accountId/conid-bearing topic segments (reject '+', whitespace, empty) with ArgumentException at the facade.
- **Suggested regression test:** MarketDataAsync_FieldContainingQuote_ProducesValidJsonOrThrows — assert the built subscribe message parses as `smd+{conid}+<valid JSON>` (or that ArgumentException is thrown) for a field value containing a double quote; companion AccountSummaryAsync_AccountIdWithPlus_Throws.

### PRB-2.3 — low — SessionManager (lifecycle notification after suppress-aborted reauth)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:408` · lens: gap probe: Question-suppression flow: SuppressQuestionsAsync failure inside EnsureInitializedAsync/ReauthenticateAsync — an SES x ORD interaction nobody mentioned
- **Claim:** A suppress failure in ReauthenticateAsync aborts after the LST was already rotated (RefreshAsync succeeded, :337) and ssodh/init re-established the server session, but before _notifier.NotifyAsync (:408). If recovery then completes via EnsureInitializedAsync (next consumer call re-inits: :137-264 contains no NotifyAsync call anywhere), the session-refresh notification for that LST rotation is never delivered, so IbkrWebSocketClient's OnSessionRefreshedAsync reconnect (subscribed at IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:137, handler :737-745) never fires for it.
- **Consequence:** A WebSocket invalidated by the reauth's session replacement is not proactively reconnected; recovery falls back to the heartbeat-failure/server-close/tickle-watchdog paths (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:549, :596, :616, :760-767), delaying streaming resubscription by up to a watchdog interval rather than losing it — a robustness/latency gap, backstopped, hence low.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:337 (LST rotated), :377-391 (suppress throw aborts), :408 (NotifyAsync only on reauth success), :137-264 (EnsureInitializedAsync never notifies); src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:137, :737-745 (session-refresh reconnect), :549/:596/:616/:760-767 (backstop watchdogs).
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced independently at HEAD 18c6a23. (1) Sole notification site: grep over src/ shows _notifier.NotifyAsync is called exactly once — src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:408 inside ReauthenticateAsync; EnsureInitializedAsync (SessionManager.cs:137-264) contains no NotifyAsync. (2) Abort window: in ReauthenticateAsync the LST is rotated at :337 (RefreshAsync), ssodh/init succeeds at :339-340 with authenticated verified at :365-375, then SuppressQuestionsAsync runs at :377-391 (only when _options.SuppressMessageIds.Count > 0; RethrowOriginal at :388 rethrows the transport exception). A throw there skips ScheduleProactiveRefresh (:406) and NotifyAsync (:408), hits the catch at :423-430 which sets _state=Uninitialized and rethrows; the reauth epoch (:419) is not bumped. (3) Recovery without notification: reauth triggers are TokenRefreshHandler.cs:98 (401 retry), the tickle failure callback (SessionManager.cs:518-522), and the proactive-refresh retry loop (:597). For the 401 and tickle triggers the exception propagates; the next consumer call re-enters EnsureInitializedAsync (state is Uninitialized per :139/:152), which re-inits to Ready (:237) and never notifies — the session-refresh notification for that LST rotation is permanently lost. Only the proactive-refresh trigger self-retries ReauthenticateAsync (:589-619) and eventually notifies on a later success (delayed by >=5s backoff). (4) Consequence: IbkrWebSocketClient subscribes to the notifier at IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:137; OnSessionRefreshedAsync (:737-746) is the only proactive ReconnectAsync("session_refresh") driver. docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md §12.3 records "WebSocket connection must be re-established after live session token refresh" — the recorded contract the missed notification breaks. Fallbacks: heartbeat send failure (:546-551), server close (:593-597), receive error (:613-617), tickle watchdog (:748-769, fires only when the socket is not Open per :755-758). So a server-closed socket recovers promptly via the pump; a stale-but-open socket would evade the watchdog, but that staleness mode is not pinned by docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md or recordings. (5) No test in tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Session/SessionManagerTests.cs or SessionManagerEdgeTests.cs pins notification delivery across a suppress-aborted reauth followed by re-init. The code-side trigger (transient HTTP failure of the suppress POST after a successful ssodh/init, with SuppressMessageIds configured) is concrete and wire-independent; only the worst-case consequence magnitude depends on unpinned server behavior. None of the merged VCR fixes (PRs #238-#249) touched this ordering.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer configures IbkrClientOptions.SuppressMessageIds (common for automated trading to suppress order-confirmation prompts) and holds live streaming subscriptions. A 401 or tickle failure triggers SessionManager.ReauthenticateAsync: RefreshAsync rotates the LST (SessionManager.cs:337) and ssodh/init re-establishes the brokerage session (:339-365), but the follow-up SuppressQuestionsAsync POST fails transiently (:377-391), aborting the reauth before _notifier.NotifyAsync (:408) and resetting state to Uninitialized (:428). The next consumer REST call recovers the session via EnsureInitializedAsync (:137-264), which never notifies — so IbkrWebSocketClient.OnSessionRefreshedAsync (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:737-746), the only proactive session_refresh reconnect, never fires for that LST rotation even though design doc §12.3 requires WS re-establishment after an LST refresh. The WebSocket lingers on the pre-rotation session until a backstop fires: server close/receive error reconnect promptly (:593-597, :613-617), heartbeat send failure (:546-551) or the next successful tickle's watchdog — which only acts on a non-Open socket (:755-758) — cover the rest, delaying streaming resubscription by up to a heartbeat/tickle interval; a hypothetical stale-but-open socket (unpinned wire behavior) would evade all backstops until TCP death. Net effect is delayed, backstopped streaming recovery on a narrow opt-in trigger — a robustness/latency gap, not data loss.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Have EnsureInitializedAsync call _notifier.NotifyAsync when it completes a re-init after a previously-established session (i.e., when _sessionEstablished is already true), or notify from ReauthenticateAsync as soon as ssodh/init succeeds (before the suppress step).
- **Suggested regression test:** ReauthenticateAsync_SuppressFails_SubsequentInitNotifiesLifecycle — fail reauth via SuppressException, then run EnsureInitializedAsync to success; assert the ISessionLifecycleNotifier session-refresh subscriber was invoked.

### PRB-4.3 — low — IbkrClient facade (DisposeAsync in the plain AddIbkrClient path)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Client/IbkrClient.cs:132` · lens: gap probe: IbkrClient facade layer: active-probe health path bypasses the VCR-07 competing-evidence flow, and facade DisposeAsync vs container ownership in the plain AddIbkrClient path
- **Claim:** IIbkrClient is publicly IAsyncDisposable (IIbkrClient.cs:8) inviting `await using client`, but the facade's DisposeAsync disposes only the container-owned SessionManager singleton (IbkrClient.cs:132-136). In the plain AddIbkrClient path (SkipLogoutOnDispose defaults false, IbkrClientOptions.cs:134) this issues the IBKR logout and stops the tickle (SessionManager.cs:457-483) while the container-owned WebSocket client and its live subscriptions are untouched (only provider disposal reaches them, StreamingAndFlexRegistration.cs:33-46); the WS reconnect path then calls the disposed SessionManager whose _semaphore is disposed (SessionManager.cs:485), throwing ObjectDisposedException. The SessionManager dispose guard is also check-then-set on a volatile bool (SessionManager.cs:441-446), so a facade dispose racing provider disposal can run the body twice — double gauge decrement (:459) and double logout (:477). Manager-path teardown was orchestrated by VCR-08 (ManagedTenant.cs:28-48); this plain-path facade dispose received no equivalent treatment.
- **Consequence:** A consumer who treats the facade's advertised IAsyncDisposable as full teardown gets a partial one: server-side session logged out under still-live streaming subscriptions, background reconnect churn throwing ObjectDisposedException until the provider is disposed, and (in the concurrent-dispose race) a negative active-session gauge and duplicate logout. Sequential facade-then-provider disposal is safe (second call no-ops at SessionManager.cs:441-443), which bounds this to a footgun rather than a correctness failure.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Client/IIbkrClient.cs:8 (public IAsyncDisposable); src/IbkrConduit/Client/IbkrClient.cs:132-136 (disposes only _sessionManager); src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:441-446 (non-atomic guard), :459 (gauge decrement), :473-483 (logout, plain path), :485 (_semaphore.Dispose), :148 (WaitAsync on disposed semaphore in EnsureInitializedAsync); src/IbkrConduit/Http/StreamingAndFlexRegistration.cs:33-46 (WS client container-owned, wired to ISessionManager); src/IbkrConduit/Client/ManagedTenant.cs:28-48 (contrast: manager path logs out then disposes the whole provider)
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Traced end-to-end from the public surface. IIbkrClient : IAsyncDisposable is public (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IIbkrClient.cs:8) and registered as a container singleton (src/IbkrConduit/Http/ServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:159-163), so a consumer can `await using` it. IbkrClient.DisposeAsync disposes only _sessionManager (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IbkrClient.cs:132-136); this exact behavior is pinned by test DisposeAsync_DisposesSessionManager (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Client/IbkrClientTests.cs:102-108). In the plain AddIbkrClient path SkipLogoutOnDispose stays false (only TenantBuilder.cs:27 sets it true; field at src/IbkrConduit/Session/IbkrClientOptions.cs:134), so SessionManager.DisposeAsync decrements the active-session gauge (src/IbkrConduit/Session/SessionManager.cs:459), stops the tickle timer (:463-466), issues the IBKR logout (:473-483), and disposes _semaphore/_disposeCts (:485-486). The container-owned IbkrWebSocketClient (src/IbkrConduit/Http/StreamingAndFlexRegistration.cs:33-46) and its live subscriptions are untouched; its channel-completing teardown runs only on provider disposal (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:387-427), so consumer stream readers never complete. The dispose guard is check-then-set on a volatile bool (SessionManager.cs:441-446), so concurrent facade+provider disposal can double-run the body (double gauge decrement :459, duplicate best-effort logout :473-483). Contrast: the manager path (VCR-08) disposes the whole child provider after one bounded logout (src/IbkrConduit/Client/ManagedTenant.cs:28-48); the plain facade path received no equivalent. However, two of the sweep's claimed consequences are overstated: (1) the WS reconnect path does NOT call the disposed SessionManager directly — ReconnectAsync → ConnectCoreAsync calls _sessionApi.TickleAsync (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:679-721, :454), and the disposed semaphore is reached only if that tickle 401s and TokenRefreshHandler (src/IbkrConduit/Session/TokenRefreshHandler.cs:98) calls ReauthenticateAsync, which waits on the semaphore (SessionManager.cs:288) before its ShuttingDown check (:292) — post-logout-tickle-401 is live wire behavior not pinned by recordings; (2) there is no 'reconnect churn' and no consumer-visible ObjectDisposedException — ReconnectAsync catches and logs all connect failures (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:712-715), each trigger (heartbeat_failure :549, server_close :596, receive_error :616) breaks its loop, StartHeartbeat/StartMessagePump only restart on a successful connect (:484-485), and the tickle watchdog's driver was already stopped by SessionManager dispose, so at most one failed reconnect fires per pre-existing trigger, then permanent silence. Sequential facade-then-provider disposal (the realistic pattern) is fully safe via the _disposed guard (:441-443). Net: the partial-teardown footgun and the non-atomic dispose guard are real and code-traceable, but the blast radius is a logged background exception, silently non-completing stream readers, and a race-only duplicate logout/negative gauge — a robustness/API-shape gap, not an auth/session integrity failure or consumer crash. Severity: low.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A plain-path consumer (AddIbkrClient, no manager) with active streaming subscriptions writes `await using var client = provider.GetRequiredService<IIbkrClient>();` and lets the facade dispose while the provider lives on: IbkrClient.DisposeAsync (IbkrClient.cs:132-136) disposes only the SessionManager, which logs the tenant out of IBKR and stops the tickle loop (SessionManager.cs:457-483) while the container-owned WebSocket client keeps its socket and subscription registry alive (StreamingAndFlexRegistration.cs:33-46). When the server subsequently drops the socket, exactly one background reconnect attempt runs, fails inside ConnectCoreAsync (via tickle 401 → TokenRefreshHandler → ReauthenticateAsync waiting on the disposed semaphore, SessionManager.cs:288/:485 — wire-dependent), is caught and logged (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:712-715), and the consumer's stream channel readers are left permanently open-but-silent (never completed) until the provider itself is disposed. Additionally, if the facade and the provider are disposed concurrently from two threads, the non-atomic _disposed guard (SessionManager.cs:441-446) lets both run the dispose body: the active-session gauge is decremented twice (:459) going negative, and a duplicate logout is sent (:473-483). Sequential facade-then-provider disposal — the normal pattern — is safe, bounding this to an API-shape footgun rather than a correctness failure.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Either make facade DisposeAsync a full-client teardown (disconnect/dispose the WS client before the session logout, mirroring ManagedTenant ordering) or document it as session-only and make the dispose guard atomic (Interlocked.Exchange) so a facade/provider dispose race cannot double-run logout and the gauge decrement.
- **Suggested regression test:** IbkrClient_DisposeAsync_WithActiveStreamingSubscription_TearsDownWebSocketBeforeLogout — subscribe via the DI stack against mock WS, `await using` the facade, assert the socket is closed and no ObjectDisposedException surfaces from the reconnect path; plus a concurrent facade+provider dispose asserting exactly one logout request hits WireMock.

### STR-6 — low — IbkrWebSocketClient.ConnectCoreAsync (adapter leak on failed connect)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:472` · lens: streaming delivery & backpressure
- **Claim:** The factory-created IWebSocketAdapter (line 465) is assigned to _webSocket only after ConnectAsync succeeds (line 473); if ws.ConnectAsync throws (network down, DNS failure, IBKR rejecting the cookie), the adapter — and its underlying ClientWebSocket — is never disposed.
- **Consequence:** During an extended outage the tickle watchdog retries a reconnect on every successful tickle, leaking one undisposed ClientWebSocket per attempt; a bounded resource drip, observable only as memory/handle growth, no functional data loss.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:464-473 (create → SetRequestHeader → ConnectAsync → assignment; no try/catch or await using around the pre-assignment window), :748-769 (watchdog retry loop per tickle); src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/ClientWebSocketAdapter.cs:48-52 (DisposeAsync is the only release of the inner ClientWebSocket).
- **Skeptic verdict:** **CONFIRMED** — Independent trace at HEAD 18c6a23. (1) Mechanism: src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:465 creates the adapter (`var ws = _webSocketFactory();`), :466-470 set headers/proxy, :472 `await ws.ConnectAsync(uri, cancellationToken);`, :473 `_webSocket = ws;`. There is no try/catch, using, or await-using around :465-472, so any exception from ConnectAsync (or even SetRequestHeader/new Uri) abandons `ws` without disposal. The only disposal sites for an adapter are DisconnectAsync (:497-518, reads `_webSocket` — still null/old value at that point) and it disposes only what was assigned; nothing ever disposes a pre-assignment adapter. (2) Production adapter: src/IbkrConduit/Http/StreamingAndFlexRegistration.cs:40 registers `() => new ClientWebSocketAdapter()`; ClientWebSocketAdapter.cs:13 allocates `new ClientWebSocket()` in the field initializer at construction, and :48-52 confirm DisposeAsync is the sole release path. (3) Reachability from public surface: IbkrWebSocketClient.ConnectAsync (:177-202) → ConnectCoreAsync (:196), reached from IIbkrClient streaming subscribe flows; the background retry paths ReconnectAsync (:679-721, ConnectCoreAsync at :707, failure swallowed and logged at :712-715) and the tickle watchdog OnTickleSucceededAsync (:748-769, fires on every successful tickle while `_webSocket` is not Open at :755) re-enter ConnectCoreAsync repeatedly. (4) Drip rate nuance the sweep glossed over: ConnectCoreAsync first calls TickleAsync (:454); if REST is also down, that throws before :465 and NO adapter is created — and the watchdog only fires on tickle SUCCESS anyway. So the leak requires the specific (but realistic) state "REST reachable, WS connect failing" (IBKR WS endpoint outage, cookie/handshake rejection, proxy blocking wss): one undisposed ClientWebSocketAdapter per tickle interval. (5) Severity re-judged down in consequence detail: a failed ClientWebSocket.ConnectAsync in the .NET runtime aborts and releases the internally created invoker/handler on the failure path, so the abandoned object is mostly GC-collectible managed state rather than a pinned OS handle; ClientWebSocket has no finalizer-critical resource left open after a failed connect in the common case. No functional data loss, no wrong data, no session corruption; DisposeAsync of the client itself is unaffected. (6) No test pins failed-connect adapter disposal (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Streaming/FakeWebSocketAdapter.cs has DisposeAsync at :130 but no connect-failure/dispose-count test in IbkrWebSocketClientTests.cs), and no VCR story (PRs #238-#249) touched this window — VCR-09/FIL-7 addressed the Meter gauge (:68-72, :432-434), not this. Verdict CONFIRMED: the trigger (ws.ConnectAsync throwing) is local .NET/network behavior, not unpinned IBKR wire behavior.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** While IBKR's REST endpoint stays reachable but the WebSocket endpoint is failing (wss outage, handshake/cookie rejection, or a proxy blocking wss), every successful tickle fires the watchdog (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:748-769) because _webSocket is not Open; each retry enters ConnectCoreAsync, where the factory-created ClientWebSocketAdapter (:465) is abandoned when ws.ConnectAsync throws at :472 before the :473 assignment — no code path ever disposes it. The exception is swallowed at :712-715 and the cycle repeats each tickle interval, dripping one undisposed adapter per attempt. Practical impact is a slow, bounded managed-memory/IDisposable-hygiene leak (the runtime releases the failed connection's own network resources internally), observable only as gradual heap growth during an extended WS-only outage; no data loss, no session or order impact.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Wrap the connect attempt in try/catch and await ws.DisposeAsync() before rethrowing when ConnectAsync (or the preceding header/proxy setup) throws.
- **Suggested regression test:** ConnectCoreAsync_ConnectThrows_DisposesCreatedAdapter — FakeWebSocketAdapter whose ConnectAsync throws; assert its DisposeAsync was invoked and ConnectAsync surfaced the original exception.

### ORD-5 — low — OrderOperations.GetLiveOrdersAsync (follow-up skip when caller passes filters+force together)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Client/OrderOperations.cs:232` · lens: orders & money path
- **Claim:** The follow-up skip condition `if (filtered && force != true)` assumes a single call carrying BOTH filters and force=true clears IBKR's cached filter behavior by itself. The spec's wording describes the clear as a force=true call issued AFTER the filtered call (docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:4150 'set force=true in a follow-up /iserver/account/orders call'), and the 2026-07-07 priming recordings only capture the separate-calls sequence (recordings/priming/001 filters-only, 002 force-only, 003 plain) — the combined filters+force shape is unpinned.
- **Consequence:** If a combined filtered+forced call re-caches the filter behavior after (or instead of) clearing it, a consumer who only ever calls GetLiveOrdersAsync(filters, force: true) and then subscribes OrderUpdatesAsync gets sor order-detail frames silently suppressed — the exact GAP1-2 delivery loss VCR-05 set out to close, resurfacing through one parameter combination.
- **Evidence:** OrderOperations.cs:226-235 (skip rationale comment and condition); docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:4150 (follow-up wording); recordings/priming/001-003 (only the separate-call sequence captured); tests pin the skip as desired behavior without upstream evidence: tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Orders/OrderOperationsTests.cs:383 (GetLiveOrdersAsync_FilteredAndAlreadyForced_IssuesNoFollowUp).
- **Skeptic verdict:** **PLAUSIBLE** — Code-side trace, all files read directly at HEAD (18c6a23). Public surface: IOrderOperations.GetLiveOrdersAsync exposes both `filters` and `force` (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IOrderOperations.cs:79-82), so the combined call is reachable by any IIbkrClient consumer; nothing guards against passing both. OrderOperations.GetLiveOrdersAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/OrderOperations.cs:209-238) forwards both params to Refit in one wire call (src/IbkrConduit/Orders/IIbkrOrderApi.cs:38-42 — both are [Query] params on the same GET), then skips the force-clear follow-up when `filtered && force != true` fails, i.e. exactly when the caller passed force=true alongside filters (OrderOperations.cs:232; the comment at 230-231 asserts 'the caller's own call already clears the cache'). Upstream evidence check: the captured spec's wording describes the clear as force=true 'in a follow-up /iserver/account/orders call' AFTER the filtered call (docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:4150) — a separate second call, not a parameter combined with the filtered call. The recordings pin only the three separate shapes: recordings/priming/001 is filters-only (?filters=cancelled), 002 is force-only (?force=true), 003 is plain — I read all three; no combined filters+force capture exists. The design doc contract states 'After ANY filtered call, the library itself issues the force=true follow-up' (docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md:749) with no recorded carve-out for caller-forced calls, and the VCR-05 spec (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-07-vcr-05-live-orders-priming.md:14,26) likewise specs 'after any GetLiveOrdersAsync call that passed filters' — the skip is an implementation-added exception pinned only by a unit test (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Orders/OrderOperationsTests.cs:383-392, GetLiveOrdersAsync_FilteredAndAlreadyForced_IssuesNoFollowUp), which is a code-side pin, not upstream evidence. I also verified no other defensive layer exists: StreamingOperations.cs contains no force/priming logic (grep for force/priming/10.6 returned nothing), so the OrderOperations follow-up is the only mechanism clearing the documented sor suppression. Counter-evidence hunt results: (a) the skip is deliberate and tested, but that only proves intent, not upstream correctness; (b) severity mitigators are real — the combined call is degenerate for reading orders (force=true returns a blank array per docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:4160 and recordings/priming/002, so filters on that call select nothing useful), and any LATER filtered-without-force call would re-trigger the follow-up (OrderOperations.cs:232) and clear residue; (c) the underlying sor-suppression effect itself is documented-but-never-observed ('documented, not independently observable on demand' — docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md:746 and the VCR-05 spec line 5). Verdict logic: the code mechanism and reachability are CONFIRMED, but the wrong outcome requires two unpinned upstream behaviors to hold simultaneously — (1) a combined filters+force call does not clear (or re-caches) the filter behavior, and (2) the documented sor suppression actually manifests. Neither is pinned by docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md, recordings/, or any test — that is PLAUSIBLE per the verdict rules, not CONFIRMED. Severity: low — the trigger is a narrow, degenerate parameter combination (the combined call always returns a blank array, so a consumer using it as their only live-orders read would immediately notice), the failure is doubly contingent on unverified wire behavior, and it self-heals on any subsequent filtered-without-force call; this is a residual unpinned edge left open by VCR-05, not a live defect.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer calls client.Orders.GetLiveOrdersAsync(filters: [OrderStatusFilter.Cancelled], force: true) — e.g. intending 'clear the cache and read my cancelled orders in one shot' — then subscribes OrderUpdatesAsync. The library sends a single GET /iserver/account/orders?filters=cancelled&force=true and, because force==true, skips the §10.6 force-clear follow-up (OrderOperations.cs:232). IF IBKR treats the filters on that combined call as (re-)caching the filter behavior rather than letting the same call's force=true clear it — a semantics never captured (recordings/priming/001-003 pin only the separate-call shapes; docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:4150 describes the clear as a follow-up call) — then sor order-detail frames stay silently suppressed for the session, resurfacing the GAP1-2 delivery loss VCR-05 closed, until some later filtered-without-force call happens to re-trigger the follow-up. Whether the failure occurs at all depends on unpinned live IBKR wire behavior (both the combined-call semantics and the suppression effect itself), and the triggering call shape is degenerate (it always returns a blank array), so a real consumer is unlikely to rely on it exclusively.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Attended live-paper probe: record filters+force=true followed by a sor subscription to pin whether detail frames flow; if suppressed, drop the `force != true` exemption and always issue the unfiltered force follow-up after any filtered call.
- **Suggested regression test:** Recording-backed pin (recordings/priming/004+) of the filters+force combined call plus a WireMock test asserting whichever follow-up policy the probe establishes.

### RST-5 — low — MarketDataOperations (snapshot preflight cache vs session re-auth)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Client/MarketDataOperations.cs:111` · lens: REST breadth & periphery
- **Claim:** The preflight cache marks a conid as 'preflighted' for PreflightCacheDuration (default 5 min, IbkrClientOptions.cs:34) the moment a preflight retry is issued (MarketDataOperations.cs:109-115), and is never invalidated on brokerage-session re-auth: MarketDataOperations takes no ISessionLifecycleNotifier dependency (constructor :58-74). After a 401 recovery / ssodh re-init resets server-side snapshot subscriptions, the next GetSnapshotAsync finds the empty-field response, sees the conid cached (GetConidsNeedingPreflight :248-266), skips the preflight retry, and returns a success Result whose MarketDataSnapshot has null LastPrice/Bid/Ask — silently — until the next call or cache expiry. The cache is also populated before the retry result is inspected, so a retry that still returns no data leaves subsequent calls returning empty snapshots without re-requesting for the cache window.
- **Consequence:** For up to PreflightCacheDuration after a session re-auth (a routine daily event), snapshot consumers receive success-shaped results with all price fields null and no signal that the data is a preflight artifact rather than a market condition.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Client/MarketDataOperations.cs:105-130 (cache set at :111-114 before retry; retry result returned as-is at :129-137), :248-266 (cache short-circuits preflight), :58-74 (no lifecycle-notifier dependency; contrast IbkrWebSocketClient which resubscribes via ISessionLifecycleNotifier, src/IbkrConduit/Http/StreamingAndFlexRegistration.cs:33-46); default duration src/IbkrConduit/Session/IbkrClientOptions.cs:34.
- **Skeptic verdict:** **PLAUSIBLE** — Code-side mechanism CONFIRMED by independent trace. Reachability: IIbkrClient.MarketData (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IbkrClient.cs:82) -> IMarketDataOperations.GetSnapshotAsync, registered as a singleton (src/IbkrConduit/Http/ConsumerPipelineRegistration.cs:61-62), so the instance-level MemoryCache _preflightCache (MarketDataOperations.cs:47, :73) survives any brokerage-session re-auth. (1) Cache is populated BEFORE the retry result is inspected: entries set at MarketDataOperations.cs:109-115 with AbsoluteExpirationRelativeToNow = _options.PreflightCacheDuration (default 5 min, src/IbkrConduit/Session/IbkrClientOptions.cs:34), retry issued at :120-122, retry result returned as-is at :124-129/:136-138 even if still empty — no cache invalidation on an empty retry. (2) Cache short-circuits future preflight retries: GetConidsNeedingPreflight (MarketDataOperations.cs:248-266) skips any cached conid at :254, so a post-re-auth empty-field response maps straight to a Success Result whose MarketDataSnapshot has null LastPrice/Bid/Ask (MapSnapshot :286-318, TryGetField returns null for absent keys :349-350). (3) No session-lifecycle hook: the constructor (:58-74) takes no ISessionLifecycleNotifier; the notifier exists (src/IbkrConduit/Session/ISessionLifecycleNotifier.cs:7-22, fired by SessionManager after re-auth) and IbkrWebSocketClient does consume it to resubscribe (src/IbkrConduit/Http/StreamingAndFlexRegistration.cs:33-46), confirming the asymmetry. (4) The library's own design doc supports the session-scoping premise: docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md §13.1 point 3 states "Pre-flight required once per conid per session" while the cache is time-scoped, and §13.2 describes ssodh/init re-initialization as routine. HOWEVER two things temper the finding. First, the trigger is not pinned: the captured spec (docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:3598) only says "A pre-flight request must be made prior to ever receiving data" and says nothing about re-auth resetting preflight state; recordings/ contains only order/priming captures, no market-data snapshot capture, and no recording spans a re-auth — the "per session" line in the design doc is a documented claim, not wire evidence. Hence PLAUSIBLE, not CONFIRMED. Second, the sweep's consequence is overstated: "up to PreflightCacheDuration ... without re-requesting" is wrong — every GetSnapshotAsync call unconditionally issues the underlying API request (MarketDataOperations.cs:93) regardless of the cache; the cache only suppresses the in-call 500ms wait+second-request. Each post-re-auth call therefore re-primes the server-side subscription itself, so the empty-success window is bounded by the consumer's polling cadence (typically one poll cycle), not 5 minutes. Additionally, an all-null-fields Success is already a reachable happy-path output even without the cache (an in-call retry that is still empty is returned as-is at :124-129; the unit test at tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/MarketData/MarketDataOperationsTests.cs:109-147 pins only the happy retry, nothing pins non-empty output), so consumers must already treat null fields as absent per the nullability-as-presence contract — the data is absent, not silently wrong. Severity re-judged: robustness/observability gap that self-heals on the next poll, no wrong numerics, no crash/wedge — low. No VCR story touched this path (VCR stream fixed streaming mappers, schema validation, live-orders priming — none modified MarketDataOperations preflight).
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer polls IIbkrClient.MarketData.GetSnapshotAsync for a conid; the first call preflights it and the conid enters the singleton MemoryCache for 5 minutes (MarketDataOperations.cs:111-114). The brokerage session then times out and SessionManager re-authenticates via ssodh/init (a routine event); if IBKR resets server-side snapshot subscription state on re-init — implied by the design doc's "pre-flight required once per conid per session" (docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md §13.1) but not pinned by the captured spec or any recording — the next GetSnapshotAsync within the cache window receives an empty-field response, finds the conid cached (MarketDataOperations.cs:254), skips the 500ms wait-and-retry, and returns a Success Result whose MarketDataSnapshot has null LastPrice/BidPrice/AskPrice with no signal distinguishing preflight artifact from market condition. Because every call still issues the underlying request (:93) and thereby re-primes the server, the window is one-to-a-few poll cycles, not the full 5 minutes; the consumer sees transiently absent (null) fields — the same shape it must already tolerate from a slow in-call preflight retry — rather than wrong values.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Subscribe MarketDataOperations to ISessionLifecycleNotifier and clear the preflight cache on re-auth; only cache a conid after the retry actually returned field data.
- **Suggested regression test:** GetSnapshot_AfterReauth_RetriesPreflightDespiteCache — simulate lifecycle re-auth between two snapshot calls returning empty fields and assert the second call re-issues the preflight retry.

### WIR-5 — low — MarketDataTickMapper (smd)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/MarketDataTickMapper.cs:36` · lens: wire fidelity & serialization
- **Claim:** Two robustness gaps: (1) updatedProp.GetInt64() (line 36) and the fallback conidProp.GetInt32() (line 31) run without ValueKind guards, so an smd frame whose _updated (or conid) arrives as a quoted string — the same numeric-identifier type drift IBKR exhibits on orderId per FlexibleStringJsonConverter's raison d'etre — throws InvalidOperationException and drops the whole tick frame (counted cause=mapper); (2) MarketDataTick.AdditionalData is documented as 'Additional data not mapped to known properties' (StreamingModels.cs:24-26) but the mapper constructs the record manually and never populates it, silently discarding every non-numeric key (e.g. server_id, conidEx) — the property is always null.
- **Consequence:** Market-data consumers lose ticks on a benign quoting drift and cannot rely on the advertised extension-data escape hatch; drops are counted so the loss is observable, and market data is outside the primary money path — hence low.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/Mappers/MarketDataTickMapper.cs:29-37 (unguarded GetInt32/GetInt64), :39-48 (only int-parseable keys captured), :54-59 (record built without AdditionalData); src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/StreamingModels.cs:24-26 (documented extension property); src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/ChannelObservable.cs:56-67 (frame-level drop on mapper throw)
- **Skeptic verdict:** **PLAUSIBLE** — Reachability: IIbkrClient streaming surface -> IStreamingOperations.MarketDataAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Client/IStreamingOperations.cs:81) -> StreamingOperations.MarketDataAsync builds the smd subscription with MarketDataTickMapper.Map (src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs:94-102). Public and reachable. Gap (1) mechanism verified: MarketDataTickMapper.cs:29-32 calls conidProp.GetInt32() and :34-37 calls updatedProp.GetInt64() with no ValueKind guard; a string-valued "_updated" or "conid" would throw InvalidOperationException. ChannelObservable.PumpAsync catches the mapper throw per frame (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/ChannelObservable.cs:56-67), records the drop (cause=mapper, :64), logs it (:65), and continues — the whole tick frame is lost but the stream survives and the loss is counted, i.e. not silent. Nuance: the conid fallback at :31 only executes when the topic string parse fails (:29 requires conid==0), so it is doubly rare; the _updated path runs on every frame carrying _updated. Trigger is NOT pinned: the captured spec documents _updated/conid as int only for the REST snapshot endpoint (docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:3620-3621, example :3631-3633); the WebSocket smd frame shape is not captured at all (the only smd mention is docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:3653), and recordings/ contains no smd captures (only bracket/bulk/cleanup/oca/orderref/orders/priming). The quoted-numeric drift is an analogy from sor orderId (StreamingModels.cs:39-44 documents IBKR sending orderId as number or quoted string), not observed on smd — so the failure trigger depends on unpinned live wire behavior, forcing PLAUSIBLE per the verdict rules. Gap (2) verified code-side: MarketDataTick.AdditionalData is documented as "Additional data not mapped to known properties" with [JsonExtensionData] (StreamingModels.cs:24-26), but the mapper constructs the record manually setting only Conid/Updated/Fields (MarketDataTickMapper.cs:54-59) and discards every non-numeric key other than topic/conid/_updated (:40-52, only int-parseable names captured at :48-51). Grep confirms StreamingOperations.cs:102 is the sole production construction path and MarketDataTick is never STJ-deserialized anywhere in src, so AdditionalData is deterministically always null on the streaming surface. Whether extension keys (server_id, conidEx) actually appear on smd WS frames is likewise unpinned, but the always-null property is a dead advertised escape hatch regardless. VCR residual check: VCR-03 (#247) was deliberately scoped to TradeExecutionMapper (str) per-element isolation and SessionStatusMapper (sts) tolerant boolean only (docs/backlog.md:85-91); MarketDataTickMapper was not touched, so this survives the merged fix stream as an unaddressed sibling of the same pattern. Test pin: the only mapper test uses numeric conid/_updated (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Unit/Streaming/StreamingOperationsTests.cs:307) — nothing pins string-drift tolerance either way. Severity re-judged low: market data is outside the order/fill money path, the drop is frame-scoped (stream survives), and it is counted and logged so the loss is observable — a robustness gap, not a silent-loss or wedge.
- **Failure scenario (refined):** A consumer subscribes via IIbkrClient streaming MarketDataAsync(conid, fields); if IBKR ever emits an smd frame with "_updated" (or, when the topic string is absent/malformed, "conid") as a quoted string — the same numeric-identifier quoting drift IBKR demonstrably exhibits on sor orderId but which is not pinned for smd by the captured spec or any recording — MarketDataTickMapper.Map throws InvalidOperationException at the unguarded GetInt64()/GetInt32() (MarketDataTickMapper.cs:36/:31) and ChannelObservable drops the entire tick frame (counted cause=mapper, logged; ChannelObservable.cs:60-67), so the consumer loses that tick's field values while the stream continues. Independently and deterministically, MarketDataTick.AdditionalData — documented as the escape hatch for unmapped keys (StreamingModels.cs:24-26) — is always null because the mapper builds the record without it and discards all non-numeric keys (MarketDataTickMapper.cs:40-59), so any extension data (e.g. server_id, conidEx) on smd frames is silently unavailable through the advertised property.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Guard _updated/conid reads with ValueKind checks (string-tolerant parse), and either populate AdditionalData with the non-numeric non-reserved keys or correct the doc to state it is unused for smd.
- **Suggested regression test:** MarketDataTickMapper_StringUpdated_StillMapsTick — smd frame with "_updated":"1751900000000" and "server_id":"q0" asserts the tick is delivered (not dropped) and non-numeric keys are surfaced (or documented as discarded).

## Unverified findings (verification-cap overflow)

The verification stage was capped at 40 findings; these were swept up but no skeptic checked them. Claims and line numbers are as reported by the sweep agents.

### RST-6 — low* — FlexOperations.PollForStatementAsync (FlexPollTimeout accounting)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Client/FlexOperations.cs:333` · lens: REST breadth & periphery
- **Claim:** The poll loop bounds only its own inter-poll delays (totalWaited += actualDelay at :333); the duration of each GetStatement HTTP round-trip — including waits inside the Flex pipeline's sustained rate limiter (10/min token bucket, queue 50, StreamingAndFlexRegistration.cs:74-82) and large-statement downloads — is never counted against FlexPollTimeout. The delay schedule (1,2,3,5,5...s, :23-24) attempts ~12+ polls/min, exceeding the 10/min sustained limiter, so limiter queuing systematically inflates wall-clock time; a configured 60s FlexPollTimeout (IbkrClientOptions.cs:116) can run multiples of that in wall time before the loop's own counter expires.
- **Consequence:** A consumer relying on FlexPollTimeout as an upper bound (e.g. a scheduled reconciliation job with its own deadline) blocks far longer than configured, with cancellation via CancellationToken the only real bound — a liveness/observability gap, not data corruption.
- **Evidence:** src/IbkrConduit/Client/FlexOperations.cs:269-334 (maxWaitMs vs totalWaited; only actualDelay accumulates at :333; GetStatementAsync await at :281 uncounted); poll cadence :23-24 vs sustained limiter src/IbkrConduit/Http/StreamingAndFlexRegistration.cs:74-82; timeout default src/IbkrConduit/Session/IbkrClientOptions.cs:116.
- **Skeptic verdict:** **UNVERIFIED** — found by a sweep agent but not adversarially verified (verification cap); treat the claim as unconfirmed until independently checked.
- **Suggested fix direction (conduit-side):** Measure elapsed wall time from a TimeProvider timestamp at loop start (elapsed = now - start) instead of summing delays, and/or align the poll cadence with the sustained Flex limiter (min 6s spacing).
- **Suggested regression test:** PollForStatement_SlowHttpCalls_HonorsWallClockTimeout — with a FakeTimeProvider advance time inside the stubbed GetStatement call and assert the loop times out at FlexPollTimeout wall-clock, not delay-sum.

## Refuted claims (checked, cleared)

These were raised by sweep agents and struck down by skeptics with concrete counter-evidence.

### STR-1 — refuted — IbkrWebSocketClient (send path)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:785`
- **Original claim:** SendTextAsync performs ws.SendAsync with no serialization, but it is invoked concurrently from four independent contexts: the heartbeat task (line 542, every 30s by default), consumer-thread SubscribeTopicAsync (line 261), consumer-thread UnsubscribeSolicitedAsync (line 831), and the replay loop inside ConnectCoreAsync (line 733). ClientWebSocket (wrapped 1:1 by ClientWebSocketAdapter.cs:23-25 with no lock) permits exactly one outstanding SendAsync; a second concurrent send throws InvalidOperationException.
- **Why refuted:** I re-traced the full send path. The sweep's code citations are accurate: SendTextAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:771-793) has no serialization and is invoked concurrently from the heartbeat task (:542, inside StartHeartbeat :526-559), consumer-thread SubscribeTopicAsync (:259-262, only when the socket is Open), UnsubscribeSolicitedAsync (:827-837), and the reconnect replay loop (:731-734); ClientWebSocketAdapter.SendAsync (src/IbkrConduit/Streaming/ClientWebSocketAdapter.cs:23-25) is a bare passthrough to ClientWebSocket.SendAsync with no lock; reachability from IIbkrClient.Streaming is real. However, the claimed consequence rests on a false premise about the runtime: src/IbkrConduit/IbkrConduit.csproj:4 pins TargetFrameworks to net8.0;net10.0, and on all .NET (Core) runtimes since 2.1 ClientWebSocket is backed by ManagedWebSocket (WebSocketHandle -> WebSocket.CreateFromStream), which internally serializes concurrent SendAsync calls on a send mutex (_sendFrameAsyncLock SemaphoreSlim historically; AsyncMutex _sendMutex since .NET 7) — a second concurrent send awaits the mutex and proceeds; it does NOT throw InvalidOperationException. That throw-on-concurrent-send behavior belongs to the legacy WinHttpWebSocket used only on .NET Framework/UWP, which cannot load a net8.0/net10.0-only library. ManagedWebSocket's ThrowIfOperationInProgress guard applies to concurrent receives/close, and this client has exactly one receive loop (StartMessagePump, IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:561-626), so it is never tripped. Frame corruption is also impossible: every SendTextAsync sends a single complete frame (endOfMessage: true, :788) and ManagedWebSocket writes the whole frame under the mutex, so a heartbeat 'tic' and a subscribe message serialize in arbitrary (semantically irrelevant) order rather than interleaving. Consequently the heartbeat catch at :546-551 (heartbeat_failure -> ReconnectAsync) fires only on genuine socket failure — exactly when a reconnect is correct — SubscribeTopicAsync cannot crash from mere send concurrency, and the unsubscribe send is additionally wrapped in its own log-and-swallow catch (:829-836). The replay-vs-heartbeat overlap is additionally near-impossible timing-wise (StartHeartbeat at :484 waits a full heartbeat interval at :539 before its first send, while replay at :486 runs immediately), and even if it happened it would just queue. The only residue is that the library relies on ManagedWebSocket's internal send serialization, which is implementation behavior rather than the documented WebSocket.SendAsync contract — a robustness nit with no concrete trigger on either pinned TFM, not a reportable defect under the ground rules.

### PRB-4.1 — refuted — IIbkrSessionApi.GetAuthStatusAsync (active health probe wire call)

- **Where:** `src/IbkrConduit/Session/IIbkrSessionApi.cs:46`
- **Original claim:** The active-probe endpoint is declared [Get("/v1/api/iserver/auth/status")] but the repo's own captured spec pins /iserver/auth/status as Method: POST (docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:6550-6551). No recording, E2E test, or live probe verifies that IBKR accepts GET on this endpoint — the only pins are WireMock stubs the repo wrote to match its own GET assumption (tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Integration/Session/SessionTests.cs:54-55 stubs .UsingGet()), and the design doc's [Get] at docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md:335 contradicts the captured spec, which per .claude/rules/contract-design.md is the ground truth that is 'never decided, only verified'.
- **Why refuted:** The code-side mechanism is real: IIbkrSessionApi.GetAuthStatusAsync is declared [Get("/v1/api/iserver/auth/status")] (src/IbkrConduit/Session/IIbkrSessionApi.cs:46-47), it is the active-probe wire call (src/IbkrConduit/Health/HealthStatusCollector.cs:107 in CollectActiveSessionHealthAsync), and the captured spec transcribes IBKR's docs as Method: POST (docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:6550-6551). But the finding's load-bearing claim — "No recording, E2E test, or live probe verifies that IBKR accepts GET on this endpoint" — is factually wrong. The repo's ApiCapture tool, which runs against the live paper account, declares this exact endpoint as HttpMethod.Get with ExpectedStatus 200 (tools/ApiCapture/EndpointTable.cs:23-24: new("Session", "GetAuthStatus_Success", HttpMethod.Get, "/v1/api/iserver/auth/status", 200)); the runner sends exactly the declared method and counts any status mismatch as a failure (tools/ApiCapture/CaptureRunner.cs:57-101, EndpointEntry.cs:11 "Recording is deleted if actual status differs"). That Session capture was run live and its output committed: git commit 0ae82b2 "test: add session and accounts fixture files from recordings" added tests/IbkrConduit.Tests.Integration/Fixtures/Session/GET-auth-status.json, whose body carries unmistakable live-wire artifacts — serverVersion "Build 10.44.1d, Mar 3, 2026" and, decisively, an "established": true field that does NOT appear in the spec doc's documented response table (docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:6555-6564), so the body cannot have been hand-authored from the docs. The library's AuthStatusResponse maps that same Established field and HealthStatusCollector consumes it (HealthStatusCollector.cs:117-122), i.e., the implementation's shape is pinned to the live GET capture, not the doc. The design doc also records empirically-observed GET semantics on session timeout (docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md:957: "GET /iserver/auth/status returns connected: true, authenticated: false"). Per .claude/rules/contract-design.md, the spec doc is a transcription of IBKR's documentation ("documented ≠ verified — IBKR's own docs are a claim, not evidence") while recordings are the verified ground truth — and the recording verified GET returns 200 with the auth-status body. The claimed failure trigger (live IBKR rejects GET with 405/404, making ActiveProbe permanently Unhealthy) is therefore contradicted by the repo's own live capture. What survives is only a documentation-consistency nit: the spec doc says POST while the verified implementation uses GET (evidently IBKR accepts both, as the endpoint's appearance inside POST /tickle responses at ibkr-web-api-spec.md:6692 also suggests) — a docs nit, explicitly excluded from reporting.

## Coverage gaps named by the completeness critic

After the main verification wave, a completeness critic reviewed what the nine lenses had and had not read and named four gaps; each was probed by a dedicated agent and the probe findings (PRB-*) were adversarially verified like every other finding.

1. **Client/StreamingOperations.cs — the streaming facade no lens owned; prefix-only frame routing means cross-conid/cross-account fan-in on smd/ssd/sld** — STR's scope enumerated Streaming/*.cs core classes and WIR owned models/mappers, but the facade that builds subscribe messages and wires subscriptions (src/IbkrConduit/Client/StreamingOperations.cs + IStreamingOperations.cs) appears in NO lens scope and no finding/clean note. I verified a concrete hazard: MarketDataAsync keys every conid subscription under topic prefix "smd" (StreamingOperations.cs:100-102), and IbkrWebSocketClient.ProcessMessage routes by prefix only — "smd+265598" -> "smd" (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:659-667, writer registry keyed at :246 by topicPrefix) — so two concurrent MarketDataAsync subscriptions for DIFFERENT conids each receive BOTH conids' ticks; same for per-account ssd/sld (StreamingOperations.cs:171-176, 186-191) under multi-account tenants. From the consumer seat that is silently wrong market data feeding a trading system unless the consumer knows to filter by MarketDataTick.ConId — nothing in the public surface says so.
2. **Question-suppression flow: SuppressQuestionsAsync failure inside EnsureInitializedAsync/ReauthenticateAsync — an SES x ORD interaction nobody mentioned** — SuppressMessageIds/SuppressQuestionsAsync appears in zero findings and zero clean notes despite sitting directly on the order-flow-blocking path (suppressed prompts are what keep automated order submission from stalling on question/reply). I verified the call sites: SessionManager.cs:198-212 (init) and :377-391 (reauth) POST the suppression AFTER a successful authenticated ssodh/init but BEFORE the health-state update, tickle start, and Ready transition — and a failure rethrows the ORIGINAL transport exception (RethrowOriginal, :209/:388), not a wrapped IbkrError, out of the whole init/reauth. So a transient blip on the suppress call fails an otherwise-successful authentication: the server session IS live, but the library reports init/reauth failed. Whether state resets correctly (SES-3), whether the tickle timer is left stopped/leaked, and whether the next attempt converges was never examined; nor was the ignored SuppressResponse body (silent partial suppression -> orders later blocked by an unexpected question).
3. **ssd/sld account-money streaming mappers (AccountSummaryUpdateMapper, AccountLedgerUpdateMapper) — money-carrying mappers absent from every WIR clean note** — WIR's clean list names SessionStatus/AccountStatus/SystemEvent/TradeExecution/OrderUpdate/MarketDataTick mappers, and WIR-1 covers sor/spl fan-out — but the two mappers carrying account balance money (AccountSummaryUpdateMapper.cs, AccountLedgerUpdateMapper.cs) are mentioned nowhere, positive or negative. I verified the pattern the findings hint at recurs here: AccountLedgerUpdateMapper.Map deserializes each result row with NO per-element isolation (AccountLedgerUpdateMapper.cs:18-25 — element.Deserialize<AccountLedgerRow> can throw JsonException, killing the WHOLE ledger frame as one mapper drop), the exact defect class VCR-03/FIL-2 fixed for str and WIR-1 re-found for sor/spl. Also the WIR-5 money-field census was wired only for sor/str (StreamingOperations.cs:115-121, 143-153) — ssd/sld money absence is uncensused, and it is unclear whether any recording pins the ssd/sld wire shape at all.
4. **IbkrClient facade layer: active-probe health path bypasses the VCR-07 competing-evidence flow, and facade DisposeAsync vs container ownership in the plain AddIbkrClient path** — src/IbkrConduit/Client/IbkrClient.cs is in no lens scope (TEN listed Manager/ManagedTenant/TenantBuilder/IbkrConduit.cs; ERR-2 touched only the flex-validation branch), and RST's HealthStatusCollector clean note covered only 'read-only snapshots'. I verified the active-probe branch is a live-session-touching path nobody read: CollectActiveSessionHealthAsync calls _sessionApi.GetAuthStatusAsync and returns the verdict WITHOUT feeding _sessionHealthState (HealthStatusCollector.cs:102-123 — a probe that sees competing=true never MarkCompeting's, unlike tickle/sts/ssodh evidence per the VCR-07 clean claim), and a probe transport failure propagates raw. Separately, IbkrClient.DisposeAsync disposes ONLY the SessionManager (IbkrClient.cs:132-136) — in the plain AddIbkrClient (non-manager) path the SessionManager is also container-owned, so consumer `await using client` + provider disposal double-disposes it, and neither disposes the WebSocket client except via the provider; the manager path got VCR-08 scrutiny, this path did not.

## Reviewed and found clean

Areas a sweep or probe agent deep-read and cleared. Anything not listed in this document — as a finding, a refutation, or a clean line below — was **not reviewed**.

### OAuth signing & credential hygiene

- OAuthCrypto DH math: 256-bit CSPRNG private key with correct little-endian positive-sign padding, ModPow, and Java-compatible two's-complement big-endian K bytes (sign byte prepended when MSB set) matching the ibind/IBKR HMAC-key convention; pinned by round-trip unit tests and live LST acquisition
- LST derivation and validation flow (HMAC-SHA1 key=K data=decrypted secret; validation HMAC over consumerKey compared ordinal) and PKCS1-then-OAEP access-token-secret decryption fallback
- RsaSha256Signer (PKCS1 SHA-256 over UTF-8 base string) and HmacSha256Signer — correct algorithms, correct base64 output, LST bytes never stringified
- OAuthEncoding: QuotePlus matches Python quote_plus for the RFC-3986 unreserved set on modern .NET; nonce is 16-char CSPRNG alphanumeric (modulo bias present but no practical impact); timestamp is Unix-seconds invariant
- PrependingBaseStringBuilder (lowercase hex prepend, LST-request-only) and OAuthHeaderBuilder composition — fixed oauth_* key set sorts identically under culture vs ordinal comparers, signature is the only header value needing encoding and gets QuotePlus
- TokenRefreshHandler retry interaction with signing: retry uses a fresh clone that strips Authorization and re-enters OAuthSigningHandler for a fresh nonce/timestamp/signature; no duplicate-Authorization or nonce-reuse path; order-mutating POST replay gate (ADR-0003) intact
- SessionTokenProvider expiry-as-miss caching and semaphore serialization (SES-3 fix present); no torn/partial publication hazard on .NET's memory model
- SessionManager re-auth epoch dedupe, compete backoff, proactive-refresh retry loop, and WrapCredentialException coverage of transport/crypto/JSON failure classes (except the LST-validation message routing reported above)
- Credential logging hygiene inside the library: AuditLogHandler never logs Authorization headers and scrubs Flex t= tokens; no internal log/Activity emits AccessToken or key material; LiveSessionToken record prints byte[] type name only
- DI registration: per-tenant handler instances, OAuthSigningHandler innermost, session-API pipeline has no session-manager recursion, credentials disposal ownership consistent in manager path

### session lifecycle

- SessionManager SES-1/SES-3 hardening: authenticated=false init/reauth treated as failure with truthful health + competing evidence (BuildUnauthenticatedInitError), failed init/reauth resets state to Uninitialized, SES-6 timer stop-before-recreate is deadlock-free (StopAsync cancels the tickle token before awaiting)
- ADR-0004 compete backoff: armed only on authenticated=false+competing under Compete=false, exponential growth capped at 5min, reset on clean reauth, mutated only under the semaphore; backoff-skip returns are covered by the still-running tickle failure loop
- Reauth epoch dedupe (issue #168) and SessionTokenProvider version-deduped RefreshAsync + expired-cache-as-miss (SES-3 fix) — no stale-LST replay path found
- Proactive refresh (SES-5): retry loop with exponential backoff, already-due tokens refresh immediately, slot supersession via linked CTS is race-free under _proactiveRefreshLock; LST expiry is server-provided epoch-ms so the Task.Delay bound is safe
- TokenRefreshHandler VCR-04 replay gate coverage: regex correctly gates place/modify/reply POSTs and excludes /orders/whatif, GET, DELETE cancel, and /iserver/notification dismiss — verified against the full IIbkrOrderApi surface; ambiguous marker set on the original request whose options ResultFactory reads
- Hidden-error capture across the 401 retry (CloneRequest drops Options) — re-examined and stands refuted per the prior review's empirical refutation: Refit reassigns RequestMessage to the original request, so GetCapturedBody/GetAmbiguousOrderOutcome read the right options
- VCR-07 competing truth end-to-end: tickle/ssodh/sts evidence flows into SessionHealthState (sticky MarkCompeting, positive clearing only), TokenRefreshHandler propagates it into IbkrSessionError on reauth-failure and post-retry-401 paths
- SessionLifecycleNotifier: snapshot-under-lock iteration, per-subscriber exception isolation for both refresh and tickle-succeeded channels, correct unsubscribe semantics
- SessionHealthState: all mutations and snapshot atomic under one lock; SetFailed preserves competing evidence
- Session DTO models (IIbkrSessionApiModels): tolerant JsonExtensionData on every response record, wire names match the captured spec
- ManagedTenant/TenantBuilder single-logout ordering (VCR-08): bounded logout before provider teardown, SkipLogoutOnDispose suppresses the duplicate, credential ownership disposed on every build-failure path
- TickleTimer transport-failure handling: 3-consecutive-failure health degradation (SES-2), reset on any HTTP response, wrapped-cancellation unwrap for clean shutdown, notifier-throw isolation on the success path

### streaming delivery & backpressure

- Drop observability taxonomy post-VCR-02: bounded-channel overflow evictions counted via itemDropped + per-connection log throttle (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:237-243, 333-346, 479-482), mapper drops (cause=mapper) and observer faults (cause=observer, surfaced via OnError never OnCompleted) in ChannelObservable/FanOutChannelObservable — all three causes counted and logged
- StreamingMetrics per-tenant tagging and VCR-09 metrics hygiene: instance-scoped connection_state Meter disposed with the client, disposal-gated gauge callback, static counters carry only tenant tags (no instance pinning)
- ConnectionEvent contract post-FIL-4: Disconnected published before teardown, Reconnected after replay with replayed-topic list, unbounded channel so lifecycle events are never overflow-dropped, writers completed on dispose
- ProcessMessage topic routing: prefix extraction, tic-echo filter with LastMessageReceivedAt still updated first, unsolicited sts/system/act/blt/ntf dispatch, JsonException isolation per frame
- Unsubscribe cancel-message refcounting happy path (shared cancel sent only by the final referencing subscription, under _subscriptionLock) and consistent _subscriptionLock→writers lock ordering across subscribe/unsubscribe/route paths
- IbkrSubscription idempotent at-most-once unsubscribe (Interlocked) and CancellationDisposable exactly-once cancel+dispose
- Pre-connect subscribe queuing and replay visibility ordering (entry added under lock before the _webSocket-open check; replay snapshot taken after _webSocket assignment, so no queued subscription is skipped — worst case a harmless duplicate subscribe message)
- Single-observer enforcement per ADR-0002 (Interlocked slot CAS, second Subscribe throws, slot freed exactly once) — modulo the pump-drain race reported above
- Message pump framing: multi-fragment reassembly into MemoryStream before parse, close-frame handling, per-message liveness timestamp via Interlocked

### wire fidelity & serialization

- EmptyTolerantNumberConverters — all 8 converters + parsing helpers (invariant culture, exponent-tolerant NumberStyles.Float, overflow/garbage rethrown as JsonException with context)
- FlexibleStringJsonConverter and FlexibleBoolJsonConverter token-kind handling (incl. ValueSequence path and non-integer-number rejection)
- IbkrRefitSettings / StreamingSerialization options wiring — converter registration, Refit-default preservation, converter precedence over [JsonNumberHandling]
- SessionStatusMapper / AccountStatusMapper / SystemEventMapper presence semantics (tolerant authenticated/competing/isPaper/isFT — VCR-03/VCR-07/GAP2-3 fixes hold at HEAD)
- OrderUpdate / TradeExecution / Trade / LiveOrder / OrderStatus DTO money-field nullability post-VCR-01 (null-as-absence intact; no fabricated 0/""/false on money or verdict fields)
- MoneyFieldCensus and its sor/str wiring (WIR-5 — absence-only, status-gated for sor, delivered-not-dropped semantics)
- FanOutChannelObservable / ChannelObservable drop taxonomy and observer-error honesty (VCR-02 — counted+logged drops, OnError never masqueraded as completion)
- TradeExecutionMapper per-element isolation (VCR-03 str fix correct: materialize-before-yield, OCE excluded, dropped element never censused)
- RefitEndpointMap path-template matching (templates carry /v1/api so anchored regexes match production AbsolutePath; literal-before-parameterized ordering)
- IbkrUrlParameterFormatter lowercase-bool query serialization (force=true wire form)
- ResponseSchemaValidationHandler collection/dictionary target enumeration, JsonExtensionData extra-field diff, and body re-buffering with content-type restoration (VCR-10 delivered scope)
- OrderWireModel request serialization (null-omission of cOID/parentId/isSingleGroup/outsideRTH; decimal quantities/prices)

### orders & money path

- ClassifyOrderResponses/ClassifyResponse trichotomy for the pinned 2xx array shapes (empty array, array-wrapped [{"error"}], numeric order_id via FlexibleStringJsonConverter, question shape) — VCR-04/AMB-4 fixes hold at HEAD
- ADR-0003 401 replay gate: regex + POST-method scoping correctly matches place/modify/reply and excludes whatif, order-status, GET/DELETE; ambiguous marker plumbing through ResultFactory including the reauth-failed path (ambiguity wins over the competing-session throw)
- LiveOrdersSnapshot priming surface (VCR-05): snapshot??false mapping, null-orders→empty list, unfiltered/already-forced no-follow-up paths, exactly-once follow-up pinned by DI-stack WireMock test
- LiveOrder/Trade/OrderStatus DTO wire mapping vs recordings/priming/003 capture (empty-tolerant numeric converters, no fabricated zeros, order_ref/order_id normalization)
- ValidateOrderGroup bracket/OCA fail-fast validation and PlaceOrdersAsync single-parent-element handling
- Per-account submission semaphore mechanics (GetOrAdd, release in finally, cross-account parallelism) for the HTTP call itself
- Hidden-error detection on bare-object 2xx bodies for cancel/whatif/status/trades paths, and cancel response shape vs spec example
- No automatic retry/resilience policy on order POSTs (rate limiting only) — no duplicate-order retry vector in the pipeline
- IbkrUrlParameterFormatter lowercase-bool force=true wire format and OrderStatusFilter EnumMember CSV serialization (pinned by integration test asserting the lowercase follow-up)

### concurrency, thread safety & disposal

- SessionManager EnsureInitializedAsync/ReauthenticateAsync core (epoch dedupe, compete backoff, SES-3 state resets — all mutations under the semaphore)
- TickleTimer start/stop lifecycle and RunAsync loop (fields confined to the single loop task; StopAsync awaits the background task)
- TokenRefreshHandler (content buffering, ADR-0003 order-replay gate, response disposal)
- SessionTokenProvider (semaphore-guarded cache + version-deduped refresh)
- SessionLifecycleNotifier (lock-guarded subscribe/notify snapshots, per-subscriber exception isolation)
- SessionHealthState / SessionHealthSnapshot (single-lock field access)
- IbkrClientManager add/remove/dispose races (sentinel + post-install re-check + drain loop — MGR-1..6 fixes hold at HEAD)
- ManagedTenant / TenantBuilder disposal ordering and credential ownership (single bounded logout, VCR-08 holds)
- GlobalRateLimitingHandler / EndpointRateLimitingHandler / EndpointRateLimiters / TenantDiagnostics (per-tenant limiter disposal and gauge scoping, VCR-09 holds)
- ChannelObservable / FanOutChannelObservable / SingleObserverChannelObservable / IbkrSubscription / CancellationDisposable (single-observer slot, once-only unsubscribe, drop taxonomy)
- HealthStatusCollector and LastSuccessfulCallTracker (read-only snapshots, Interlocked ticks)
- OrderOperations per-account SemaphoreSlim serialization (GetOrAdd + try/finally release, token passed to WaitAsync)
- OAuthSigningHandler (per-request local signer state, no shared mutable state)

### DI pipeline & multi-tenancy

- IbkrClientManager add/remove/dispose race protocol (sentinel TryAdd/TryUpdate, disposed re-check, drain loop) — MGR-3 fix holds at HEAD
- Credential ownership on every AddAsync/TenantBuilder failure path (entry guards, sentinel conflict, validate/override throws, builder ownership handoff) — MGR-2 holds
- Rate-limiter DI ownership and disposal (factory-registered global RateLimiter, EndpointRateLimiters and FlexRateLimiters holders) — VCR-09/MGR-5 fix holds, no timer leaks on tenant churn
- TenantDiagnostics per-tenant Meter/queue-depth gauge registration exactly once via the IIbkrClient factory, disposed with the provider — VCR-09/MGR-4 holds in both build paths
- Global 10 req/s per-tenant token bucket matches the captured spec pacing line (docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:3653); per-endpoint limiter table and burst/queue settings consistent
- Handler pipeline ordering for auth: 401 retry re-traverses schema-validation, both rate limiters and OAuth signing; re-auth traffic shares the same global limiter via the session pipeline
- ADR-0003 order-mutating-POST replay gate and AmbiguousOrderOutcome propagation (marker set on the original request whose 401 response is returned — options readback works on that path)
- TenantContext seeding precedence (manager-seeded instance wins over TryAddSingleton fallback) and tenant tags on all handler metrics/spans/log scopes
- AuditLogHandler flex-token redaction and body re-buffering; ResponseBodyCaptureHandler re-buffering itself (defect is only the retry-path options readback)
- IbkrClientOptions.Clone() covers every current field including internal SkipLogoutOnDispose/LogoutTimeout; per-tenant override cannot mutate the shared baseline
- TenantBuilder child-provider seeding (external loggerFactory/sharedGovernor instances not container-disposed; no cross-tenant singleton leakage)
- ManagedTenant bounded single logout ordering (logout before provider teardown, linked CTS cap) on the success-path lifecycle

### error taxonomy & result mapping

- ResultFactory non-2xx mapping (empty/HTML/plain-text/malformed-JSON bodies, 429 Retry-After seconds parsing) — pinned by unit + integration tests
- RefitResponseExtensions ThrowOnSendFailure / RethrowIfWrappedCancellation / RethrowOriginal — cancellation and send-failure unwrapping correct, pinned by ResultFactoryTests
- ADR-0003 ambiguous-order gate end-to-end: order-mutating-POST regex anchoring (whatif/status excluded), reauth-failure still yields ambiguous (never session-throw), marker read-back off the original response, IbkrAmbiguousOrderError construction with ReauthSucceeded
- IbkrSessionError.IsCompeting reachability with true (SessionManager.BuildUnauthenticatedInitError, TokenRefreshHandler GAP3-1 pre/post-retry paths fed from SessionHealthState)
- FlexErrorCodes table and ClassifyPollResponse transient/permanent/timeout loop (incl. 1018 backoff, jitter floor, poll-timeout retryable classification)
- FlexClient transport mapping (HttpRequestException→IbkrApiError, XmlException→IbkrApiError with raw body) and flex-token log sanitization in AuditLogHandler
- OrderOperations AMB-3/AMB-4 classification: empty-array refusal, array-wrapped reject, WIR-4 hardened reply deserialization via IbkrRefitSettings.Options, residual-shape throw carrying raw body
- SessionManager.WrapCredentialException taxonomy (crypto/401-403/transport/timeout/DH/JSON) with caller-cancellation rethrow guards ahead of it
- ResponseSchemaValidationHandler strict/lax flow, body re-buffering, and IbkrSchemaViolationException surfacing through ThrowOnSendFailure
- Duplicate internal IbkrErrorBody records (Errors/ vs Http/) — identical shape, same-namespace resolution wins, no behavioral divergence

### REST breadth & periphery

- Thin operations pass-throughs (Account/Alert/Watchlist/Fyi/EventContract/Contract/Portfolio Operations) — consistent ResultFactory + ThrowOnApiError handling, activity tags, CancellationToken propagation
- IbkrUrlParameterFormatter — lowercase bools only, defers enums/rest to Refit's default (EnumMember-aware) formatter; HistoryPeriod/BarSize/ExpiryMonth/HistoryDirection wire formats invariant and correct
- FlexClient transport + AuditLogHandler token sanitization — Flex t= token scrubbed for the FlexWebService path prefix used in production; XDocument.Parse default DTD-prohibit posture (no XXE); raw-body logging Trace/Debug-gated
- FlexErrorCodes classification table and SendRequest retry/backoff + poll-loop termination logic (no infinite loop, permanent/transient split matches IBKR docs)
- Rate limiter wiring — global 10/s bucket matches HealthStatusCollector's hardcoded 10-permit assumption; endpoint substring patterns match the /v1/api-prefixed request paths; container-owned disposal (VCR-09 fix) verified in registration
- LastSuccessfulCallHandler placement in both consumer and session pipelines — tickle liveness feeds staleness per ADR-0004 as documented
- IbkrConduit.HealthChecks package — IbkrHealthCheck state mapping, exception-to-Unhealthy, data dictionary, and AddIbkrHealthCheck DI extension
- MarketDataOperations field-id mapping (31/84/86/85/88/7059/70/71/7295/7296 etc.) and BuildAllFieldsDictionary/_nonDataKeys handling; regsnapshot fee warning
- Watchlist and Alert DTO shapes vs captured spec (wrapper responses, MID, instruments); Fyi API routes and query encoding
- ResultFactory hidden-error detection, ambiguous-order marker plumbing, and Result<T> Map/EnsureSuccess semantics
- Portfolio money DTOs (LedgerEntry/AccountSummaryEntry/Position) — decimal-typed, extension-data bags, conid AllowReadingFromString
- SessionHealthState locking and snapshot atomicity; LastSuccessfulCallTracker Interlocked usage

### gap probes

- Unsubscribe refcount and writer removal (IbkrWebSocketClient.UnsubscribeSolicitedAsync:795-838): the disposed subscription's own writer is removed by reference from the shared prefix list, entry removed before the stillReferenced check, and same-cancel duplicates (e.g. two same-conid smd subs) correctly send the wire cancel only on last dispose — matching the recorded contract in docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md §12.5.1
- Bare 'utr' cancel for TradeExecutionsAsync (StreamingOperations.cs:141): pinned by the repo's captured WebSocket reference (docs/ibkr-websocket-api-reference.md:20, 334-340 — 'cancel is bare utr, no braces') and design doc §12.5 line 909; not a defect
- Reconnect replay path (ReplayActiveSubscriptionsAsync:723-735): replays only live _subscriptions entries (disposed entries removed first) with Distinct(), so disposed or duplicate subscribe messages are not resent
- sor/str/spl fan-in is correct-by-design: those topics are account-wide singletons with no per-target segment, and the facade's per-element mapper isolation + money-field census wiring (StreamingOperations.cs:106-162, 226-243) matches the VCR-02/03/10 contracts
- OrderUpdatesAsync/TradeExecutionsAsync argument construction (StreamingOperations.cs:106-141): only int/bool values interpolated, no injection surface
- Mappers populate the discriminator (MarketDataTick.Conid, AccountSummaryUpdate/AccountLedgerUpdate.AccountId) so a consumer-side defensive filter is at least possible pending the routing fix
- SES-3 state reset after a suppress throw: both paths reset _state to Uninitialized (SessionManager.cs:252-257, :423-429) — no permanent wedge, next call re-enters init cleanly
- Tickle-timer hygiene around suppress failure: init-path failure occurs before timer creation (:232), and a failed-reauth-then-re-init stops the prior timer (:227-230, SES-6) — no leaked second tickle loop
- Cancellation propagation through the suppress call: a wrapped OperationCanceledException is rethrown with original type/stack by RethrowOriginal (RefitResponseExtensions.cs:58-64); null-inner ApiRequestException is rethrown as itself (:210)
- Suppression re-applied after every init AND reauth, matching the design doc's per-session semantics (SessionManager.cs:198, :377; docs/ibkr_conduit_design.md:206, 449-458) — competing-takeover/steal-back is covered by the reauth re-suppress
- Reauth epoch and compete-backoff semantics on suppress failure: epoch correctly not bumped (reauth genuinely incomplete), backoff untouched, and the proactive-refresh retry loop (RunProactiveRefreshAsync:588-619) keeps retrying with capped exponential backoff — converges
- Session health honesty around the suppress step: health is never marked authenticated before suppress succeeds, and a successful tickle repopulates health within one interval (TickleTimer.cs:160-197), so no durable stale-unauthenticated claim
- Suppress ordering relative to health/tickle/Ready is correct fail-closed design: no Ready transition and no tickle start on a failed suppress, so the library never runs 'up' with prompts unsuppressed
- SuppressRequest wire mapping (messageIds, IIbkrSessionApiModels.cs:124-125) matches the captured spec (docs/ibkr-web-api-spec.md:5079-5086) and is pinned by SessionModelsTests
- StreamingFrameHelpers.ParseAccountIdFromTopic — never fabricates an account id: missing/non-string topic or no '+' yields string.Empty (StreamingFrameHelpers.cs:15-22), and routing guarantees a string topic is present before any mapper runs (IbkrWebSocketClient.cs:641-650); malformed-topic case pinned by AccountSummaryUpdateMapperTests.Map_MissingTopic_AccountIdIsEmpty
- EmptyTolerant streaming converters — nullable decimal path maps empty string/null to null (never silent 0), quoted numbers parse invariant, genuine garbage throws a diagnostic JsonException (EmptyTolerantNumberConverters.cs:38-48, 219-268); exponent-notation ledger values (E-component pinned at docs/ibkr-websocket-api-reference.md:93,102) are handled by Utf8JsonReader.GetDecimal
- AccountLedgerRow DTO shape — all captured money fields nullable-decimal with correct [JsonPropertyName] casing incl. lowercase 'cashbalance', blank-row (key+timestamp only) semantics and AdditionalData escape hatch pinned by AccountLedgerUpdateMapperTests (real captured frame)
- ssd/sld missing/non-array result handling — yields empty row list rather than throwing (both mappers line 16), pinned by Map_MissingResult tests
